Uttama Carita · Chapter 11 of 13 · 55 mantras (11.1–11.55)

देव्याः स्तुतिः

devyāḥ stutiḥ

The Hymn to Nārāyaṇī · Complete

Recited before the chapter (liturgical, from the Gita Press pāṭha)

These lines belong to the recitation, not to the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa text, and are kept apart from the numbered mantras. They are given here in Sanskrit and IAST only.

Dhyāna

ॐ बालरविद्युतिमिन्दुकिरीटां
तुङ्गकुचां नयनत्रययुक्ताम्।
स्मेरमुखीं वरदाङ्कुशपाशा
भीतिकरां प्रभजे भुवनेशीम्॥

ॐ bālaravidyutimindukirīṭāṃ
tuṅgakucāṃ nayanatrayayuktām|
smeramukhīṃ varadāṅkuśapāśā
bhītikarāṃ prabhaje bhuvaneśīm||

11.1Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

ॐ ऋषिरुवाच ॥ १ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ऋषिः | उवाच

3IAST

As recitedṛṣiruvāca ||

Separatedṛṣiḥ | uvāca

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ऋषिःṛṣiḥthe seer (Medhas)m. nom. sg. of ऋषि (i-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ऋषिरुवाच ṛṣiruvāca → ऋषिः + उवाच
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after i, before a vowel, is sounded as r.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ऋषिः उवाच।

ṛṣiḥ uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe seer said:

ReadableThe seer said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningMedhas, still telling the story to King Suratha and the merchant Samādhi, goes on from the death of Śumbha at the end of chapter 10. What follows is the gods’ hymn of thanksgiving (11.2–35) and the Goddess’s boon, with her promise of future descents (11.37–55).

Traditional interpretationIn the Gita Press pāṭha the chapter is preceded by a dhyāna of Bhuvaneśī (बालरविद्युतिमिन्दुकिरीटां …): the Goddess bright as the morning sun, crowned with the moon, three-eyed, holding goad and noose and showing the gestures of boon and fearlessness. The ॐ that opens this mantra in recitation belongs to the same pāṭha, not to the Purāṇa. The chapter stands in the third carita, which the pāṭha assigns to Mahāsarasvatī.

11.2Ślokaupajāti (triṣṭubh)

1Original śloka

देव्या हते तत्र महासुरेन्द्रे
सेन्द्राः सुरा वह्निपुरोगमास्ताम्।
कात्यायनीं तुष्टुवुरिष्टलाभाद्
विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः ॥ २ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देव्या | हते | तत्र | महासुरेन्द्रेसेन्द्राः | सुराः | वह्निपुरोगमाः | ताम्कात्यायनीम् | तुष्टुवुः | इष्टलाभात्विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः

3IAST

As reciteddevyā hate tatra mahāsurendre
sendrāḥ surā vahnipurogamāstām |
kātyāyanīṃ tuṣṭuvuriṣṭalābhād
vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ ||

Separateddevyā | hate | tatra | mahāsurendre
sendrāḥ | surāḥ | vahnipurogamāḥ | tām
kātyāyanīm | tuṣṭuvuḥ | iṣṭalābhāt
vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
देव्याdevyāby the Goddessf. instr. sg. of देवी (ī-stem); agent of the participle हते
हतेhateslainpast passive participle (kta) of √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), m. loc. sg.; with महासुरेन्द्रे forms a locative absolute
तत्रtatrathere, then (on that battlefield)indeclinable (adverb of place, from तद् + tral)
महासुरेन्द्रेmahāsurendrethe great lord of the asuras (Śumbha)m. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of the locative absolute
सेन्द्राःsendrāḥtogether with Indram. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies सुराः
सुराःsurāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of सुर; subject of तुष्टुवुः

तुष्टुवुःtuṣṭuvuḥpraised√स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • महासुरेन्द्रे mahāsurendre → महासुर + इन्द्रे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + i → e. The same in सेन्द्राः (स + इन्द्राः).
  • सुरा वह्निपुरोगमास्ताम् surā vahnipurogamāstām → सुराः + वह्निपुरोगमाः + ताम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    -āḥ before the voiced v becomes -ā (the y from the visarga is dropped before a consonant); -āḥ before t becomes -ās.
  • तुष्टुवुरिष्टलाभाद् tuṣṭuvuriṣṭalābhād → तुष्टुवुः + इष्टलाभात्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after u, before a vowel, is sounded as r.
  • इष्टलाभाद् विकाशि iṣṭalābhād vikāśi → इष्टलाभात् + विकाशि…
    consonant sandhi (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    Final t becomes d before the voiced v, across the line break; the Gita Press writes the d with a virāma.
  • विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ → विकाशि + वक्त्र + अब्ज + विकाशित + आशाः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā (वक्त्राब्ज) and a + ā → ā (विकाशिताशाः).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महासुरेन्द्रे mahāsurendre — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member महासुर is a karmadhāraya, with महत् → महा)
    Members: महासुर + इन्द्र · Vigraha: महान्तः असुराः महासुराः; महासुराणाम् इन्द्रः महासुरेन्द्रः, तस्मिन् mahāntaḥ asurāḥ mahāsurāḥ; mahāsurāṇām indraḥ mahāsurendraḥ, tasmin
    Literally “the lord of the great asuras”; in the verse: Śumbha, whose death closes chapter 10
  • सेन्द्राः sendrāḥ — bahuvrīhi (saha-bahuvrīhi: सह becomes स)
    Members: स (= सह) + इन्द्र · Vigraha: इन्द्रेण सह वर्तमानाः indreṇa saha vartamānāḥ
    Literally “being with Indra”; in the verse: the gods with Indra among them
  • वह्निपुरोगमाः vahnipurogamāḥ — bahuvrīhi (पुरोगम ‘going in front’ is itself an upapada tatpuruṣa: पुरः गच्छति इति)
    Members: वह्नि + पुरोगम · Vigraha: वह्निः पुरोगमः येषां ते vahniḥ purogamaḥ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “having Vahni as the one who goes in front”; in the verse: with Agni at their head
  • इष्टलाभात् iṣṭalābhāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: इष्ट + लाभ · Vigraha: इष्टस्य लाभः, तस्मात् iṣṭasya lābhaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from the gaining of what was desired”; in the verse: because their wish — the end of the asuras and the return of their heaven — was fulfilled
  • विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ — bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā relation and a karmadhāraya (विकाशिवक्त्राब्ज)
    Members: विकाशि + वक्त्राब्ज + विकाशित + आशा · Vigraha: विकाशीनि वक्त्राब्जानि विकाशिवक्त्राब्जानि; विकाशिवक्त्राब्जैः विकाशिताः आशाः यैः ते vikāśīni vaktrābjāni vikāśivaktrābjāni; vikāśivaktrābjaiḥ vikāśitāḥ āśāḥ yaiḥ te
    Literally “by whom the quarters were made bright with radiant face-lotuses”; in the verse: their faces, shining like opened lotuses, lit up every direction
  • वक्त्राब्ज vaktrābja — karmadhāraya (rūpaka: ‘the face that is a lotus’)
    Members: वक्त्र + अब्ज · Vigraha: वक्त्रम् एव अब्जम् vaktram eva abjam
    Literally “face-lotus”; in the verse: a face as fresh and open as a lotus
    Alternative: Also read as an upamita karmadhāraya, वक्त्रम् अब्जम् इव, ‘a face like a lotus’; the image is the same either way.

C. Morphology

  • देव्या devyā — Instrumental singular of देवी (देवी + आ → देव्या, ī → y before the vowel). The ablative/genitive देव्याः would also be written देव्या before the voiced h; the case is fixed by the sense, as the agent of the passive participle हते.
  • तुष्टुवुः tuṣṭuvuḥ — Perfect of √स्तु: reduplicated तु-ष्टु-, the s of the root turning to ṣ after the reduplicating u, with the 3rd plural ending -उः (स्तु → तुष्टुव् before the vowel). Compare प्रतुष्टुवुः in 5.7.
  • हते hate — Past passive participle हत (√हन् loses its nasal before -ta), here locative singular in a locative absolute: देव्या हते महासुरेन्द्रे ‘the great asura-lord having been slain by the Goddess’.
  • विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ — विकाशिन् ‘shining, opening’ (वि + √काश् with the agent suffix -in) and विकाशित ‘made bright’ (its causative participle) frame the image: the faces shine, and by their shining the quarters are lit.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तत्र देव्या महासुरेन्द्रे हते (सति), इष्टलाभात् विकाशिवक्त्राब्जविकाशिताशाः वह्निपुरोगमाः सेन्द्राः सुराः तां कात्यायनीं तुष्टुवुः।

tatra devyā mahāsurendre hate (sati), iṣṭalābhāt vikāśivaktrābjavikāśitāśāḥ vahnipurogamāḥ sendrāḥ surāḥ tāṃ kātyāyanīṃ tuṣṭuvuḥ |

A locative absolute sets the time (देव्या हते … महासुरेन्द्रे); the main clause is सुराः … तुष्टुवुः with its object तां कात्यायनीम्; इष्टलाभात् gives the cause and the long bahuvrīhi describes the gods.

6Translation

LiteralWhen the great lord of the asuras had been slain there by the Goddess, the gods together with Indra, led by Vahni, praised that Kātyāyanī, because they had gained their desire — they whose radiant lotus-faces made the quarters bright.

ReadableWhen the Goddess had slain the mighty lord of the asuras, the gods, with Indra among them and Agni at their head, their faces shining like opened lotuses and lighting up every quarter for joy at their wish fulfilled, sang the praise of Kātyāyanī.

‘For joy’ renders the ablative of cause इष्टलाभात् (‘because of gaining what they wished’), which the bright faces express.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe narrative frame of the hymn. Śumbha has fallen (10.27); the gods, led by Agni and with Indra among them, praise the Goddess under the name Kātyāyanī, the name she already bore on the battlefield (8.29). Their joy is pictured in their faces, which shine like opened lotuses and light up the directions. Verses 11.2–6 are in upajāti (lines of indravajrā and upendravajrā, eleven syllables).

Traditional interpretationThis is the fourth of the great hymns of the Devī Māhātmya, after Brahmā’s hymn (1.73–87), the gods’ hymn in chapter 4 and the hymn of chapter 5. From its refrain नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते (11.8 onward) it is known as the Nārāyaṇī Stuti, and it is often recited by itself. Agni at the head of the gods recalls his Vedic role as their priest and messenger; the text itself gives no reason.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also names the Goddess Kātyāyanī (name 556, कात्यायनी). The name is traditionally connected with the sage Kātyāyana; the Devī Māhātmya does not explain it. The two texts are separate works.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: विकाशि-/विकाशित- are printed with ś (वि + √काश् ‘to shine’). Printings often write विकासि-/विकासित- with s (वि + √कस् ‘to open, blossom’), and the two spellings are frequently confused in manuscripts. The Gita Press reading is kept; either root gives the image of faces opening and shining like lotuses.

11.3Ślokaupajāti (triṣṭubh)

1Original śloka

देवि प्रपन्नार्तिहरे प्रसीद
प्रसीद मातर्जगतोऽखिलस्य।
प्रसीद विश्वेश्वरि पाहि विश्वं
त्वमीश्वरी देवि चराचरस्य ॥ ३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देवि | प्रपन्नार्तिहरे | प्रसीदप्रसीद | मातः | जगतः | अखिलस्यप्रसीद | विश्वेश्वरि | पाहि | विश्वम्त्वम् | ईश्वरी | देवि | चराचरस्य

3IAST

As reciteddevi prapannārtihare prasīda
prasīda mātarjagato'khilasya |
prasīda viśveśvari pāhi viśvaṃ
tvamīśvarī devi carācarasya ||

Separateddevi | prapannārtihare | prasīda
prasīda | mātaḥ | jagataḥ | akhilasya
prasīda | viśveśvari | pāhi | viśvam
tvam | īśvarī | devi | carācarasya

4Word by word

प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P, present stem सीद- ‘to sit’; with प्र ‘to be pleased, gracious’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousas above: loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana

अखिलस्यakhilasyawhole, entiren. gen. sg. of अखिल; qualifies जगतः
प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousas above: loṭ, madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana
विश्वेश्वरिviśveśvariO Sovereign of the universef. voc. sg. of विश्वेश्वरी; compound (see Samāsa)

विश्वम्viśvamthe universen. acc. sg. of विश्व; object of पाहि
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject (असि understood)
ईश्वरीīśvarīthe ruler, the sovereignf. nom. sg. of ईश्वरी (feminine of ईश्वर); predicate
देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
चराचरस्यcarācarasyaof all that moves and does not moven. gen. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on ईश्वरी

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रपन्नार्तिहरे prapannārtihare → प्रपन्न + आर्ति + हरे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • मातर्जगतोऽखिलस्य mātarjagato'khilasya → मातः + जगतः + अखिलस्य
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66); अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    The visarga of the vocative मातः stands for r (मातर्) and reappears before the voiced j. Then -aḥ before a: the visarga becomes u, a + u → o, and the following a is elided (जगतोऽखिलस्य).
  • विश्वेश्वरि viśveśvari → विश्व + ईश्वरि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e.
  • त्वमीश्वरी tvamīśvarī → त्वम् + ईश्वरी
    no change (m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    Final m stays m before a vowel and is written together with it; contrast विश्वं at the end of the line above.
  • चराचरस्य carācarasya → चर + अचरस्य
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • प्रपन्नार्तिहरे prapannārtihare — upapada tatpuruṣa (-हर ‘taking away’, from √हृ) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (प्रपन्नार्ति)
    Members: प्रपन्न + आर्ति + हर · Vigraha: प्रपन्नानाम् आर्तिः प्रपन्नार्तिः; प्रपन्नार्तिं हरति इति प्रपन्नार्तिहरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ prapannānām ārtiḥ prapannārtiḥ; prapannārtiṃ harati iti prapannārtiharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “remover of the affliction of those who have taken refuge”; in the verse: she who ends the suffering of whoever turns to her
  • विश्वेश्वरि viśveśvari — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विश्व + ईश्वरी · Vigraha: विश्वस्य ईश्वरी viśvasya īśvarī
    Literally “sovereign of the universe”; in the verse: ruler of all
  • चराचरस्य carācarasya — samāhāra dvandva
    Members: चर + अचर · Vigraha: चरं च अचरं च, तयोः समाहारः चराचरम्, तस्य caraṃ ca acaraṃ ca, tayoḥ samāhāraḥ carācaram, tasya
    Literally “of the moving and the unmoving”; in the verse: of every creature and thing, animate and inanimate
    Alternative: Could be taken as an itaretara dvandva (चराणि च अचराणि च); the singular shows the collective (samāhāra) sense.

C. Morphology

  • प्रसीद prasīda — Imperative of प्र + √सद्: the root takes the present stem सीद- (bhvādi). With प्र it means ‘be pleased, be gracious’. It is said three times, opening three of the four lines.
  • मातः mātaḥ — Vocative of मातृ: the stem in -ṛ takes guṇa (मातर्) and loses its final r to visarga in pause; the r returns before a voiced sound (मातर्जगतः).
  • पाहि pāhi — Imperative of √पा ‘protect’ (adādi): the bare root with the ending -हि.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) प्रपन्नार्तिहरे देवि, प्रसीद। (हे) अखिलस्य जगतः मातः, प्रसीद। (हे) विश्वेश्वरि, प्रसीद, विश्वं पाहि। (हे) देवि, त्वं चराचरस्य ईश्वरी (असि)।

(he) prapannārtihare devi, prasīda | (he) akhilasya jagataḥ mātaḥ, prasīda | (he) viśveśvari, prasīda, viśvaṃ pāhi | (he) devi, tvaṃ carācarasya īśvarī (asi) |

Four short sentences, three of them prayers in the imperative (प्रसीद, प्रसीद, प्रसीद … पाहि), each with its own vocative; the last is a statement with असि understood.

6Translation

LiteralO Goddess, remover of the distress of those who take refuge, be gracious! Be gracious, O Mother of the whole world! Be gracious, O Sovereign of the universe; protect the universe! You, O Goddess, are the ruler of the moving and the unmoving.

ReadableO Goddess, who take away the sorrow of all who come to you for refuge, be gracious! Be gracious, Mother of the whole world! Be gracious, Queen of the universe, and protect it! For you, O Goddess, are sovereign over all that moves and all that is still.

‘For’ makes explicit the link the last line has with the prayers before it; the Sanskrit simply states it.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn opens with a threefold prayer, प्रसीद, ‘be gracious’, and each time names the Goddess by a relation: to those who seek refuge, to the world as its mother, to the universe as its sovereign. The last line gives the ground of the prayer: she rules all that moves and all that does not.

Traditional interpretationThe first epithet, प्रपन्नार्तिहरा, puts the hymn’s theme at its head: she is first approached as the one who takes away the distress of the प्रपन्न, those who have come to her for shelter. The theme returns in the refrain verse 11.12 (शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे) and in the gods’ closing prayer for protection (11.24–28, 11.34–35).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names ईश्वरी (271) and चराचरजगन्नाथा (244), ‘ruler of the moving and unmoving world’, which say what the last line of this verse says. The likeness is shared Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate works.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the gods have just been rescued, yet their first word is not thanks but ‘be gracious’. Gratitude here takes the form of asking to remain under the same care.

11.4Ślokaupajāti (triṣṭubh)

1Original śloka

आधारभूता जगतस्त्वमेका
महीस्वरूपेण यतः स्थितासि।
अपां स्वरूपस्थितया त्वयैत-
दाप्यायते कृत्स्नमलङ्घ्यवीर्ये ॥ ४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

आधारभूता | जगतः | त्वम् | एकामहीस्वरूपेण | यतः | स्थिता | असिअपाम् | स्वरूपस्थितया | त्वया | एतत्आप्यायते | कृत्स्नम् | अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये

3IAST

As recitedādhārabhūtā jagatastvamekā
mahīsvarūpeṇa yataḥ sthitāsi |
apāṃ svarūpasthitayā tvayaita-
dāpyāyate kṛtsnamalaṅghyavīrye ||

Separatedādhārabhūtā | jagataḥ | tvam | ekā
mahīsvarūpeṇa | yataḥ | sthitā | asi
apām | svarūpasthitayā | tvayā | etat
āpyāyate | kṛtsnam | alaṅghyavīrye

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
आधारभूताādhārabhūtābeing the supportf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of त्वम्
जगतःjagataḥof the worldn. gen. sg. of जगत्; depends on आधार-
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
एकाekāalone, the onef. nom. sg. of एक; qualifies त्वम्
महीस्वरूपेणmahīsvarūpeṇain the form of the earthn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
यतःyataḥbecause, sinceindeclinable (relative adverb, यद् + tasil)
स्थिताsthitāabiding, establishedpast participle (kta) of √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’), f. nom. sg.; with असि
असिasiyou are√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
अपाम्apāmof the watersf. gen. pl. of अप् (used only in the plural); depends on स्वरूप-
स्वरूपस्थितयाsvarūpasthitayāabiding in the form (of the waters)f. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वया
त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; the agent through whom the world is nourished
एतत्etatthis (world)pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject of आप्यायते, with कृत्स्नम्
आप्यायतेāpyāyateis nourished, swells, is replenishedआ + √प्यै (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to swell, grow’; present stem प्याय-), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, ātmanepada
कृत्स्नम्kṛtsnamwhole, entiren. nom. sg. of कृत्स्न; qualifies एतत्
अलङ्घ्यवीर्येalaṅghyavīryeO you whose might cannot be overcomef. voc. sg. of अलङ्घ्यवीर्या; compound (see Samāsa)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • जगतस्त्वमेका jagatastvamekā → जगतः + त्वम् + एका
    visarga sandhi; m before a vowel · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s. The final m of त्वम् stays m before the vowel of एका and is written with it.
  • स्थितासि sthitāsi → स्थिता + असि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • अपां स्वरूप apāṃ svarūpa → अपाम् + स्वरूप…
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • त्वयैतदाप्यायते tvayaitadāpyāyate → त्वया + एतत् + आप्यायते
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi); consonant sandhi (voicing) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    ā + e → ai; then the final t of एतत् becomes d before the vowel of आप्यायते. The word एतत् is broken across the printed lines (त्वयैत- / दाप्यायते); it belongs to the third line, where it begins.
  • कृत्स्नमलङ्घ्यवीर्ये kṛtsnamalaṅghyavīrye → कृत्स्नम् + अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये
    no change (m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    Final m before a vowel is kept and written together with it.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • आधारभूता ādhārabhūtā — karmadhāraya (भूत ‘being, existing as’ makes the first member a predicate)
    Members: आधार + भूत · Vigraha: आधारः भूता ādhāraḥ bhūtā
    Literally “being the support”; in the verse: she who is the ground on which the world rests
    Alternative: Compounds in -भूत are also treated as a supsupā compound (‘any case with any case’); the sense, ‘being the support’, is the same.
  • महीस्वरूपेण mahīsvarūpeṇa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मही + स्वरूप · Vigraha: मह्याः स्वरूपम्, तेन mahyāḥ svarūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form of the earth”; in the verse: as the earth itself
  • स्वरूपस्थितया svarūpasthitayā — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: स्वरूप + स्थित · Vigraha: स्वरूपे स्थिता, तया svarūpe sthitā, tayā
    Literally “abiding in the form”; in the verse: existing as (the waters); the genitive अपाम् outside the compound depends on its first member स्वरूप
  • अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये alaṅghyavīrye — bahuvrīhi (its first member अलङ्घ्य is a nañ tatpuruṣa: न लङ्घ्यम्)
    Members: अलङ्घ्य + वीर्य · Vigraha: अलङ्घ्यं वीर्यं यस्याः सा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ alaṅghyaṃ vīryaṃ yasyāḥ sā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “whose might is not to be overstepped”; in the verse: O you of invincible power

C. Morphology

  • आप्यायते āpyāyate — Present ātmanepada of आ + √प्यै (प्याय-): ‘swells, is filled, thrives’. It is intransitive, not a passive; त्वया names the one through whom the world is nourished.
  • अपाम् apām — Genitive plural of अप् ‘water’, a feminine noun used only in the plural (nom. आपः, instr. अद्भिः, gen. अपाम्).
  • अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये alaṅghyavīrye — लङ्घ्य is a gerundive (√लङ्घ् ‘to leap over’ + -ya): ‘to be overstepped’; with the negative अ-, ‘not to be overstepped’, i.e. irresistible.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) अलङ्घ्यवीर्ये, त्वम् एका जगतः आधारभूता (असि), यतः महीस्वरूपेण स्थिता असि। अपां स्वरूपस्थितया त्वया एतत् कृत्स्नम् आप्यायते।

(he) alaṅghyavīrye, tvam ekā jagataḥ ādhārabhūtā (asi), yataḥ mahīsvarūpeṇa sthitā asi | apāṃ svarūpasthitayā tvayā etat kṛtsnam āpyāyate |

Two sentences: the first states that she alone is the support of the world and gives the reason in a यतः clause; the second, with the intransitive verb आप्यायते, says the whole world is nourished through her as water. The vocative stands at the end.

6Translation

LiteralYou alone are the support of the world, since you abide in the form of the earth. By you, abiding in the form of the waters, this whole (world) is nourished, O you whose might cannot be overcome.

ReadableYou alone are the ground on which the world rests, for you abide as the earth. And as the waters you nourish this whole world, O Goddess of invincible power.

The tr turns ‘by you … this whole is nourished’ into an active sentence (‘you nourish’); the vocative is rendered ‘O Goddess of invincible power’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods name two of the elements as her forms. As earth she holds the world up; as water she makes it swell and thrive. The word यतः makes the first line a reason: she is the world’s support because she is the earth.

Traditional interpretationThe verse continues the teaching of Brahmā’s hymn, that the Goddess is not only the power behind the world but its very substance (1.76: त्वयैतत्पाल्यते, ‘by you this is protected’). Earth and water are named because they are the ground and the nourishment of life; the tradition reads them as standing for all the elements.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names मही (718), ‘the earth’, and सर्वाधारा (659), ‘the support of all’. The agreement is in shared Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate works.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the ground under one’s feet and the water one drinks are named here as the Goddess herself. Reverence begins with what is most ordinary and most needed.

11.5Ślokaindravajrā

1Original śloka

त्वं वैष्णवी शक्तिरनन्तवीर्या
विश्वस्य बीजं परमासि माया।
सम्मोहितं देवि समस्तमेतत्
त्वं वै प्रसन्ना भुवि मुक्तिहेतुः ॥ ५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

त्वम् | वैष्णवी | शक्तिः | अनन्तवीर्याविश्वस्य | बीजम् | परमा | असि | मायासम्मोहितम् | देवि | समस्तम् | एतत्त्वम् | वै | प्रसन्ना | भुवि | मुक्तिहेतुः

3IAST

As recitedtvaṃ vaiṣṇavī śaktiranantavīryā
viśvasya bījaṃ paramāsi māyā |
sammohitaṃ devi samastametat
tvaṃ vai prasannā bhuvi muktihetuḥ ||

Separatedtvam | vaiṣṇavī | śaktiḥ | anantavīryā
viśvasya | bījam | paramā | asi | māyā
sammohitam | devi | samastam | etat
tvam | vai | prasannā | bhuvi | muktihetuḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
वैष्णवीvaiṣṇavīof Viṣṇu, Vaiṣṇavīf. nom. sg. of वैष्णवी (taddhita from विष्णु: ‘belonging to Viṣṇu’, with feminine -ī); qualifies शक्तिः
शक्तिःśaktiḥpower, Śaktif. nom. sg. of शक्ति (i-stem); predicate
अनन्तवीर्याanantavīryāof boundless mightf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies शक्तिः
विश्वस्यviśvasyaof the universen. gen. sg. of विश्व; depends on बीजम्
बीजम्bījamthe seedn. nom. sg. of बीज; predicate
परमाparamāsupremef. nom. sg. of परम; qualifies माया
असिasiyou are√अस् (adādi, 2P), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
मायाmāyāMāyā, the power that veils and enchantsf. nom. sg. of माया; predicate
सम्मोहितम्sammohitamis held in delusion, bewilderedpast passive participle (kta) of the causative of सम् + √मुह् (divādi, 4P ‘to be confused’), n. nom. sg.; predicate of एतत्
देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
समस्तम्samastamall, entiren. nom. sg. of समस्त; qualifies एतत्
एतत्etatthis (world)pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; subject
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject
वैvaiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
प्रसन्नाprasannāwhen pleased, graciouspast participle (kta) of प्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be pleased’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies त्वम्
भुविbhuvion earthf. loc. sg. of भू (ū-stem)
मुक्तिहेतुःmuktihetuḥthe cause of liberationm. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शक्तिरनन्तवीर्या śaktiranantavīryā → शक्तिः + अनन्तवीर्या
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    Visarga after i, before a vowel, is sounded as r.
  • परमासि paramāsi → परमा + असि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.
  • सम्मोहितं देवि sammohitaṃ devi → सम्मोहितम् + देवि
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra. The same gives त्वं वैष्णवी, बीजं परमा and त्वं वै; before the vowel of एतत् the m of समस्तम् stays m (समस्तमेतत्).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अनन्तवीर्या anantavīryā — bahuvrīhi (its first member अनन्त is itself a nañ-bahuvrīhi: न विद्यते अन्तः यस्य)
    Members: अनन्त + वीर्य · Vigraha: अनन्तं वीर्यं यस्याः सा anantaṃ vīryaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “whose might has no end”; in the verse: of boundless power
  • मुक्तिहेतुः muktihetuḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मुक्ति + हेतु · Vigraha: मुक्तेः हेतुः mukteḥ hetuḥ
    Literally “the cause of release”; in the verse: the one through whom beings are set free

C. Morphology

  • सम्मोहितम् sammohitam — मोहित is the past participle of the causative मोहयति ‘causes to be bewildered’: the world ‘has been bewildered’ — by her, as the previous line implies. Compare 1.55, तया सम्मोह्यते जगत्.
  • मुक्तिहेतुः muktihetuḥ — A tatpuruṣa keeps the gender of its last member: हेतु is masculine, so ‘you (f.) are the cause’ is त्वं … मुक्तिहेतुः, as in 4.9 (या मुक्तिहेतुः).
  • वैष्णवी vaiṣṇavī — Taddhita: विष्णु + aṇ with vṛddhi of the first syllable (वैष्णव) and feminine -ī: ‘belonging to Viṣṇu, Viṣṇu’s’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

त्वम् अनन्तवीर्या वैष्णवी शक्तिः (असि)। (त्वं) विश्वस्य बीजं परमा माया असि। (हे) देवि, एतत् समस्तं (त्वया) सम्मोहितम्। त्वं वै प्रसन्ना (सती) भुवि मुक्तिहेतुः (भवसि)।

tvam anantavīryā vaiṣṇavī śaktiḥ (asi) | (tvaṃ) viśvasya bījaṃ paramā māyā asi | (he) devi, etat samastaṃ (tvayā) sammohitam | tvaṃ vai prasannā (satī) bhuvi muktihetuḥ (bhavasi) |

Four predications about the Goddess: Viṣṇu’s power, the seed of the world, the supreme Māyā (verb असि), and, when gracious, the cause of liberation. Between them stands the effect of her Māyā: the whole world is bewildered.

6Translation

LiteralYou are the power of Viṣṇu, of boundless might; you are the seed of the universe, the supreme Māyā. All this, O Goddess, is held in delusion; you indeed, when pleased, are the cause of liberation on earth.

ReadableYou are Viṣṇu’s own power, boundless in might, the seed of the universe, the supreme Māyā. By you, O Goddess, this whole world is held in enchantment; and you, when you are gracious, are the cause of liberation on earth.

‘By you’ in the third sentence is supplied: the participle सम्मोहितम् implies an agent, and the verse has just named her as Māyā.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe verse holds both sides of the Goddess together: as Māyā she keeps the whole world bewildered, and when she is pleased she is the cause of release. Binding and freeing come from the same power.

Traditional interpretationThis is the teaching of the first carita repeated in the gods’ mouths: Mahāmāyā, Hari’s power, bewilders the world (1.55), and when gracious (प्रसन्ना) she becomes the cause of liberation (1.57). ‘Vaiṣṇavī śakti’ recalls the name Viṣṇumāyā under which the gods praised her in chapter 5 (5.14–16).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names वैष्णवी (892), विष्णुमाया (339), महामाया (215) and मुक्तिदा (736), ‘giver of liberation’. The names belong to a shared Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate works.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: what clouds the mind and what clears it are not two rival forces here. The same reality appears as confusion while it is resisted and as freedom once it is met with devotion.

11.6Ślokaupajāti (triṣṭubh)

1Original śloka

विद्याः समस्तास्तव देवि भेदाः
स्त्रियः समस्ताः सकला जगत्सु।
त्वयैकया पूरितमम्बयैतत्
का ते स्तुतिः स्तव्यपरा परोक्तिः ॥ ६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

विद्याः | समस्ताः | तव | देवि | भेदाःस्त्रियः | समस्ताः | सकलाः | जगत्सुत्वया | एकया | पूरितम् | अम्बया | एतत्का | ते | स्तुतिः | स्तव्यपरा | परोक्तिः

3IAST

As recitedvidyāḥ samastāstava devi bhedāḥ
striyaḥ samastāḥ sakalā jagatsu |
tvayaikayā pūritamambayaitat
kā te stutiḥ stavyaparā paroktiḥ ||

Separatedvidyāḥ | samastāḥ | tava | devi | bhedāḥ
striyaḥ | samastāḥ | sakalāḥ | jagatsu
tvayā | ekayā | pūritam | ambayā | etat
kā | te | stutiḥ | stavyaparā | paroktiḥ

4Word by word

परोक्तिःparoktiḥan exalted utterancef. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to स्तुतिः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • समस्तास्तव samastāstava → समस्ताः + तव
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s. Before s the visarga is kept (विद्याः समस्ताः, स्त्रियः समस्ताः, समस्ताः सकलाः, स्तुतिः स्तव्यपरा).
  • सकला जगत्सु sakalā jagatsu → सकलाः + जगत्सु
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced sound becomes -ā (the y from the visarga is dropped before a consonant).
  • त्वयैकया tvayaikayā → त्वया + एकया
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + e → ai. The same in अम्बयैतत् (अम्बया + एतत्).
  • पूरितमम्बयैतत् pūritamambayaitat → पूरितम् + अम्बया + एतत्
    no change (m before a vowel); vṛddhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant; वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    The m of पूरितम् is kept before the vowel and written with it; then ā + e → ai.
  • परोक्तिः paroktiḥ → परा + उक्तिः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    In the compound the feminine परा takes the masculine stem form पर (a karmadhāraya), and a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • स्तव्यपरा stavyaparā — bahuvrīhi (पर at the end of a compound: ‘having … as its chief concern’)
    Members: स्तव्य + पर · Vigraha: स्तव्यं परं प्रधानं यस्याः सा stavyaṃ paraṃ pradhānaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “having what is to be praised as its chief concern”; in the verse: speech whose whole business is something worth praising — the definition of a hymn
    Alternative: Also read as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa, स्तव्येभ्यः परा, ‘beyond the things that can be praised’, and referred to the Goddess (‘you who are beyond the praiseworthy’). But the word is nominative and agrees with स्तुतिः, not with the genitive ते; the bahuvrīhi keeps the grammar of the line.
  • परोक्तिः paroktiḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: पर + उक्ति · Vigraha: परा उक्तिः parā uktiḥ
    Literally “a high utterance”; in the verse: lofty, excellent speech
    Alternative: Also read as परा उक्तिः in the sense of ‘the supreme Word’ (the Goddess as speech itself); again the agreement with स्तुतिः favours ‘exalted utterance’.

C. Morphology

  • स्त्रियः striyaḥ — Nominative plural of स्त्री: the ī becomes iy before a vowel ending (स्त्रि-य्-अः) instead of y, an irregularity of this word.
  • जगत्सु jagatsu — Locative plural of जगत्: the ending -su is added directly to the stem in t.
  • स्तव्यपरा stavyaparā — स्तव्य is a gerundive of √स्तु with -ya (yat): the root vowel takes guṇa (स्तो-) and o becomes av before the y (स्तव्य), ‘to be praised’.
  • पूरितम् pūritam — Participle of the causative stem पूरय- (√पॄ ‘fill’) with -ita: ‘made full, filled’. Its agent is त्वया एकया … अम्बया, ‘by you alone, the Mother’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) देवि, समस्ताः विद्याः तव भेदाः (सन्ति); जगत्सु समस्ताः सकलाः स्त्रियः (तव भेदाः)। त्वया एकया अम्बया एतत् पूरितम्। ते का स्तुतिः (स्यात्)? (स्तुतिः हि) स्तव्यपरा परोक्तिः।

(he) devi, samastāḥ vidyāḥ tava bhedāḥ (santi); jagatsu samastāḥ sakalāḥ striyaḥ (tava bhedāḥ) | tvayā ekayā ambayā etat pūritam | te kā stutiḥ (syāt)? (stutiḥ hi) stavyaparā paroktiḥ |

Two nominal sentences (all knowledge, all women are her forms); a passive one (the world is filled by her alone); then a question and, in apposition, what praise is: the last two nominatives define स्तुतिः.

6Translation

LiteralAll branches of knowledge, O Goddess, are your distinct forms; all women in the worlds, every one of them. By you, the one Mother, this (world) is filled. What praise is there of you — (praise being) an exalted utterance directed to what is to be praised?

ReadableEvery branch of knowledge, O Goddess, is a form of you, and so is every woman in all the worlds. You alone, the Mother, fill this whole world. What praise can there be of you? Praise is lofty speech about something to be praised.

The last pāda is read as a definition of praise: praise speaks of something as its object, and the verse has just said there is nothing she does not fill. The other reading (‘you who are beyond the praiseworthy, the supreme Word’) is given under Samāsa and in the flags.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningEvery form of knowledge and every woman in every world are named as her own forms, and the whole world is filled by her alone. The verse then asks what praise could be offered to such a one: praise speaks of an object, and there is nothing outside her to speak of.

Traditional interpretationThe Śākta tradition has drawn from this verse its teaching that every woman is to be honoured as a form of the Goddess. The ‘branches of knowledge’ are traditionally understood as all the sciences, sacred and secular. The doubt about praise is a classic theme of hymns: the hymn goes on praising while confessing that praise cannot reach her (compare 4.4 and 4.6, and the close of Brahmā’s hymn, 1.83–85: कस्त्वां स्तोतुमिहेश्वरः).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names विद्या (549) and विद्याऽविद्यास्वरूपिणी (402), ‘whose form is both knowledge and ignorance’. The idea is shared Śākta thought; the two texts are separate works.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the verse places the sacred in the women one actually meets and in every honest form of learning. Its reverence is meant to be practised in ordinary life, not kept for the shrine.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The last pāda, स्तव्यपरा परोक्तिः, is read in two ways: as a definition of praise in apposition to स्तुतिः (‘an exalted utterance directed to what is to be praised’), followed here because both words are nominative like स्तुतिः; or as epithets of the Goddess (‘beyond the praiseworthy, the supreme Word’), which needs त्वम् to be understood. Both readings are traditional in translation; the point of the verse — no praise can match her — is the same.

11.7Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सर्वभूता यदा देवी स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी।
त्वं स्तुता स्तुतये का वा भवन्तु परमोक्तयः ॥ ७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सर्वभूता | यदा | देवी | स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनीत्वम् | स्तुता | स्तुतये | काः | वा | भवन्तु | परमोक्तयः

3IAST

As recitedsarvabhūtā yadā devī svargamuktipradāyinī |
tvaṃ stutā stutaye kā vā bhavantu paramoktayaḥ ||

Separatedsarvabhūtā | yadā | devī | svargamuktipradāyinī
tvam | stutā | stutaye | kāḥ | vā | bhavantu | paramoktayaḥ

4Word by word

सर्वभूताsarvabhūtāwho have become all thingsf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वम् … देवी
यदाyadāwhen, sinceindeclinable (relative adverb of time)
देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी; in apposition to त्वम्
स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनीsvargamuktipradāyinīthe giver of heaven and liberationf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies त्वम् … देवी
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of स्तुता (असि understood)
स्तुताstutāare praised, have been praisedpast passive participle (kta) of √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), f. nom. sg.; predicate
स्तुतयेstutayefor praisef. dat. sg. of स्तुति (i-stem); dative of purpose
काःkāḥwhatinterrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. pl.; qualifies परमोक्तयः
वाvāthen, indeed (in a question)indeclinable (particle, here strengthening the question)
भवन्तुbhavantucould be, should be√भू (bhvādi, 1P ‘to be’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada; in a question, ‘what could be?’
परमोक्तयःparamoktayaḥsupreme utterances, loftier wordsf. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of भवन्तु

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • त्वं स्तुता tvaṃ stutā → त्वम् + स्तुता
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • का वा kā vā → काः + वा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced sound becomes -ā. The printed का is therefore the plural काः, agreeing with परमोक्तयः and भवन्तु, not the singular का.
  • परमोक्तयः paramoktayaḥ → परम + उक्तयः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वभूता sarvabhūtā — karmadhāraya (भूत ‘become, being’ with the first member as predicate, as in आधारभूता, 11.4)
    Members: सर्व + भूत · Vigraha: सर्वं भूता sarvaṃ bhūtā
    Literally “become all”; in the verse: she who is everything that exists
    Alternative: Also taken as ‘she who is all beings’ (सर्वाणि भूतानि यस्याः स्वरूपम्, a bahuvrīhi); but भूत at the end of such compounds usually means ‘being, become’, and the sense is the same.
  • स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी svargamuktipradāyinī — upapada tatpuruṣa (-प्रदायिन्, from प्र + √दा with -in) containing an itaretara dvandva (स्वर्गमुक्ती)
    Members: स्वर्ग + मुक्ति + प्रदायिन् · Vigraha: स्वर्गः च मुक्तिः च स्वर्गमुक्ती; ते प्रददाति इति स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी svargaḥ ca muktiḥ ca svargamuktī; te pradadāti iti svargamuktipradāyinī
    Literally “she who gives heaven and liberation”; in the verse: the giver of both worldly-heavenly reward and final release
  • परमोक्तयः paramoktayaḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: परम + उक्ति · Vigraha: परमाः उक्तयः paramāḥ uktayaḥ
    Literally “supreme utterances”; in the verse: words higher still, more exalted praise

C. Morphology

  • प्रदायिनी pradāyinī — From प्र + √दा with the agent suffix -in: before it the root in ā takes y (दा → दाय्), giving प्रदायिन्, feminine प्रदायिनी ‘giver’.
  • भवन्तु bhavantu — An imperative used in a rhetorical question: ‘what loftier words should there be?’ — i.e. none could be.
  • स्तुतये stutaye — Dative of स्तुति: i-stems take guṇa before the dative ending (स्तुते + ए → स्तुतये). The dative expresses purpose: ‘for (the sake of) praise’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यदा सर्वभूता स्वर्गमुक्तिप्रदायिनी देवी त्वं स्तुता (असि), (तदा) स्तुतये काः वा परमोक्तयः भवन्तु?

yadā sarvabhūtā svargamuktipradāyinī devī tvaṃ stutā (asi), (tadā) stutaye kāḥ vā paramoktayaḥ bhavantu?

A यदा clause (with तदा understood) and a rhetorical question: once she has been named as all that is and as the giver of heaven and liberation, no further words for praise remain.

6Translation

LiteralWhen you, the Goddess who have become all things, the giver of heaven and liberation, are (thus) praised, what supreme utterances could there be for praise?

ReadableWhen you, the Goddess who are all that is, who grant both heaven and liberation, have been praised as that, what loftier words could be left to praise you?

‘As that’ makes explicit that the praise consists in the epithets just given; the Sanskrit says only ‘you are praised’. ‘Left’ renders the force of the rhetorical question.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe upajāti stanzas end and the anuṣṭubh verses begin. The gods close their first argument: to say that she has become all things, and that she grants both heaven and liberation, is already the whole of praise; no finer words remain.

Traditional interpretationThe verse completes the thought of 11.6. Since everything is her form, naming her as everything is the highest praise. From the next verse the hymn turns from statement to salutation, ending each verse with नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name स्वर्गापवर्गदा (764), ‘giver of heaven and liberation’, which the next verse here also uses (स्वर्गापवर्गदे, 11.8). The two texts are separate works.

11.8Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सर्वस्य बुद्धिरूपेण जनस्य हृदि संस्थिते।
स्वर्गापवर्गदे देवि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ ८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सर्वस्य | बुद्धिरूपेण | जनस्य | हृदि | संस्थितेस्वर्गापवर्गदे | देवि | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedsarvasya buddhirūpeṇa janasya hṛdi saṃsthite |
svargāpavargade devi nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedsarvasya | buddhirūpeṇa | janasya | hṛdi | saṃsthite
svargāpavargade | devi | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सर्वस्यsarvasyaof everypronominal adjective सर्व, m. gen. sg.; qualifies जनस्य
बुद्धिरूपेणbuddhirūpeṇain the form of understandingn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
जनस्यjanasyaperson, beingm. gen. sg. of जन; depends on हृदि
हृदिhṛdiin the heartn. loc. sg. of हृद् (used for हृदय)
संस्थितेsaṃsthiteO you who abidef. voc. sg. of संस्थिता, past participle (kta) of सम् + √स्था (bhvādi, 1P ‘to stand’)
स्वर्गापवर्गदेsvargāpavargadeO giver of heaven and final releasef. voc. sg. of स्वर्गापवर्गदा; compound (see Samāsa)
देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी (feminine of नारायण, with -ī: ‘she who belongs to, or is the power of, Nārāyaṇa’)
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस् (s-stem); subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic form); dative with नमः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • संस्थिते saṃsthite → सम् + स्थिते
    anusvāra sandhi with a prefix · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    The m of the prefix सम् becomes anusvāra before s.
  • स्वर्गापवर्गदे svargāpavargade → स्वर्ग + अपवर्ग + दे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a: the s (as रु) becomes u, a + u → o, and the following a is elided (avagraha). This junction closes every verse of the refrain.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • बुद्धिरूपेण buddhirūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (avadhāraṇa), as in the yā devī series (5.20-22)
    Members: बुद्धि + रूप · Vigraha: बुद्धिः एव रूपम्, तेन buddhiḥ eva rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form that is understanding”; in the verse: as the intellect, the discerning mind
    Alternative: Also read as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, बुद्धेः रूपम् ‘the form of understanding’; the sense is the same.
  • स्वर्गापवर्गदे svargāpavargade — upapada tatpuruṣa (-द ‘giving’, from √दा) containing an itaretara dvandva (स्वर्गापवर्गौ)
    Members: स्वर्ग + अपवर्ग + द · Vigraha: स्वर्गः च अपवर्गः च स्वर्गापवर्गौ; तौ ददाति इति स्वर्गापवर्गदा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ svargaḥ ca apavargaḥ ca svargāpavargau; tau dadāti iti svargāpavargadā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “giver of heaven and release”; in the verse: she who grants both the reward of heaven and final liberation (अपवर्ग = मोक्ष)

C. Morphology

  • नारायणि nārāyaṇi — Vocative of नारायणी: ī-stems shorten the final vowel in the vocative (नारायणी → नारायणि). The feminine of नारायण names the Goddess as Nārāyaṇa’s power, or Nārāyaṇa in feminine form.
  • अस्तु astu — Imperative third person of √अस्: ‘let (homage) be’. With नमः and the dative ते it forms the standard formula of salutation.
  • हृदि hṛdi — Locative of the consonant stem हृद्, which supplies the oblique cases of हृदय ‘heart’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) सर्वस्य जनस्य हृदि बुद्धिरूपेण संस्थिते, स्वर्गापवर्गदे देवि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) sarvasya janasya hṛdi buddhirūpeṇa saṃsthite, svargāpavargade devi nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

A chain of vocatives (संस्थिते … स्वर्गापवर्गदे … देवि … नारायणि) and one main clause, नमः अस्तु ते ‘homage be to you’ — the pattern of every refrain verse.

6Translation

LiteralO you who abide in the heart of every person in the form of understanding, giver of heaven and final release, O Goddess, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableYou dwell as understanding in the heart of every being, and you grant both heaven and final release: O Goddess Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

The vocative epithets are turned into statements (‘You dwell …, you grant …’) and the address is gathered at the end.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first verse of the refrain. The Goddess is greeted as the understanding (buddhi) that lives in every heart, and as the giver of heaven and final release; the line नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते, ‘O Nārāyaṇī, homage to you’, now closes every verse to 11.23.

Traditional interpretationThe refrain gives the hymn its name, Nārāyaṇī Stuti. The thought of the first line repeats the yā devī stanza of chapter 5, या देवी सर्वभूतेषु बुद्धिरूपेण संस्थिता (5.20–22), and the second line returns at the end of the text, where the Goddess, worshipped, is भोगस्वर्गापवर्गदा (13.5).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has both names: स्वर्गापवर्गदा (764) and नारायणी (298), and also महाबुद्धिः (223). The agreement is in shared Śākta vocabulary; the two texts are separate works.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the Goddess is placed first not in heaven but in the mind’s power to understand. Clear understanding, on this view, is already her presence, and the way to both the good life and release runs through it.

11.9Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण परिणामप्रदायिनि।
विश्वस्योपरतौ शक्ते नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ ९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण | परिणामप्रदायिनिविश्वस्य | उपरतौ | शक्ते | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedkalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa pariṇāmapradāyini |
viśvasyoparatau śakte nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedkalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa | pariṇāmapradāyini
viśvasya | uparatau | śakte | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेणkalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇain the form of kalās, kāṣṭhās and the other units of timen. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
परिणामप्रदायिनिpariṇāmapradāyiniO you who bring about changef. voc. sg. of परिणामप्रदायिनी; compound (see Samāsa)
विश्वस्यviśvasyaof the universen. gen. sg. of विश्व; depends on उपरतौ
उपरतौuparatauin the ceasing, in the dissolutionf. loc. sg. of उपरति (i-stem; उप + √रम् ‘to cease’ + -ti)
शक्तेśakteO Power (able to bring it about)f. voc. sg. of शक्ति (i-stem); see Morph for शक्ता
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa → कला + काष्ठा + आदि + रूपेण
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + ā → ā (काष्ठा + आदि).
  • विश्वस्योपरतौ viśvasyoparatau → विश्वस्य + उपरतौ
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, containing a bahuvrīhi (-आदि ‘and the rest’) built on an itaretara dvandva (कलाकाष्ठे)
    Members: कला + काष्ठा + आदि + रूप · Vigraha: कला च काष्ठा च कलाकाष्ठे; कलाकाष्ठे आदी येषां ते कलाकाष्ठादयः; तेषां रूपम्, तेन kalā ca kāṣṭhā ca kalākāṣṭhe; kalākāṣṭhe ādī yeṣāṃ te kalākāṣṭhādayaḥ; teṣāṃ rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form of kalā, kāṣṭhā and the rest”; in the verse: as time itself, in its measured units
  • परिणामप्रदायिनि pariṇāmapradāyini — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: परिणाम + प्रदायिन् · Vigraha: परिणामं प्रददाति इति परिणामप्रदायिनी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ pariṇāmaṃ pradadāti iti pariṇāmapradāyinī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “giver of transformation”; in the verse: she who brings every thing through its changes: growth, ripening, decay

C. Morphology

  • शक्ते śakte — The vocative शक्ते belongs equally to शक्ति ‘power’ (i-stems take guṇa: शक्ति → शक्ते) and to शक्ता ‘able’ (ā-stems: शक्ता → शक्ते). As शक्ता with the locative it means ‘O you who are able to bring about the world’s dissolution’; as शक्ति, ‘O Power at work in the world’s dissolution’. The hymn calls her शक्ति in 11.5 and शक्तिभूते in 11.11, which favours शक्ति; the sense is close either way.
  • उपरतौ uparatau — Locative of उपरति (√रम् with उप ‘to cease’; the nasal is lost before -ti): ‘in the cessation’, i.e. at the dissolution of the universe.
  • परिणामप्रदायिनि pariṇāmapradāyini — Vocative of the feminine in -inī (प्रदायिन् + ī), shortened to -ini in address.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण परिणामप्रदायिनि, विश्वस्य उपरतौ शक्ते नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) kalākāṣṭhādirūpeṇa pariṇāmapradāyini, viśvasya uparatau śakte nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Vocatives (परिणामप्रदायिनि, शक्ते, नारायणि) followed by the refrain; the instrumental कलाकाष्ठादिरूपेण says how she brings change, the locative विश्वस्य उपरतौ where her power is shown.

6Translation

LiteralO you who, in the form of kalās, kāṣṭhās and the rest, bring about change; O Power in the dissolution of the universe, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableAs the moments and measures of time you bring all things through their changes, and yours is the power that brings the universe to rest: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

‘Moments and measures of time’ renders कला, काष्ठा and the rest, which are small units of time; ‘brings to rest’ renders उपरति ‘cessation’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is time. As its units — kāṣṭhā, kalā and the rest — she brings every thing through its changes, and she is the power by which the universe at last comes to rest.

Traditional interpretationIn the classical reckoning of time a kāṣṭhā is eighteen blinks (nimeṣa) and thirty kāṣṭhās make a kalā; the verse names the smallest measures to say that no moment is outside her. परिणाम, ‘transformation’, is the Sāṃkhya word for the change of prakṛti, and the text has already called her the supreme prakṛti (4.7, 1.78).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: change and ending are named here among her gifts. The same power that sustains things also lets them pass, and both are met with salutation.

11.10Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये शिवे सर्वार्थसाधिके।
शरण्ये त्र्यम्बके गौरि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ १० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये | शिवे | सर्वार्थसाधिकेशरण्ये | त्र्यम्बके | गौरि | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedsarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye śive sarvārthasādhike |
śaraṇye tryambake gauri nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedsarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye | śive | sarvārthasādhike
śaraṇye | tryambake | gauri | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्येsarvamaṅgalamaṅgalyeO auspiciousness of all that is auspiciousf. voc. sg. of सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्या; compound (see Samāsa)
शिवेśiveO auspicious one, O Śivāf. voc. sg. of शिवा
सर्वार्थसाधिकेsarvārthasādhikeO you who accomplish every aimf. voc. sg. of सर्वार्थसाधिका; compound (see Samāsa)
शरण्येśaraṇyeO refuge, O you who give shelterf. voc. sg. of शरण्या (taddhita from शरण with -ya: ‘fit for refuge, protecting’)
त्र्यम्बकेtryambakeO three-eyed onef. voc. sg. of त्र्यम्बका; compound (see Samāsa)
गौरिgauriO Gaurī, O fair onef. voc. sg. of गौरी
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वार्थसाधिके sarvārthasādhike → सर्व + अर्थ + साधिके
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • त्र्यम्बके tryambake → त्रि + अम्बके
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before a vowel becomes y.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8. Between the vocatives of this verse there is no junction to make: each ends in a vowel and the next begins with a consonant.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, its first member a karmadhāraya (सर्वमङ्गल)
    Members: सर्व + मङ्गल + मङ्गल्य · Vigraha: सर्वाणि मङ्गलानि सर्वमङ्गलानि; सर्वमङ्गलानां मङ्गल्या सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्या, तत्सम्बुद्धौ sarvāṇi maṅgalāni sarvamaṅgalāni; sarvamaṅgalānāṃ maṅgalyā sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalyā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “the auspicious one of all auspicious things”; in the verse: she who makes every auspicious thing auspicious — the source of all blessing
    Alternative: Also read with a superlative sense, ‘the most auspicious among all auspicious things’; a partitive (‘among’) relation is expressed by the locative, सर्वमङ्गलेषु मङ्गल्या, a saptamī tatpuruṣa. The source-sense fits the verse’s run of epithets of blessing.
  • सर्वार्थसाधिके sarvārthasādhike — upapada tatpuruṣa (-साधिका, agent noun of the causative of √साध्) containing a karmadhāraya (सर्वार्थ)
    Members: सर्व + अर्थ + साधिका · Vigraha: सर्वे अर्थाः सर्वार्थाः; सर्वार्थान् साधयति इति सर्वार्थसाधिका, तत्सम्बुद्धौ sarve arthāḥ sarvārthāḥ; sarvārthān sādhayati iti sarvārthasādhikā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “accomplisher of all aims”; in the verse: she who brings every purpose of her devotees to fulfilment
  • त्र्यम्बके tryambake — bahuvrīhi
    Members: त्रि + अम्बक · Vigraha: त्रीणि अम्बकानि यस्याः सा त्र्यम्बका, तत्सम्बुद्धौ trīṇi ambakāni yasyāḥ sā tryambakā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she who has three eyes”; in the verse: the three-eyed one, the consort of Tryambaka (Śiva)
    Alternative: अम्बक is ‘eye’; the name is sometimes also connected with अम्बा ‘mother’, but the grammar of the word supports ‘three-eyed’.

C. Morphology

  • सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye — मङ्गल्य is a taddhita adjective from मङ्गल with -ya, ‘auspicious, bringing good fortune’; feminine मङ्गल्या, vocative -ल्ये. The variant माङ्गल्य (with vṛddhi) is the abstract noun ‘auspiciousness’ — see the flags.
  • सर्वार्थसाधिके sarvārthasādhike — साधक ‘one who accomplishes’ (causative of √साध् with -aka); in the feminine the a before k becomes i: साधिका.
  • गौरि gauri — Vocative of गौरी (‘the fair one’, the name of Pārvatī): the final ī is shortened in address.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये शिवे सर्वार्थसाधिके शरण्ये त्र्यम्बके गौरि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) sarvamaṅgalamaṅgalye śive sarvārthasādhike śaraṇye tryambake gauri nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Seven vocatives in a row, then the refrain नमः अस्तु ते.

6Translation

LiteralO auspiciousness of all that is auspicious, O Śivā, accomplisher of every aim, O refuge, O three-eyed one, O Gaurī, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableBlessing of all that is blessed, auspicious Śivā, who bring every aim to fulfilment, our refuge, three-eyed Gaurī: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

शिवे is rendered both by its meaning ‘auspicious’ and as the name Śivā; the vocatives are grouped into phrases.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningA verse made only of names. She is the blessing in all that is blessed, Śivā, the one who fulfils every aim, the refuge, the three-eyed Gaurī — and, in the refrain, Nārāyaṇī.

Traditional interpretationThis is the most widely recited verse of the Devī Māhātmya; it is said on its own as a verse of blessing at the beginning and end of worship and on auspicious occasions. Its names belong to Śiva’s consort (Śivā, Tryambakā, Gaurī), while the refrain names her as Nārāyaṇī, the power of Viṣṇu. The tradition reads this as the text’s teaching that the powers of all the gods are one Goddess (as in her birth from the gods’ combined radiance in chapter 2).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has सर्वमङ्गला (200), शिवा (53), त्र्यम्बका (762), गौरी (635) and नारायणी (298). Most names of this verse are among its names; the two texts are separate works.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: a verse of pure address, asking nothing. It is recited at beginnings and endings because it names the good before any request is made.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Variant (corrections.json, status ‘Textual Verification Required’): GRETIL and Freund read सर्वमङ्गलमाङ्गल्ये, with the vṛddhi form माङ्गल्य (‘auspiciousness’). The Gita Press सर्वमङ्गलमङ्गल्ये, the form generally recited, is kept. The metre is unaffected (both are eight syllables, with the sixth heavy either way), and the sense — she is the auspiciousness of all auspicious things — is the same.

11.11Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानां शक्तिभूते सनातनि।
गुणाश्रये गुणमये नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ ११ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम् | शक्तिभूते | सनातनिगुणाश्रये | गुणमये | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedsṛṣṭisthitivināśānāṃ śaktibhūte sanātani |
guṇāśraye guṇamaye nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedsṛṣṭisthitivināśānām | śaktibhūte | sanātani
guṇāśraye | guṇamaye | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम्sṛṣṭisthitivināśānāmof creation, preservation and destructionm. gen. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); depends on शक्ति-
शक्तिभूतेśaktibhūteO you who are the powerf. voc. sg. of शक्तिभूता; compound (see Samāsa)
सनातनिsanātaniO eternal onef. voc. sg. of सनातनी (feminine of सनातन ‘everlasting’)
गुणाश्रयेguṇāśrayeO resting-place of the guṇasf. voc. sg. of गुणाश्रया; compound (see Samāsa)
गुणमयेguṇamayeO you who consist of the guṇasf. voc. sg. of गुणमयी (taddhita: गुण + -maya ‘consisting of’, feminine -ī)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानां शक्तिभूते sṛṣṭisthitivināśānāṃ śaktibhūte → सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम् + शक्तिभूते
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • गुणाश्रये guṇāśraye → गुण + आश्रये
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम् sṛṣṭisthitivināśānām — itaretara dvandva
    Members: सृष्टि + स्थिति + विनाश · Vigraha: सृष्टिः च स्थितिः च विनाशः च सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशाः, तेषाम् sṛṣṭiḥ ca sthitiḥ ca vināśaḥ ca sṛṣṭisthitivināśāḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of creation, continuance and destruction”; in the verse: of the three acts of the world: its making, its keeping and its ending
    Alternative: The compound takes the gender of its last member (विनाश, m.) and the plural for three members.
  • शक्तिभूते śaktibhūte — karmadhāraya (भूत ‘being, existing as’, as in आधारभूता, 11.4)
    Members: शक्ति + भूत · Vigraha: शक्तिः भूता, तत्सम्बुद्धौ śaktiḥ bhūtā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “being the power”; in the verse: you who are the very power behind creation, preservation and dissolution
  • गुणाश्रये guṇāśraye — bahuvrīhi (vyadhikaraṇa)
    Members: गुण + आश्रय · Vigraha: गुणानाम् आश्रयः यस्यां सा गुणाश्रया, तत्सम्बुद्धौ guṇānām āśrayaḥ yasyāṃ sā guṇāśrayā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she in whom the guṇas have their resting-place”; in the verse: the ground on which the three guṇas — sattva, rajas, tamas — rest
    Alternative: Often translated as a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, गुणानाम् आश्रयः ‘support of the guṇas’; but a tatpuruṣa keeps the gender of आश्रय (m.), and the feminine vocative -आश्रये shows the bahuvrīhi. The meaning is the same.

C. Morphology

  • गुणमये guṇamaye — -मय (mayaṭ) means ‘made of, consisting of’; its feminine is -मयी (vocative -मये). गुणाश्रये and गुणमये together say that she both supports the guṇas and is made of them.
  • सनातनि sanātani — सनातन ‘eternal’ (from सना ‘always’) with feminine -ī; vocative with the ī shortened.
  • सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानाम् sṛṣṭisthitivināśānām — Genitive plural of an a-stem dvandva: -आनाम्. It depends on the first member of the next word (शक्ति-), a construction common in verse.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) सृष्टिस्थितिविनाशानां शक्तिभूते सनातनि गुणाश्रये गुणमये नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) sṛṣṭisthitivināśānāṃ śaktibhūte sanātani guṇāśraye guṇamaye nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Vocatives (शक्तिभूते, सनातनि, गुणाश्रये, गुणमये, नारायणि) followed by the refrain; the genitive dvandva depends on शक्ति-.

6Translation

LiteralO you who are the power of creation, preservation and destruction, O eternal one, O resting-place of the guṇas, O you who consist of the guṇas, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableEternal one, you are the power that creates, sustains and destroys; the guṇas rest in you and are made of you: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

The vocative epithets are turned into statements. ‘Are made of you’ renders गुणमये (‘consisting of the guṇas’) from the other side; a closer rendering is ‘you are made of the guṇas’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is the single power behind the three acts of the world — creation, continuance and destruction — and the eternal ground of the three guṇas, which both rest in her and make up her form.

Traditional interpretationThe verse gathers the teaching of Brahmā’s hymn: in creation she is creation, in preserving she is preservation, at the end she is dissolution (1.76–77), and she is the prakṛti that brings forth the three guṇas (1.78, गुणत्रयविभाविनी). The tradition links the three acts with the three guṇas (rajas, sattva, tamas) and with Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Rudra, all of whom act through her power. The word सनातनी returns in 12.39.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has सृष्टिकर्त्री (264), संहारिणी (268), त्रिगुणात्मिका (763) and also गुणातीता (961), ‘beyond the guṇas’. The two texts are separate works; together the names show the Śākta view that she is both within and beyond the guṇas.

11.12Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे।
सर्वस्यार्तिहरे देवि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ १२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणेसर्वस्य | आर्तिहरे | देवि | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedśaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe |
sarvasyārtihare devi nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedśaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe
sarvasya | ārtihare | devi | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणेśaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇeO you who are wholly intent on saving the wretched and afflicted who come to you for refugef. voc. sg. of -परायणा; compound (see Samāsa)
सर्वस्यsarvasyaof all, of everyonepronominal adjective सर्व, m./n. gen. sg.; depends on आर्ति-
आर्तिहरेārtihareO remover of distressf. voc. sg. of आर्तिहरा; compound (see Samāsa)
देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe → शरण + आगत + दीन + आर्त + परित्राण + पर + अयने
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha, three times) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā (शरणागत, दीनार्त) and a + a → ā (परायण). The n of अयन becomes ṇ after the r of पर (परायण), by the rule of retroflexion inside a word.
  • सर्वस्यार्तिहरे sarvasyārtihare → सर्वस्य + आर्तिहरे
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe — bahuvrīhi (-परायण ‘having … as highest goal’), containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (…परित्राण), a karmadhāraya (शरणागतदीनार्त), a dvitīyā tatpuruṣa (शरणागत) and a dvandva or karmadhāraya (दीनार्त)
    Members: शरणागतदीनार्त + परित्राण + परायण · Vigraha: शरणम् आगताः शरणागताः; दीनाः च ते आर्ताः च दीनार्ताः; शरणागताः दीनार्ताः शरणागतदीनार्ताः; तेषां परित्राणम्; तत् परम् अयनं यस्याः सा शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ śaraṇam āgatāḥ śaraṇāgatāḥ; dīnāḥ ca te ārtāḥ ca dīnārtāḥ; śaraṇāgatāḥ dīnārtāḥ śaraṇāgatadīnārtāḥ; teṣāṃ paritrāṇam; tat param ayanaṃ yasyāḥ sā śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she whose highest aim is the rescue of the wretched and afflicted who have come for refuge”; in the verse: she who lives to save the helpless who turn to her
    Alternative: शरणागतदीनार्त can also be a three-member dvandva, ‘those who have taken refuge, the wretched and the afflicted’ (three groups instead of one). The karmadhāraya, one group described three ways, is the more common reading; the sense differs little.
  • आर्तिहरे ārtihare — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: आर्ति + हर · Vigraha: आर्तिं हरति इति आर्तिहरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ ārtiṃ harati iti ārtiharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “remover of distress”; in the verse: as in 11.3 (प्रपन्नार्तिहरे); the genitive सर्वस्य outside the compound depends on its first member आर्ति

C. Morphology

  • शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe — A single compound fills the whole first line (eight syllables). परायण at the end of a compound means ‘wholly devoted to, intent on’ (lit. ‘having … as the highest resort’). परित्राण is the action noun of परि + √त्रै ‘to rescue’.
  • आर्तिहरे ārtihare — -हर is the agent of √हृ ‘to take away’ in compounds, feminine -हरा; vocative -हरे.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) शरणागतदीनार्तपरित्राणपरायणे, सर्वस्य आर्तिहरे देवि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) śaraṇāgatadīnārtaparitrāṇaparāyaṇe, sarvasya ārtihare devi nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Two vocative phrases and the refrain; the first line is a single compound.

6Translation

LiteralO you who are wholly intent on rescuing the wretched and afflicted who have come for refuge, O Goddess who take away the distress of all, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableYou live to rescue the helpless and suffering who come to you for shelter, and you take away the sorrow of all: O Goddess Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

The vocatives are turned into statements; ‘live to rescue’ renders -परायणे, ‘wholly intent on’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess is greeted as the one whose whole purpose is to rescue those who come to her in helplessness and pain, and who takes away the distress of all.

Traditional interpretationThe verse returns to the first word of the hymn: प्रपन्नार्तिहरे (11.3) becomes सर्वस्यार्तिहरे, and the prapanna, the one who has come for refuge, becomes the शरणागत. The tradition values the verse as a prayer of surrender: it asks for nothing but names her as the refuge of the helpless.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the verse does not ask the one who comes for refuge to be worthy, only to be in need and to come. Refuge is described from the side of the one who gives it.

11.13Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि।
कौशाम्भःक्षरिके देवि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ १३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे | ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणिकौशाम्भःक्षरिके | देवि | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedhaṃsayuktavimānasthe brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi |
kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike devi nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedhaṃsayuktavimānasthe | brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi
kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike | devi | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणिbrahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇiO you who bear the form of Brahmāṇīf. voc. sg. of ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणी; compound (see Samāsa)
कौशाम्भःक्षरिकेkauśāmbhaḥkṣarikeO you who sprinkle water with kuśa grassf. voc. sg. of कौशाम्भःक्षरिका; compound (see Samāsa)
देविdeviO Goddessf. voc. sg. of देवी
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी

अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • कौशाम्भःक्षरिके kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike → कौश + अम्भः + क्षरिके
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha); visarga kept · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    a + a → ā (कौशाम्भः). The s-stem अम्भस् shows visarga before the unvoiced k of क्षरिका, kept as printed.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे haṃsayuktavimānasthe — upapada tatpuruṣa (-स्थ ‘standing, seated’), containing a karmadhāraya (हंसयुक्तविमान) and a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (हंसयुक्त)
    Members: हंस + युक्त + विमान + स्थ · Vigraha: हंसैः युक्तम् हंसयुक्तम्; हंसयुक्तं च तत् विमानं च हंसयुक्तविमानम्; हंसयुक्तविमाने तिष्ठति इति हंसयुक्तविमानस्था, तत्सम्बुद्धौ haṃsaiḥ yuktam haṃsayuktam; haṃsayuktaṃ ca tat vimānaṃ ca haṃsayuktavimānam; haṃsayuktavimāne tiṣṭhati iti haṃsayuktavimānasthā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she who stands in the car yoked with swans”; in the verse: Brahmāṇī, riding the swan-drawn chariot of Brahmā
  • ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi — upapada tatpuruṣa (-धारिन् ‘bearing’) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: ब्रह्माणी + रूप + धारिन् · Vigraha: ब्रह्माण्याः रूपम् ब्रह्माणीरूपम्; तत् धारयति इति ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ brahmāṇyāḥ rūpam brahmāṇīrūpam; tat dhārayati iti brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “bearer of the form of Brahmāṇī”; in the verse: you who take the form of Brahmā’s Śakti
  • कौशाम्भःक्षरिके kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike — upapada tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (कौशाम्भस्)
    Members: कौश + अम्भस् + क्षरिका · Vigraha: कौशम् अम्भः कौशाम्भः; तत् क्षरयति इति कौशाम्भःक्षरिका, तत्सम्बुद्धौ kauśam ambhaḥ kauśāmbhaḥ; tat kṣarayati iti kauśāmbhaḥkṣarikā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she who makes kuśa-water flow”; in the verse: you who sprinkle water consecrated with kuśa grass

C. Morphology

  • कौशाम्भःक्षरिके kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike — कौश is a taddhita from कुश (with vṛddhi): ‘of kuśa grass’, i.e. water sprinkled with or sanctified by kuśa. क्षरिका is an agent noun from √क्षर् ‘to flow’ (here ‘to make flow, sprinkle’) with the suffix -aka, feminine -ikā; the formation is uncommon, and the word is known chiefly from this verse.
  • ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi — ब्रह्माणी is the feminine of ब्रह्मन् formed with -ānī (like इन्द्राणी, रुद्राणी): Brahmā’s consort or power. -धारिन् (√धृ with -in) ‘bearing’, feminine -धारिणी.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) हंसयुक्तविमानस्थे ब्रह्माणीरूपधारिणि कौशाम्भःक्षरिके देवि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) haṃsayuktavimānasthe brahmāṇīrūpadhāriṇi kauśāmbhaḥkṣarike devi nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Three descriptive vocatives naming Brahmāṇī, then देवि नारायणि and the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO you who are seated in the celestial car yoked with swans, who bear the form of Brahmāṇī, who sprinkle water with kuśa grass, O Goddess, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableRiding the swan-drawn chariot in the form of Brahmāṇī, sprinkling the water hallowed with kuśa grass: O Goddess Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

‘Hallowed with kuśa’ interprets कौश- (‘of kuśa’): in rites, water is sprinkled with a bunch of kuśa grass.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningWith this verse the hymn turns to the Mātṛkās, the Śaktis of the gods who fought beside the Goddess against Raktabīja’s army (chapter 8): Brahmāṇī here, then Māheśvarī, Kaumārī, Vaiṣṇavī, Vārāhī, Nārasiṃhī, Aindrī, Śivadūtī and Cāmuṇḍā (11.14–21). Each is greeted as a form of the one Nārāyaṇī.

Traditional interpretationThe picture is the one given in the battle: Brahmāṇī came on a car drawn by swans, with rosary and water-pot (8.15), and struck down the asuras by sprinkling water from her kamaṇḍalu (8.33), water made pure by mantra (9.38). The hymn turns her weapon into an image of blessing: the sprinkling of consecrated water.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also has the name ब्रह्माणी (821). The Mātṛkās are a shared Śākta group; the two texts are separate works.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: क्षरिका is a rare formation (from √क्षर् ‘to flow, let flow’); the sense ‘sprinkler of water’ is clear from the context and from 8.33, but the exact derivation is not certain.

11.14Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे महावृषभवाहिनि।

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda
