    a + a → ā (चन्द्र + अहि → चन्द्राहि).
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे triśūlacandrāhidhare — upapada tatpuruṣa (-धर ‘bearing’) containing an itaretara dvandva
    Members: त्रिशूल + चन्द्र + अहि + धर · Vigraha: त्रिशूलं च चन्द्रः च अहिः च त्रिशूलचन्द्राहयः; तान् धरति इति त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ triśūlaṃ ca candraḥ ca ahiḥ ca triśūlacandrāhayaḥ; tān dharati iti triśūlacandrāhidharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “bearer of trident, moon and serpent”; in the verse: you who carry Śiva’s emblems: the trident in hand, the crescent moon on the head, the serpent as ornament
  • महावृषभवाहिनि mahāvṛṣabhavāhini — upapada tatpuruṣa (-वाहिन्, from the causative of √वह्: ‘one who has (it) carry her, rider’) containing a karmadhāraya (महावृषभ)
    Members: महावृषभ + वाहिन् · Vigraha: महान् च असौ वृषभः च महावृषभः; महावृषभं वाहयति इति महावृषभवाहिनी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahān ca asau vṛṣabhaḥ ca mahāvṛṣabhaḥ; mahāvṛṣabhaṃ vāhayati iti mahāvṛṣabhavāhinī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she who rides the great bull”; in the verse: riding Nandin, Śiva’s bull
    Alternative: Also analysed with the possessive suffix -in on वाह ‘mount’: ‘having the great bull as her mount’ (महावृषभः वाहः अस्याः अस्ति). The sense is the same.
  • माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण māheśvarīsvarūpeṇa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: माहेश्वरी + स्वरूप · Vigraha: माहेश्वर्याः स्वरूपम्, तेन māheśvaryāḥ svarūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form of Māheśvarī”; in the verse: as Māheśvarī, the Śakti of Maheśvara (Śiva)

C. Morphology

  • माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण māheśvarīsvarūpeṇa — An instrumental, not a vocative: the verse has no vocative ‘O Māheśvarī’ but says ‘(you who are) in the form of Māheśvarī’. The refrain’s नारायणि is the only name in the address.
  • त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे triśūlacandrāhidhare — -धर (√धृ with -a) ‘bearing, holding’, feminine -धरा; vocative -धरे.
  • महावृषभवाहिनि mahāvṛṣabhavāhini — महत् ‘great’ becomes महा at the head of a karmadhāraya (a rule of compound formation, not of sandhi), as in महासुर (11.2).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे महावृषभवाहिनि, माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण (स्थिते) नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) triśūlacandrāhidhare mahāvṛṣabhavāhini, māheśvarīsvarūpeṇa (sthite) nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Two vocatives, an instrumental of manner (with a participle such as स्थिते understood), and the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO bearer of the trident, the moon and the serpent, O rider of the great bull, O Nārāyaṇī (who are) in the form of Māheśvarī, homage be to you.

ReadableBearing the trident, the crescent moon and the serpent, riding the great bull in the form of Māheśvarī: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

‘Crescent’ is added to ‘moon’ (चन्द्र), as Māheśvarī is described in 8.16 with the moon’s digit (चन्द्ररेखा).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe second of the Mātṛkās: Māheśvarī, Śiva’s Śakti, with his emblems — the trident, the crescent moon and the serpent — and riding his great bull.

Traditional interpretationSo she came to the battle in chapter 8: mounted on the bull, holding the trident, with great serpents for bracelets and the moon’s crescent as her ornament (8.16), and she slew asuras with her trident (8.34, 8.49). The hymn salutes the same figure as a form of Nārāyaṇī.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also has the name माहेश्वरी (208). The two texts are separate works.

11.15Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मयूरकुक्कुटवृते महाशक्तिधरेऽनघे।
कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ १५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मयूरकुक्कुटवृते | महाशक्तिधरे | अनघेकौमारीरूपसंस्थाने | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedmayūrakukkuṭavṛte mahāśaktidhare'naghe |
kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedmayūrakukkuṭavṛte | mahāśaktidhare | anaghe
kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मयूरकुक्कुटवृतेmayūrakukkuṭavṛteO you who are attended by peacocks and cocksf. voc. sg. of मयूरकुक्कुटवृता; compound (see Samāsa)
महाशक्तिधरेmahāśaktidhareO bearer of the great spearf. voc. sg. of महाशक्तिधरा; compound (see Samāsa)
अनघेanagheO sinless onef. voc. sg. of अनघा; compound (see Samāsa)
कौमारीरूपसंस्थानेkaumārīrūpasaṃsthāneO you whose shape is the form of Kaumārīf. voc. sg. of कौमारीरूपसंस्थाना; compound (see Samāsa)
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • महाशक्तिधरेऽनघे mahāśaktidhare'naghe → महाशक्तिधरे + अनघे
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a of अनघे is elided (avagraha).
  • संस्थाने saṃsthāne → सम् + स्थाने
    anusvāra sandhi with a prefix · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    The m of the prefix सम् becomes anusvāra before s.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • मयूरकुक्कुटवृते mayūrakukkuṭavṛte — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing an itaretara dvandva
    Members: मयूर + कुक्कुट + वृत · Vigraha: मयूराः च कुक्कुटाः च मयूरकुक्कुटाः; तैः वृता मयूरकुक्कुटवृता, तत्सम्बुद्धौ mayūrāḥ ca kukkuṭāḥ ca mayūrakukkuṭāḥ; taiḥ vṛtā mayūrakukkuṭavṛtā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “surrounded by peacocks and cocks”; in the verse: attended by the birds of Skanda: the peacock his mount, the cock his banner
  • महाशक्तिधरे mahāśaktidhare — upapada tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (महाशक्ति)
    Members: महाशक्ति + धर · Vigraha: महती चासौ शक्तिः च महाशक्तिः; तां धरति इति महाशक्तिधरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahatī cāsau śaktiḥ ca mahāśaktiḥ; tāṃ dharati iti mahāśaktidharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “bearer of the great śakti”; in the verse: you who hold the great spear (शक्ति), Skanda’s weapon
    Alternative: शक्ति means both ‘spear’ and ‘power’; ‘bearer of great power’ is possible, but Kaumārī’s weapon in the battle is the śakti-spear (8.17, शक्तिहस्ता), which fixes the first sense. The pun is natural in a hymn to the Śakti.
  • अनघे anaghe — bahuvrīhi (nañ-bahuvrīhi)
    Members: अन् (= न) + अघ · Vigraha: न विद्यते अघं यस्याः सा अनघा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ na vidyate aghaṃ yasyāḥ sā anaghā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she in whom there is no sin”; in the verse: O spotless, faultless one
  • कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (कौमारीरूप)
    Members: कौमारीरूप + संस्थान · Vigraha: कौमार्याः रूपम् कौमारीरूपम्; कौमारीरूपं संस्थानं यस्याः सा कौमारीरूपसंस्थाना, तत्सम्बुद्धौ kaumāryāḥ rūpam kaumārīrūpam; kaumārīrūpaṃ saṃsthānaṃ yasyāḥ sā kaumārīrūpasaṃsthānā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she whose configuration is the form of Kaumārī”; in the verse: you who appear as Kaumārī, the Śakti of Kumāra (Skanda)
    Alternative: संस्थान can also be ‘abiding’: ‘she whose abiding is in Kaumārī’s form’ (कौमारीरूपे संस्थानं यस्याः). The sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • मयूरकुक्कुटवृते mayūrakukkuṭavṛte — वृत is the past participle of √वृ (svādi, ‘to cover, surround’): ‘surrounded, attended’; feminine -वृता, vocative -वृते.
  • कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne — कौमारी is a taddhita from कुमार (with vṛddhi and feminine -ī): ‘belonging to Kumāra’, i.e. Skanda’s Śakti.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) मयूरकुक्कुटवृते महाशक्तिधरे अनघे कौमारीरूपसंस्थाने नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) mayūrakukkuṭavṛte mahāśaktidhare anaghe kaumārīrūpasaṃsthāne nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Four vocative epithets of Kaumārī, then the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO you who are attended by peacocks and cocks, bearer of the great spear, O sinless one, you whose shape is the form of Kaumārī, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableAttended by peacock and cock, holding the mighty spear, spotless one in the form of Kaumārī: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe third Mātṛkā: Kaumārī, the Śakti of Kumāra (Skanda), with his birds, the peacock and the cock, and his great spear.

Traditional interpretationIn the battle Kaumārī came spear in hand, riding a fine peacock, in the form of Guha (8.17), and slew asuras with her spear (8.34, 8.49; 9.38). The peacock is Skanda’s mount and the cock his banner in Purāṇic iconography.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: अनघा, ‘sinless’, stands in the middle of a warrior’s description. The force that fights here is pictured as clean of malice.

11.16Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे।
प्रसीद वैष्णवीरूपे नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ १६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधेप्रसीद | वैष्णवीरूपे | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedśaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe |
prasīda vaiṣṇavīrūpe nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedśaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe
prasīda | vaiṣṇavīrūpe | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधेśaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudheO you who have taken up the supreme weapons — conch, discus, mace and the Śārṅga bowf. voc. sg. of -परमायुधा; compound (see Samāsa)
प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P, present stem सीद-), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
वैष्णवीरूपेvaiṣṇavīrūpeO you who are in the form of Vaiṣṇavīf. voc. sg. of वैष्णवीरूपा; compound (see Samāsa)
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • परमायुधे paramāyudhe → परम + आयुधे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe — bahuvrīhi containing an itaretara dvandva (शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्ग) and a karmadhāraya (परमायुध)
    Members: शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्ग + गृहीत + परमायुध · Vigraha: शङ्खः च चक्रं च गदा च शार्ङ्गं च शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गाणि; परमाणि आयुधानि परमायुधानि; शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गाणि (एव) परमायुधानि गृहीतानि यया सा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ śaṅkhaḥ ca cakraṃ ca gadā ca śārṅgaṃ ca śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgāṇi; paramāṇi āyudhāni paramāyudhāni; śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgāṇi (eva) paramāyudhāni gṛhītāni yayā sā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she by whom the supreme weapons, conch, discus, mace and Śārṅga, have been taken up”; in the verse: you who carry Viṣṇu’s weapons
    Alternative: The participle गृहीत stands between the two groups. The compound may also be divided as शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीता (‘who has taken conch … Śārṅga’, with the participle placed second, as is allowed in such bahuvrīhis) and परमायुधा (‘whose weapons are supreme’); the sense is the same.
  • वैष्णवीरूपे vaiṣṇavīrūpe — bahuvrīhi (vyadhikaraṇa)
    Members: वैष्णवी + रूप · Vigraha: वैष्णव्याः रूपं यस्याः सा वैष्णवीरूपा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ vaiṣṇavyāḥ rūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā vaiṣṇavīrūpā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she whose form is (that of) Vaiṣṇavī”; in the verse: you who appear as Vaiṣṇavī, Viṣṇu’s Śakti

C. Morphology

  • शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe — गृहीत is the past participle of √ग्रह् (kryādi, ‘to seize’): the ra of the root becomes ṛ (samprasāraṇa) and the connecting vowel is long (गृह् + ई + त). शार्ङ्ग, Viṣṇu’s bow, is a taddhita from शृङ्ग ‘horn’: ‘made of horn’.
  • प्रसीद prasīda — As in 11.3: the only imperative in the refrain verses so far besides अस्तु, a direct prayer inside the salutation.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) शङ्खचक्रगदाशार्ङ्गगृहीतपरमायुधे वैष्णवीरूपे नारायणि, प्रसीद; ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) śaṅkhacakragadāśārṅgagṛhītaparamāyudhe vaiṣṇavīrūpe nārāyaṇi, prasīda; te namaḥ astu |

A compound vocative fills the first line; the second adds a prayer (प्रसीद) to the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO you who have taken up the supreme weapons — conch, discus, mace and Śārṅga — be gracious, O you in the form of Vaiṣṇavī, O Nārāyaṇī; homage be to you.

ReadableHolding the conch, the discus, the mace and the bow Śārṅga, those supreme weapons, in the form of Vaiṣṇavī — be gracious, O Nārāyaṇī; salutation to you.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe fourth Mātṛkā: Vaiṣṇavī, the Śakti of Viṣṇu, holding his four weapons — conch, discus, mace and the bow Śārṅga. In this verse alone of the Mātṛkā series the gods add a prayer, प्रसीद, ‘be gracious’.

Traditional interpretationIn the battle Vaiṣṇavī came seated on Garuḍa, with conch, discus, mace, Śārṅga bow and sword in her hands (8.18), and her discus struck and pierced Raktabīja (8.47–48). Here the name of the refrain and the name of the form coincide: Nārāyaṇī is greeted as Vaiṣṇavī, the power of Nārāyaṇa himself (compare 11.5, वैष्णवी शक्तिः).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the names वैष्णवी (892) and विष्णुरूपिणी (893). The two texts are separate works.

11.17Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरे।
वराहरूपिणि शिवे नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ १७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे | दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरेवराहरूपिणि | शिवे | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedgṛhītogramahācakre daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare |
varāharūpiṇi śive nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedgṛhītogramahācakre | daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare
varāharūpiṇi | śive | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रेgṛhītogramahācakreO you who have taken up the fierce great discusf. voc. sg. of गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रा; compound (see Samāsa)
दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरेdaṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhareO you who lifted up the earth on your tuskf. voc. sg. of दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरा; compound (see Samāsa)
वराहरूपिणिvarāharūpiṇiO you who have the form of a boarf. voc. sg. of वराहरूपिणी (वराहरूप + possessive -in, feminine -inī)
शिवेśiveO auspicious onef. voc. sg. of शिवा
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे gṛhītogramahācakre → गृहीत + उग्र + महाचक्रे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • दंष्ट्रोद्धृत daṃṣṭroddhṛta → दंष्ट्रा + उद्धृत
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa); h after a stop · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); झयो होऽन्यतरस्याम् (8.4.62)
    ā + u → o. Inside उद्धृत (उद् + हृत) the h after d becomes dh.
  • वसुंधरे vasuṃdhare → वसुम् + धरे
    anusvāra sandhi inside a compound · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    वसुंधरा takes the augment m after वसु (वसुम्-धरा); the m is written as anusvāra before dh.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे gṛhītogramahācakre — bahuvrīhi containing a karmadhāraya (उग्रमहाचक्र, itself containing the karmadhāraya महाचक्र)
    Members: गृहीत + उग्र + महाचक्र · Vigraha: उग्रं च तत् महाचक्रं च उग्रमहाचक्रम्; गृहीतम् उग्रमहाचक्रं यया सा गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ ugraṃ ca tat mahācakraṃ ca ugramahācakram; gṛhītam ugramahācakraṃ yayā sā gṛhītogramahācakrā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she by whom the fierce great discus has been taken up”; in the verse: you who wield the terrible great discus
  • दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरे daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare — bahuvrīhi (containing a tṛtīyā relation: दंष्ट्रया उद्धृता)
    Members: दंष्ट्रा + उद्धृत + वसुंधरा · Vigraha: दंष्ट्रया उद्धृता वसुंधरा यया सा दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ daṃṣṭrayā uddhṛtā vasuṃdharā yayā sā daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdharā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she by whom the earth was raised with a tusk”; in the verse: you who, as the Boar, lifted the earth from the waters on your tusk
  • वसुंधरे vasuṃdhare — upapada tatpuruṣa (with the augment m on the first member)
    Members: वसु + धरा · Vigraha: वसूनि धारयति इति वसुंधरा vasūni dhārayati iti vasuṃdharā
    Literally “bearer of wealth”; in the verse: the earth
  • वराहरूपिणि varāharūpiṇi — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (वराहरूप) with the possessive suffix -in
    Members: वराह + रूप (+ -इन्) · Vigraha: वराहस्य रूपम् वराहरूपम्; तत् अस्याः अस्ति इति वराहरूपिणी varāhasya rūpam varāharūpam; tat asyāḥ asti iti varāharūpiṇī
    Literally “she who has the boar’s form”; in the verse: Vārāhī, the Śakti of the Boar

C. Morphology

  • दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरे daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare — उद्धृत is the past participle of उद् + √हृ ‘to lift up’ (उद् + हृत, with the h aspirating the d: उद्धृत).
  • वराहरूपिणि varāharūpiṇi — रूपिन् ‘having the form of’ (रूप + -in, possessive), feminine रूपिणी, vocative -रूपिणि.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) गृहीतोग्रमहाचक्रे दंष्ट्रोद्धृतवसुंधरे वराहरूपिणि शिवे नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) gṛhītogramahācakre daṃṣṭroddhṛtavasuṃdhare varāharūpiṇi śive nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Four vocatives of Vārāhī, then the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO you who have taken up the fierce great discus, who raised the earth with your tusk, O you who have the form of a boar, O auspicious one, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableWielding the fierce great discus, raising the earth upon your tusk, auspicious one in the form of the Boar: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe fifth Mātṛkā: Vārāhī, the Śakti of the Boar. She is greeted with the Boar’s great deed: lifting the earth on its tusk.

Traditional interpretationIn the battle the Śakti of Hari, who bears the form of the sacrificial Boar, came in a boar’s body (8.19) and fought beside the others (8.49; 9.39, where her snout crushes asuras). The hymn gives her the Boar’s own deed, the raising of the earth from the waters, so that the deed of Viṣṇu’s avatāra is praised as hers.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also knows Vārāhī as a warrior power: name 76, विशुक्रप्राणहरणवाराहीवीर्यनन्दिता, ‘delighted by the valour of Vārāhī, who took Viśukra’s life’. The battles are different and the two texts are separate works.

11.18Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

नृसिंहरूपेणोग्रेण हन्तुं दैत्यान् कृतोद्यमे।
त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ १८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

नृसिंहरूपेण | उग्रेण | हन्तुम् | दैत्यान् | कृतोद्यमेत्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitednṛsiṃharūpeṇogreṇa hantuṃ daityān kṛtodyame |
trailokyatrāṇasahite nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatednṛsiṃharūpeṇa | ugreṇa | hantum | daityān | kṛtodyame
trailokyatrāṇasahite | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
नृसिंहरूपेणnṛsiṃharūpeṇain the form of the Man-Lionn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner
उग्रेणugreṇafierce, terriblen. instr. sg. of उग्र; qualifies नृसिंहरूपेण
हन्तुम्hantumto slayinfinitive (tumun) of √हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’)
दैत्यान्daityānthe Daityasm. acc. pl. of दैत्य; object of हन्तुम्
कृतोद्यमेkṛtodyameO you who set out with resolvef. voc. sg. of कृतोद्यमा; compound (see Samāsa)
त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहितेtrailokyatrāṇasahiteO you who are bent on protecting the three worldsf. voc. sg. of त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिता; compound (see Samāsa)
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥsalutation, homagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastube, let there be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, dat. sg. (enclitic)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नृसिंहरूपेणोग्रेण nṛsiṃharūpeṇogreṇa → नृसिंहरूपेण + उग्रेण
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • हन्तुं दैत्यान् hantuṃ daityān → हन्तुम् + दैत्यान्
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • कृतोद्यमे kṛtodyame → कृत + उद्यमे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.8.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • नृसिंहरूपेण nṛsiṃharūpeṇa — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (नृसिंह is itself a karmadhāraya: ना एव सिंहः, or नृषु सिंहः — ‘the man who is a lion’)
    Members: नृसिंह + रूप · Vigraha: नृसिंहस्य रूपम्, तेन nṛsiṃhasya rūpam, tena
    Literally “by the form of the Man-Lion”; in the verse: as Nārasiṃhī, the Śakti of Viṣṇu’s Man-Lion
  • कृतोद्यमे kṛtodyame — bahuvrīhi
    Members: कृत + उद्यम · Vigraha: कृतः उद्यमः यया सा कृतोद्यमा, तत्सम्बुद्धौ kṛtaḥ udyamaḥ yayā sā kṛtodyamā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she by whom effort has been made”; in the verse: you who have set yourself to the task
  • त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते trailokyatrāṇasahite — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: त्रैलोक्य + त्राण + सहित · Vigraha: त्रैलोक्यस्य त्राणम् त्रैलोक्यत्राणम्; तेन सहिता त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिता, तत्सम्बुद्धौ trailokyasya trāṇam trailokyatrāṇam; tena sahitā trailokyatrāṇasahitā, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “joined with the protection of the three worlds”; in the verse: you who are wholly engaged in saving the three worlds

C. Morphology

  • हन्तुम् hantum — Infinitive of purpose (√हन् + tum): ‘in order to slay’, depending on कृतोद्यमे.
  • त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते trailokyatrāṇasahite — त्रैलोक्य is a taddhita (with vṛddhi) from त्रिलोक ‘the three worlds’: ‘the triad of worlds’. सहित ‘accompanied by, joined with’ (सह + हित) here means ‘devoted to’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) उग्रेण नृसिंहरूपेण दैत्यान् हन्तुं कृतोद्यमे, त्रैलोक्यत्राणसहिते नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

(he) ugreṇa nṛsiṃharūpeṇa daityān hantuṃ kṛtodyame, trailokyatrāṇasahite nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

The first line is one vocative phrase built on कृतोद्यमे, with an instrumental of manner and an infinitive of purpose; the second adds a further vocative and the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO you who, in the fierce form of the Man-Lion, set out with resolve to slay the Daityas, O you who are bent on protecting the three worlds, O Nārāyaṇī, homage be to you.

ReadableIn the terrible form of the Man-Lion you rose to slay the demons, intent on saving the three worlds: O Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

The vocatives are turned into statements.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe sixth Mātṛkā: Nārasiṃhī, the Śakti of the Man-Lion, in her fierce form, set on destroying the Daityas to protect the three worlds.

Traditional interpretationIn the battle Nārasiṃhī came in a body like Nṛsiṃha’s, scattering the stars with the toss of her mane (8.20), and roamed the field tearing asuras with her claws (8.37). As in 11.17, the purpose of Viṣṇu’s avatāra — to protect the worlds from the Daityas — is praised as the Goddess’s own.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has a related idea in name 80, कराङ्गुलिनखोत्पन्ननारायणदशाकृतिः, ‘from whose fingernails arose the ten forms of Nārāyaṇa’: there too Viṣṇu’s avatāras, the Boar and the Man-Lion among them, come from the Goddess. The two texts are separate works.

11.19Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

किरीटिनि महावज्रे सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले।
वृत्रप्राणहरे चैन्द्रि नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ १९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

किरीटिनि | महावज्रे | सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वलेवृत्रप्राणहरे | च | ऐन्द्रि | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedkirīṭini mahāvajre sahasranayanojjvale |
vṛtraprāṇahare caindri nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedkirīṭini | mahāvajre | sahasranayanojjvale
vṛtraprāṇahare | ca | aindri | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
किरीटिनिkirīṭiniO crowned one, wearer of the diademf. voc. sg. of किरीटिनी (feminine of किरीटिन् ‘having a diadem’, possessive taddhita -इन् on किरीट)
महावज्रेmahāvajreO you of the great thunderboltf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वलेsahasranayanojjvaleO you who blaze with a thousand eyesf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
वृत्रप्राणहरेvṛtraprāṇahareO taker of Vṛtra’s lifef. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); adds the last epithet and the name ऐन्द्रि to the series
ऐन्द्रिaindriO Aindrī, power of Indraf. voc. sg. of ऐन्द्री (taddhita अण् on इन्द्र ‘belonging to Indra’, feminine in -ī)
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी (ī-stem, feminine of नारायण)
नमःnamaḥhomage, salutationn. nom. sg. of नमस् (s-stem); subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P ‘to be’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.; dative with नमः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले sahasranayanojjvale → सहस्रनयन + उज्ज्वले
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • चैन्द्रि caindri → च + ऐन्द्रि
    vowel sandhi (vṛddhi) · वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    a + ai → ai: the vowel of the particle disappears into the ai. Read च ऐन्द्रि, not a form of इन्द्र.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -aḥ before a: the visarga (as ru) becomes u, a + u gives o, and the initial a of अस्तु is absorbed into the o (avagraha). The same junction closes every stanza of the refrain (11.8–23).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महावज्रे mahāvajre — bahuvrīhi
    Members: महत् + वज्र · Vigraha: महत् वज्रं यस्याः सा (महावज्रा), तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahat vajraṃ yasyāḥ sā (mahāvajrā), tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she whose thunderbolt is great”; in the verse: Aindrī with Indra’s vajra in hand (8.21 वज्रहस्ता)
  • सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले sahasranayanojjvale — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a dvigu (सहस्रनयन: a numeral compound standing before a further member)
    Members: सहस्रनयन + उज्ज्वल · Vigraha: सहस्रं नयनानि सहस्रनयनानि; सहस्रनयनैः उज्ज्वला sahasraṃ nayanāni sahasranayanāni; sahasranayanaiḥ ujjvalā
    Literally “blazing with a thousand eyes”; in the verse: bearing Indra’s thousand eyes (8.21 सहस्रनयना)
    Alternative: Also a bahuvrīhi, सहस्रं नयनानि उज्ज्वलानि यस्याः सा ‘whose thousand eyes blaze’. The sense is the same; the tatpuruṣa keeps उज्ज्वला as the word that describes her.
  • वृत्रप्राणहरे vṛtraprāṇahare — upapada tatpuruṣa (हर = √हृ with the agent affix a after its object) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (वृत्रप्राण)
    Members: वृत्र + प्राण + हर · Vigraha: वृत्रस्य प्राणाः वृत्रप्राणाः; वृत्रप्राणान् हरति इति वृत्रप्राणहरा vṛtrasya prāṇāḥ vṛtraprāṇāḥ; vṛtraprāṇān harati iti vṛtraprāṇaharā
    Literally “she who takes away the life-breaths of Vṛtra”; in the verse: slayer of Vṛtra — Indra’s great deed, ascribed to his power

C. Morphology

  • किरीटिनि kirīṭini — किरीट ‘diadem’ + the possessive -इन् gives किरीटिन् ‘crowned’; the feminine adds -ī (किरीटिनी), and the vocative shortens it: किरीटिनि.
  • ऐन्द्रि aindri — Taddhita: इन्द्र + अ (अण्) with vṛddhi of the first vowel (इ → ऐ) gives ऐन्द्र ‘of Indra’; feminine ऐन्द्री, vocative ऐन्द्रि. वैष्णवी, माहेश्वरी and कौमारी in the stanzas before are formed the same way.
  • अस्तु astu — Imperative third singular of √अस्: with नमः ‘let there be homage’; the dative ते names the one honoured.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे किरीटिनि महावज्रे सहस्रनयनोज्ज्वले वृत्रप्राणहरे च ऐन्द्रि नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he kirīṭini mahāvajre sahasranayanojjvale vṛtraprāṇahare ca aindri nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

A chain of vocatives ending in the name ऐन्द्रि; च adds the last epithet. The refrain नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते is the main clause.

6Translation

LiteralO crowned one, O you of the great thunderbolt, O you who blaze with a thousand eyes, and O Aindrī, taker of Vṛtra’s life — O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

ReadableCrowned, wielding the great thunderbolt, ablaze with a thousand eyes, Aindrī who took the life of Vṛtra — Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe seventh of the Mātṛkā stanzas (11.13–21). The Goddess is praised as Aindrī, Indra’s power, as she came to the battlefield in 8.21 (वज्रहस्ता … गजराजोपरि स्थिता … सहस्रनयना यथा शक्रः) and struck Raktabīja with her thunderbolt (8.42, 8.46). Every attribute is Indra’s: the diadem, the vajra, the thousand eyes, the killing of Vṛtra.

Traditional interpretationIn 8.13–14 the powers of the gods leave their bodies with the same form, ornaments and mounts as the gods themselves. The hymn names each Mātṛkā by her god’s deed: Vārāhī lifts the earth on her tusk (11.17), Nārasiṃhī strikes the daityas (11.18), Aindrī slays Vṛtra. The Vedic victory of Indra is credited to the power through which he won it.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name वज्रिणी (name 944), ‘she who holds the thunderbolt’. The image is shared; the two texts are separate works.

11.20Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण हतदैत्यमहाबले।
घोररूपे महारावे नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ २० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण | हतदैत्यमहाबलेघोररूपे | महारावे | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedśivadūtīsvarūpeṇa hatadaityamahābale |
ghorarūpe mahārāve nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedśivadūtīsvarūpeṇa | hatadaityamahābale
ghorarūpe | mahārāve | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
शिवदूतीस्वरूपेणśivadūtīsvarūpeṇain the form of Śivadūtīn. instr. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); instrumental of manner, ‘in the form of’
हतदैत्यमहाबलेhatadaityamahābaleO you by whom the great army of the daityas was slainf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
घोररूपेghorarūpeO you of terrible formf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
महारावेmahārāveO you of the mighty roarf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.19.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa — karmadhāraya (the first member names the form)
    Members: शिवदूती + स्वरूप · Vigraha: शिवदूती एव स्वरूपम्, तेन śivadūtī eva svarūpam, tena
    Literally “by the own-form that is Śivadūtī”; in the verse: as Śivadūtī, the form that sent Śiva as messenger (8.24–28)
  • शिवदूती śivadūtī — bahuvrīhi (feminine in -ī)
    Members: शिव + दूत · Vigraha: शिवः दूतः यस्याः सा śivaḥ dūtaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose messenger is Śiva”; in the verse: the name given in 8.28: यतो नियुक्तो दौत्येन तया देव्या शिवः स्वयम्
  • हतदैत्यमहाबले hatadaityamahābale — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (दैत्यमहाबल) and a karmadhāraya (महाबल); महत् takes the form महा- before the noun it qualifies
    Members: हत + दैत्य + महाबल · Vigraha: महत् बलं महाबलम्; दैत्यानां महाबलं दैत्यमहाबलम्; हतं दैत्यमहाबलं यया सा mahat balaṃ mahābalam; daityānāṃ mahābalaṃ daityamahābalam; hataṃ daityamahābalaṃ yayā sā
    Literally “she by whom the great army of the daityas was slain”; in the verse: destroyer of the asura host (9.23, 9.37, 9.41)
    Alternative: बल can also mean ‘strength’: हताः दैत्याः यया सा च महाबला च, ‘slayer of the daityas, of great might’. The compound is printed as one word, and बल ‘army’ with हत ‘slain’ reads more naturally; both senses fit the narrative.
  • घोररूपे ghorarūpe — bahuvrīhi
    Members: घोर + रूप · Vigraha: घोरं रूपं यस्याः सा ghoraṃ rūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose form is terrible”; in the verse: fearsome to behold (8.23 अतिभीषणा … अत्युग्रा)
  • महारावे mahārāve — bahuvrīhi
    Members: महत् + राव · Vigraha: महान् रावः यस्याः सा mahān rāvaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose roar is great”; in the verse: the howl of a hundred jackals (8.23 शिवाशतनिनादिनी) and her laughter (9.23)

C. Morphology

  • शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa — Instrumental of manner among vocatives, as माहेश्वरीस्वरूपेण in 11.14: ‘(you who appear) in the form of Śivadūtī’.
  • हतदैत्यमहाबले hatadaityamahābale — हत is the past participle (kta) of √हन् (adādi 2P ‘to strike, kill’); as the first member of a bahuvrīhi it gives the passive sense ‘by whom … was slain’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे शिवदूतीस्वरूपेण (स्थिते) हतदैत्यमहाबले घोररूपे महारावे नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he śivadūtīsvarūpeṇa (sthite) hatadaityamahābale ghorarūpe mahārāve nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

The instrumental phrase describes the manner of her appearance; three bahuvrīhi vocatives follow; then the refrain.

6Translation

Literal(You who are) in the form of Śivadūtī, O you by whom the great army of the daityas was slain, O you of terrible form, O you of the mighty roar — O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

ReadableAppearing as Śivadūtī, destroyer of the daityas’ mighty host, terrible of form, loud of roar — Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

‘Appearing’ supplies the participle implied by the instrumental of form.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe eighth Mātṛkā stanza praises Śivadūtī, the fierce power that sprang from Caṇḍikā’s own body, howling like a hundred jackals (8.23). She sent Śiva as her envoy to Śumbha and Niśumbha (8.24–27) and so was named Śivadūtī, ‘she who has Śiva as messenger’ (8.28). Her laughter terrified the asuras (8.38, 9.23) and she devoured them (9.37, 9.41).

Traditional interpretationUnlike the six Mātṛkās before her, Śivadūtī is no god’s power but the Devī’s own: she comes from Caṇḍikā’s body, not from a god’s. The stanza thus moves from the gods’ śaktis back to the Goddess herself, completed by Cāmuṇḍā in 11.21.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name शिवदूती (name 405). The name and its sense are shared Śākta usage; the two texts are separate works.

11.21Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

दंष्ट्राकरालवदने शिरोमालाविभूषणे।
चामुण्डे मुण्डमथने नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ २१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

दंष्ट्राकरालवदने | शिरोमालाविभूषणेचामुण्डे | मुण्डमथने | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As reciteddaṃṣṭrākarālavadane śiromālāvibhūṣaṇe |
cāmuṇḍe muṇḍamathane nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separateddaṃṣṭrākarālavadane | śiromālāvibhūṣaṇe
cāmuṇḍe | muṇḍamathane | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
दंष्ट्राकरालवदनेdaṃṣṭrākarālavadaneO you whose face is dreadful with tusksf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
शिरोमालाविभूषणेśiromālāvibhūṣaṇeO you adorned with a garland of headsf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
चामुण्डेcāmuṇḍeO Cāmuṇḍāf. voc. sg. of चामुण्डा (proper name)
मुण्डमथनेmuṇḍamathaneO crusher of Muṇḍaf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • शिरोमाला śiromālā → शिरस् + माला
    visarga sandhi inside a compound · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    The final s of the stem शिरस् becomes ru, then u before the voiced m, and a + u gives o: शिरोमाला.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.19.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • दंष्ट्राकरालवदने daṃṣṭrākarālavadane — bahuvrīhi containing a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (दंष्ट्राकराल)
    Members: दंष्ट्राकराल + वदन · Vigraha: दंष्ट्राभिः करालम् दंष्ट्राकरालम्; दंष्ट्राकरालं वदनं यस्याः सा daṃṣṭrābhiḥ karālam daṃṣṭrākarālam; daṃṣṭrākarālaṃ vadanaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose face is frightful with tusks”; in the verse: Kālī’s dreadful, gaping mouth (7.6 करालवदना, 7.8 अतिविस्तारवदना)
  • शिरोमालाविभूषणे śiromālāvibhūṣaṇe — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (शिरोमाला)
    Members: शिरोमाला + विभूषण · Vigraha: शिरसां माला शिरोमाला; शिरोमाला विभूषणं यस्याः सा śirasāṃ mālā śiromālā; śiromālā vibhūṣaṇaṃ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose ornament is a garland of heads”; in the verse: wearing a garland of heads, as Kālī appears in 7.7 (नरमालाविभूषणा)
  • मुण्डमथने muṇḍamathane — upapada tatpuruṣa (मथन = √मथ् with the agent affix -अन)
    Members: मुण्ड + मथन · Vigraha: मुण्डं मथ्नाति इति मुण्डमथना muṇḍaṃ mathnāti iti muṇḍamathanā
    Literally “she who crushes Muṇḍa”; in the verse: slayer of the general Muṇḍa (7.21)
    Alternative: Also a bahuvrīhi: मुण्डस्य मथनं यया सा, ‘she by whom the crushing of Muṇḍa (was done)’; or with मुण्ड in its common-noun sense ‘shaven head’, ‘crusher of heads’. The proper name fits the pairing with चामुण्डे.

C. Morphology

  • चामुण्डे cāmuṇḍe — Vocative of चामुण्डा. The text gives the name’s origin in 7.27: because Kālī brought the heads of Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa, she would be known as Cāmuṇḍā (यस्माच्चण्डं च मुण्डं च … चामुण्डेति ततो लोके ख्याता देवि भविष्यसि). It is a name, not a regular compound.
  • मुण्डमथने muṇḍamathane — मथन is an agent noun from √मथ् / मन्थ् (‘to churn, crush’) in -अन; feminine मथना, vocative मथने.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे दंष्ट्राकरालवदने शिरोमालाविभूषणे मुण्डमथने चामुण्डे नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he daṃṣṭrākarālavadane śiromālāvibhūṣaṇe muṇḍamathane cāmuṇḍe nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Two bahuvrīhi vocatives describing her appearance, the name with its explanatory epithet, then the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO you whose face is dreadful with tusks, O you whose ornament is a garland of heads, O Cāmuṇḍā, O crusher of Muṇḍa — O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

ReadableFanged and fearful of face, garlanded with heads, Cāmuṇḍā, slayer of Muṇḍa — Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last Mātṛkā stanza praises Cāmuṇḍā, the Kālī who sprang from Ambikā’s brow (7.5–7), killed Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa (7.20–21) and received her name for it (7.27). In the Raktabīja battle she drank the demon’s blood so that no new asura could rise from it (8.53–61). The epithets are those of her first appearance: a gaping fanged mouth and a garland of heads.

Traditional interpretationWith Cāmuṇḍā the series of nine forms (Brahmāṇī to Cāmuṇḍā, 11.13–21) is complete. In the Śākta tradition these forms are worshipped as the Mātṛkās, the ‘Mothers’, with Cāmuṇḍā often counted among them; the hymn presents them all as forms of one Nārāyaṇī.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) names her चण्डमुण्डासुरनिषूदिनी (name 756), ‘destroyer of the asuras Caṇḍa and Muṇḍa’ — the same story in a different text.

11.22Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

लक्ष्मि लज्जे महाविद्ये श्रद्धे पुष्टिस्वधे ध्रुवे।
महारात्रि महाऽविद्ये नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ २२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

लक्ष्मि | लज्जे | महाविद्ये | श्रद्धे | पुष्टि | स्वधे | ध्रुवेमहारात्रि | महाऽविद्ये | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedlakṣmi lajje mahāvidye śraddhe puṣṭisvadhe dhruve |
mahārātri mahā'vidye nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedlakṣmi | lajje | mahāvidye | śraddhe | puṣṭi | svadhe | dhruve
mahārātri | mahā'vidye | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
लक्ष्मिlakṣmiO Lakṣmī, fortunef. voc. sg. of लक्ष्मी (ī-stem; nom. लक्ष्मीः)
लज्जेlajjeO modestyf. voc. sg. of लज्जा
महाविद्येmahāvidyeO great knowledgef. voc. sg. of महाविद्या; compound (see Samāsa)
श्रद्धेśraddheO faithf. voc. sg. of श्रद्धा
पुष्टिpuṣṭiO nourishment, increasef. voc. sg. of पुष्टि (i-stem); the regular vocative is पुष्टे — here the stem form, as भूति in 11.23 (see flags)
स्वधेsvadheO Svadhā, the offering to the ancestorsf. voc. sg. of स्वधा
ध्रुवेdhruveO steadfast onef. voc. sg. of ध्रुवा (feminine of the adjective ध्रुव ‘fixed, constant’)
महारात्रिmahārātriO great nightf. voc. sg. of महारात्री (ī-stem by-form of रात्रि); compound (see Samāsa)
महाऽविद्येmahā'vidyeO great nesciencef. voc. sg. of the compound महा + अविद्या (written महाऽविद्या); compound (see Samāsa)
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • महाऽविद्ये mahā'vidye → महा + अविद्ये
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. The Gita Press writes an avagraha to show that an a has merged into the ā: mahā + avidyā, not mahāvidyā. The sound is the same either way (see the variant).
  • पुष्टिस्वधे puṣṭisvadhe → पुष्टि + स्वधे
    no sandhi (vowel before consonant) · —
    The two vocatives are printed without a space; nothing changes at the junction.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.19.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महाविद्ये mahāvidye — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + विद्या · Vigraha: महती विद्या (महाविद्या), तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahatī vidyā (mahāvidyā), tatsambuddhau
    Literally “great knowledge”; in the verse: the supreme knowledge that frees (cf. 1.57 सा विद्या परमा मुक्तेर्हेतुभूता)
  • महारात्रि mahārātri — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + रात्री · Vigraha: महती रात्री (महारात्री), तत्सम्बुद्धौ mahatī rātrī (mahārātrī), tatsambuddhau
    Literally “great night”; in the verse: the night of dissolution, as in Brahmā’s hymn (1.79 कालरात्रिर्महारात्रिर्मोहरात्रिश्च दारुणा)
  • महाऽविद्ये mahā'vidye — karmadhāraya containing a nañ tatpuruṣa (अविद्या)
    Members: महत् + अविद्या · Vigraha: महती अविद्या (महाविद्या), तत्सम्बुद्धौ; अविद्या = न विद्या mahatī avidyā (mahāvidyā), tatsambuddhau; avidyā = na vidyā
    Literally “great nescience”; in the verse: the ignorance that binds, also her power (cf. 1.58 संसारबन्धहेतुश्च सैव)
    Alternative: Without the avagraha (GRETIL, Freund) the word is महाविद्ये again, ‘great knowledge’, repeating the first line. The Gita Press reading pairs it with महारात्रि and balances महाविद्ये in the first line.

C. Morphology

  • लक्ष्मि lakṣmi — लक्ष्मी is an ī-stem of the type that keeps visarga in the nominative (लक्ष्मीः); the vocative shortens the ī: लक्ष्मि. Contrast the ā-stems of the line, whose vocative ends in -e (लज्जे, श्रद्धे, स्वधे).
  • पुष्टि puṣṭi — The i-stem vocative should be पुष्टे (guṇa of the final). The printed पुष्टि (written together with स्वधे) keeps the stem form, as भूति does in 11.23; Purāṇic verse allows such vocatives. The metre is unaffected.
  • महारात्रि mahārātri — रात्रि has a by-form रात्री (ī-stem); the vocative of महारात्री is महारात्रि. (Of the i-stem, used in 1.79, it would be महारात्रे.)

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे लक्ष्मि लज्जे महाविद्ये श्रद्धे पुष्टि स्वधे ध्रुवे महारात्रि महाऽविद्ये नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he lakṣmi lajje mahāvidye śraddhe puṣṭi svadhe dhruve mahārātri mahā'vidye nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Nine names in the vocative, each a power the Goddess is; then the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO Lakṣmī, O Modesty, O Great Knowledge, O Faith, O Nourishment, O Svadhā, O Steadfast One, O Great Night, O Great Nescience — O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

ReadableFortune, Modesty, Great Knowledge, Faith, Nourishment, Svadhā, the Steadfast; Great Night and Great Ignorance — Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningAfter the nine warrior forms the hymn turns to abstract powers. Most of these names were already given her by Brahmā (1.73 स्वधा, 1.77 महाविद्या, 1.79 महारात्रिः, 1.80 लज्जा पुष्टिः) and by the gods (4.5 श्रीः … श्रद्धा … लज्जा; 5.44–58 लज्जा, श्रद्धा, लक्ष्मी).

Direct textual meaningThe line ends on a pair of opposites: महाविद्या ‘great knowledge’ at the start of the first line, महाऽविद्या ‘great nescience’ at the end of the second. The Gita Press marks the second with an avagraha; other witnesses (GRETIL, Freund) read महाविद्ये twice. Both readings are traditional (corrections.json, variant).

Traditional interpretationThe Gita Press reading states a teaching the text gives from the start: the same Mahāmāyā is the knowledge that liberates and the power that binds (1.57–58 सा विद्या परमा मुक्तेर्हेतुभूता … संसारबन्धहेतुश्च सैव). Knowledge and ignorance are not two goddesses.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has महाविद्या (name 584) and विद्याऽविद्यास्वरूपिणी (name 402), ‘whose form is both knowledge and ignorance’, and also लज्जा (740), पुष्टिः (444) and स्वधा (536). The pairing of knowledge and nescience is a shared Śākta idea; the texts are separate works.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Variant (recorded): महाऽविद्ये (Gita Press, kept) against महाविद्ये (GRETIL, Freund) — two readings, ‘great nescience’ or ‘great knowledge’; both traditional. The digital text prints पुष्टिस्वधे as one word; it is taken here as two vocatives (पुष्टि, a stem-form vocative for पुष्टे, and स्वधे), since a dvandva of two names would not stand in the vocative singular. If the joining is deliberate, the alternative is an irregular compound vocative of ‘Puṣṭi-Svadhā’.

11.23Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

मेधे सरस्वति वरे भूति बाभ्रवि तामसि।
नियते त्वं प्रसीदेशे नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ २३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

मेधे | सरस्वति | वरे | भूति | बाभ्रवि | तामसिनियते | त्वम् | प्रसीद | ईशे | नारायणि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedmedhe sarasvati vare bhūti bābhravi tāmasi |
niyate tvaṃ prasīdeśe nārāyaṇi namo'stu te ||

Separatedmedhe | sarasvati | vare | bhūti | bābhravi | tāmasi
niyate | tvam | prasīda | īśe | nārāyaṇi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
मेधेmedheO Medhā, intelligencef. voc. sg. of मेधा
सरस्वतिsarasvatiO Sarasvatīf. voc. sg. of सरस्वती (ī-stem)
वरेvareO excellent onef. voc. sg. of वरा (feminine of the adjective वर ‘best, choice’)
भूतिbhūtiO prosperity, powerf. voc. sg. of भूति (i-stem); the regular vocative is भूते — here the stem form (see Morph)
बाभ्रविbābhraviO Bābhravī, the tawny onef. voc. sg. of बाभ्रवी (taddhita on बभ्रु ‘tawny’, feminine in -ī)
तामसिtāmasiO Tāmasī, you of the nature of tamasf. voc. sg. of तामसी (taddhita अण् on तमस्, feminine in -ī)
नियतेniyateO Niyati, ordaining powerf. voc. sg. of नियति (i-stem, regular vocative); see Morph for the alternative
त्वम्tvamyousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of प्रसीद
प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to sit, settle’; present stem सीद-), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
ईशेīśeO sovereign Ladyf. voc. sg. of ईशा (feminine of ईश ‘ruler’)
नारायणिnārāyaṇiO Nārāyaṇīf. voc. sg. of नारायणी
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रसीदेशे prasīdeśe → प्रसीद + ईशे
    vowel sandhi (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e. The imperative and the vocative are written as one.
  • त्वं प्रसीदेशे tvaṃ prasīdeśe → त्वम् + प्रसीद
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before the consonant p becomes anusvāra.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.19.

C. Morphology

  • भूति bhūti — The i-stem vocative would be भूते. The text has भूति, a stem-form vocative found in Purāṇic verse (compare पुष्टि in 11.22). The syllable count is the same either way.
  • नियते niyate — Taken as the regular vocative of नियति (नि + √यम्, kṛt -ति) ‘restraint, fixed order, destiny’, which fits a list of powers (मेधा, भूति). It can also be the vocative of नियता (kta of नि + √यम्) ‘self-restrained one’; the form is the same.
  • प्रसीद prasīda — Imperative of प्र + √सद्: the root takes the present stem सीद- (सद् → सीद), and with प्र means ‘be pleased, be gracious’.
  • तामसि tāmasi — Taddhita: तमस् + अ with vṛddhi (त → ता) gives तामस ‘belonging to tamas’; feminine तामसी, vocative तामसि.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे मेधे सरस्वति वरे भूति बाभ्रवि तामसि नियते ईशे, त्वं प्रसीद। (हे) नारायणि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he medhe sarasvati vare bhūti bābhravi tāmasi niyate īśe, tvaṃ prasīda | (he) nārāyaṇi, te namaḥ astu |

Seven vocatives, a prayer (त्वं प्रसीद) with an eighth vocative (ईशे) set inside it, then the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralO Medhā, O Sarasvatī, O excellent one, O Prosperity, O Bābhravī, O Tāmasī, O Niyati — you, be gracious, O sovereign Lady; O Nārāyaṇī, let homage be to you.

ReadableIntelligence, Sarasvatī, most excellent, Prosperity, tawny Bābhravī, dark Tāmasī, Ordaining Power — be gracious, sovereign Lady. Nārāyaṇī, salutation to you.

बाभ्रवि and तामसि are rendered with their literal sense (‘tawny’, ‘dark’) as well as the name.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe list of powers continues: intellect and speech (मेधा, सरस्वती), prosperity (भूति), and two names of her darker nature (बाभ्रवी, तामसी), closing with Niyati, the power that fixes the order of things. In the middle of the list the gods break into a direct prayer: त्वं प्रसीद, ‘be gracious’.

Traditional interpretationMedhā and Sarasvatī recall the gods’ hymn of chapter 4 (4.11 मेधासि देवि विदिताखिलशास्त्रसारा). Tāmasī is the Goddess as tamas, the power of sleep and dissolution: Brahmā’s hymn calls her the bearer of the three guṇas (1.78 गुणत्रयविभाविनी), and in the first carita she is Viṣṇu’s yoganidrā, his sleep (1.66, 1.69–71).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) also has मेधा (name 538) and सरस्वती (name 704). Shared names; separate works.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: भूति (and पुष्टि in 11.22) are stem-form vocatives, not the regular भूते; kept as printed. नियते may be read as Niyati ‘destiny’ (chosen) or नियता ‘the self-restrained’. बाभ्रवी: lexically ‘tawny’ (from बभ्रु) or ‘belonging to Babhru’; the specific mythological reference is not stated in the text.

11.24Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सर्वस्वरूपे सर्वेशे सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते।
भयेभ्यस्त्राहि नो देवि दुर्गे देवि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ २४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सर्वस्वरूपे | सर्वेशे | सर्वशक्तिसमन्वितेभयेभ्यः | त्राहि | नः | देवि | दुर्गे | देवि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedsarvasvarūpe sarveśe sarvaśaktisamanvite |
bhayebhyastrāhi no devi durge devi namo'stu te ||

Separatedsarvasvarūpe | sarveśe | sarvaśaktisamanvite
bhayebhyaḥ | trāhi | naḥ | devi | durge | devi | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सर्वस्वरूपेsarvasvarūpeO you whose own form is everythingf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
सर्वेशेsarveśeO Lady of allf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
सर्वशक्तिसमन्वितेsarvaśaktisamanviteO you endowed with every powerf. voc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
भयेभ्यःbhayebhyaḥfrom fears, from dangersn. abl. pl. of भय; ablative of what one is protected from
त्राहिtrāhiprotect, save√त्रा (adādi, 2P ‘to protect’; by-form of √त्रै, bhvādi 1Ā), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
नःnaḥusfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, enclitic acc. pl.; object of त्राहि
देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी
दुर्गेdurgeO Durgāf. voc. sg. of दुर्गा
देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी; repeated with the name
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वेशे sarveśe → सर्व + ईशे
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e.
  • भयेभ्यस्त्राहि bhayebhyastrāhi → भयेभ्यः + त्राहि
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • नो देवि no devi → नः + देवि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.19.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वस्वरूपे sarvasvarūpe — bahuvrīhi
    Members: सर्व + स्वरूप · Vigraha: सर्वं स्वरूपं यस्याः सा (सर्वस्वरूपा), तत्सम्बुद्धौ sarvaṃ svarūpaṃ yasyāḥ sā (sarvasvarūpā), tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she whose own form is all”; in the verse: the Goddess who is everything that exists (cf. 1.82 सदसद्वाखिलात्मिके)
    Alternative: As a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (सर्वस्य स्वरूपम्, ‘the true nature of all’) the compound would be neuter, with the vocative सर्वस्वरूप; the feminine vocative -e shows the bahuvrīhi.
  • सर्वेशे sarveśe — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: सर्व + ईशा · Vigraha: सर्वस्य ईशा sarvasya īśā
    Literally “ruler of all”; in the verse: sovereign over everything
  • सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते sarvaśaktisamanvite — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a karmadhāraya (सर्वशक्ति)
    Members: सर्वशक्ति + समन्वित · Vigraha: सर्वाः शक्तयः सर्वशक्तयः; सर्वशक्तिभिः समन्विता sarvāḥ śaktayaḥ sarvaśaktayaḥ; sarvaśaktibhiḥ samanvitā
    Literally “endowed with all powers”; in the verse: possessing every power — the gods’ powers gathered in her (2.10–19, 8.12–21)

C. Morphology

  • त्राहि trāhi — Imperative second singular with the ending -हि, as in पाहि and याहि: √त्रा is the athematic by-form of the root that also appears as त्रायते, त्रायस्व.
  • भयेभ्यः bhayebhyaḥ — Ablative plural: with verbs of protecting, the danger one is protected from stands in the ablative.
  • सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते sarvaśaktisamanvite — समन्वित is the past participle (kta) of सम् + अनु + √इ (adādi 2P ‘to go’): ‘gone along with’, hence ‘accompanied by, endowed with’, taking the instrumental.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे सर्वस्वरूपे सर्वेशे सर्वशक्तिसमन्विते देवि, नः भयेभ्यः त्राहि। हे दुर्गे देवि, ते नमः अस्तु।

he sarvasvarūpe sarveśe sarvaśaktisamanvite devi, naḥ bhayebhyaḥ trāhi | he durge devi, te namaḥ astu |

Three bahuvrīhi and tatpuruṣa vocatives, then a prayer (त्राहि नः) and a new refrain addressed to Durgā.

6Translation

LiteralO you whose form is all, O Lady of all, O you endowed with every power — protect us from fears, O Devī; O Durgā, O Devī, let homage be to you.

ReadableForm of all things, Lady of all, possessed of every power — protect us from every fear, Devī. Durgā, Devī, salutation to you.

‘every fear’ renders the plural भयेभ्यः.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe refrain changes. After sixteen stanzas ending नारायणि नमोऽस्तु ते (11.8–23), the gods address her as Durgā and ask for protection from fear. From here to 11.28 each stanza is a prayer for protection, as at the end of the gods’ hymn in chapter 4 (4.24–27 शूलेन पाहि नो देवि …).

Traditional interpretationThe name Durgā is explained later in this chapter: the Devī foretells that she will slay the asura Durgama and be known as Durgā Devī (11.49–50). Traditionally this stanza is among the most widely recited of the Saptaśatī, used on its own as a prayer for freedom from fear.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has दुर्गा (name 190), सर्वेश्वरी (name 202), सर्वशक्तिमयी (name 199) and भयापहा (name 121), ‘remover of fear’. Shared names and ideas; the works are separate.

11.25Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

एतत्ते वदनं सौम्यं लोचनत्रयभूषितम्।
पातु नः सर्वभीतिभ्यः कात्यायनि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ २५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एतत् | ते | वदनम् | सौम्यम् | लोचनत्रयभूषितम्पातु | नः | सर्वभीतिभ्यः | कात्यायनि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedetatte vadanaṃ saumyaṃ locanatrayabhūṣitam |
pātu naḥ sarvabhītibhyaḥ kātyāyani namo'stu te ||

Separatedetat | te | vadanam | saumyam | locanatrayabhūṣitam
pātu | naḥ | sarvabhītibhyaḥ | kātyāyani | namaḥ | astu | te

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. nom. sg.; qualifies वदनम्
तेteyoursecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, enclitic gen. sg.
वदनम्vadanamfacen. nom. sg. of वदन; subject of पातु
सौम्यम्saumyamgentle, benignn. nom. sg. of सौम्य (taddhita from सोम ‘moon’: ‘moon-like, mild’); qualifies वदनम्
लोचनत्रयभूषितम्locanatrayabhūṣitamadorned with three eyesn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies वदनम्
पातुpātumay (it) protect√पा (adādi, 2P ‘to protect’), loṭ (imperative), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
नःnaḥusअस्मद्, enclitic acc. pl.; object of पातु
सर्वभीतिभ्यःsarvabhītibhyaḥfrom all fearsf. abl. pl.; compound (see Samāsa)
कात्यायनिkātyāyaniO Kātyāyanīf. voc. sg. of कात्यायनी (patronymic feminine, ‘of the Kātyāyana line’)
नमःnamaḥhomagen. nom. sg. of नमस्; subject of अस्तु
अस्तुastulet (it) be√अस् (adādi, 2P), loṭ, prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एतत्ते etatte → एतत् + ते
    no change · खरि च (8.4.55)
    Final t before the unvoiced t stays t; the two words are only written together.
  • वदनं सौम्यं लोचन vadanaṃ saumyaṃ locana → वदनम् + सौम्यम् + लोचन…
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra.
  • नः सर्वभीतिभ्यः naḥ sarvabhītibhyaḥ → नः + सर्वभीतिभ्यः
    visarga before a sibilant · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before s the visarga is kept (the optional alternative to assimilation).
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.19.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • लोचनत्रयभूषितम् locanatrayabhūṣitam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (लोचनत्रय)
    Members: लोचनत्रय + भूषित · Vigraha: लोचनानां त्रयम् लोचनत्रयम्; लोचनत्रयेण भूषितम् locanānāṃ trayam locanatrayam; locanatrayeṇa bhūṣitam
    Literally “adorned with a triad of eyes”; in the verse: the three-eyed face of the Goddess
  • सर्वभीतिभ्यः sarvabhītibhyaḥ — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + भीति · Vigraha: सर्वाः भीतयः, ताभ्यः sarvāḥ bhītayaḥ, tābhyaḥ
    Literally “from all fears”; in the verse: from every danger

C. Morphology

  • पातु pātu — Imperative third singular of √पा ‘to protect’ (पाति, पातु): the face, not the Goddess, is its subject — the start of a series in which her face, trident, bell and sword are asked to protect.
  • भूषितम् bhūṣitam — Not a separate word: भूषित, the past participle (kta) of √भूष् (curādi 10 ‘to adorn’), is the last member of लोचनत्रयभूषितम् and takes the gender of वदनम्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे कात्यायनि, एतत् लोचनत्रयभूषितं सौम्यं ते वदनं नः सर्वभीतिभ्यः पातु। ते नमः अस्तु।

he kātyāyani, etat locanatrayabhūṣitaṃ saumyaṃ te vadanaṃ naḥ sarvabhītibhyaḥ pātu | te namaḥ astu |

The subject (वदनम् with three qualifiers) and the imperative पातु; then the refrain to Kātyāyanī.

6Translation

LiteralMay this gentle face of yours, adorned with three eyes, protect us from all fears; O Kātyāyanī, let homage be to you.

ReadableMay this gentle face of yours, graced with three eyes, protect us from every fear. Kātyāyanī, salutation to you.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first of four stanzas (11.25–28) in which the Goddess’s own attributes — her face, trident, bell and sword — are asked to protect. The gods began this hymn by praising her as Kātyāyanī (11.2 कात्यायनीं तुष्टुवुः), and the asuras sought her under that name (8.29 यत्र कात्यायनी स्थिता).

Traditional interpretationThe gentle face is asked first, the fierce weapons after. The gods of chapter 4 prayed in the same order of thought: ‘protect us with your gentle forms and with your terrible ones’ (4.26 सौम्यानि यानि रूपाणि … यानि चात्यर्थघोराणि तै रक्षास्मान्).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has कात्यायनी (name 556) and त्र्यम्बका (name 762), ‘the three-eyed’. Shared names; separate works.

11.26Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ज्वालाकरालमत्युग्रमशेषासुरसूदनम्।
त्रिशूलं पातु नो भीतेर्भद्रकालि नमोऽस्तु ते ॥ २६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ज्वालाकरालम् | अत्युग्रम् | अशेषासुरसूदनम्त्रिशूलम् | पातु | नः | भीतेः | भद्रकालि | नमः | अस्तु | ते

3IAST

As recitedjvālākarālamatyugramaśeṣāsurasūdanam |
triśūlaṃ pātu no bhīterbhadrakāli namo'stu te ||

Separatedjvālākarālam | atyugram | aśeṣāsurasūdanam
triśūlam | pātu | naḥ | bhīteḥ | bhadrakāli | namaḥ | astu | te

तेteto youयुष्मद्, enclitic dat. sg.

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ज्वालाकरालमत्युग्रमशेषासुरसूदनम् jvālākarālamatyugramaśeṣāsurasūdanam → ज्वालाकरालम् + अत्युग्रम् + अशेषासुरसूदनम्
    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    Both final m’s stand before vowels and are written together with them; the whole first line is one written unit.
  • अत्युग्रम् atyugram → अति + उग्रम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    i before the unlike vowel u becomes y.
  • अशेषासुर aśeṣāsura → अशेष + असुर
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā.
  • नो भीतेर्भद्रकालि no bhīterbhadrakāli → नः + भीतेः + भद्रकालि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114)
    नः before the voiced bh becomes नो. In भीतेः the visarga follows e, not a or ā, so its ru stays as r before the voiced bh: भीतेर्भद्रकालि.
  • नमोऽस्तु namo'stu → नमः + अस्तु
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    As in 11.19.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • ज्वालाकरालम् jvālākarālam — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: ज्वाला + कराल · Vigraha: ज्वालाभिः करालम् jvālābhiḥ karālam
    Literally “dreadful with flames”; in the verse: a trident blazing with fire
  • अत्युग्रम् atyugram — prādi tatpuruṣa (the prefix अति as first member)
    Members: अति + उग्र · Vigraha: अतिशयेन उग्रम् atiśayena ugram
    Literally “beyond fierce”; in the verse: exceedingly fierce
  • अशेषासुरसूदनम् aśeṣāsurasūdanam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa with the agent noun सूदन, containing a karmadhāraya (अशेषासुर); अशेष is itself a nañ bahuvrīhi (न विद्यते शेषः यस्य)
    Members: अशेष + असुर + सूदन · Vigraha: अशेषाः असुराः अशेषासुराः; अशेषासुराणां सूदनम् aśeṣāḥ asurāḥ aśeṣāsurāḥ; aśeṣāsurāṇāṃ sūdanam
    Literally “destroyer of the asuras without remainder”; in the verse: the trident that slew every asura
  • त्रिशूलम् triśūlam — bahuvrīhi (used as a noun)
    Members: त्रि + शूल · Vigraha: त्रीणि शूलानि यस्य तत् trīṇi śūlāni yasya tat
    Literally “that which has three points”; in the verse: the trident, weapon of Māheśvarī (11.14) and of the Devī (4.24)
  • भद्रकालि bhadrakāli — karmadhāraya
    Members: भद्रा + काली · Vigraha: भद्रा चासौ काली च (भद्रकाली), तत्सम्बुद्धौ bhadrā cāsau kālī ca (bhadrakālī), tatsambuddhau
    Literally “auspicious Kālī”; in the verse: the dark, fierce Goddess who is kindly to her devotees
    Alternative: Also read ‘Kālī who brings good (भद्रम्)’; the compound’s form is the same. In a karmadhāraya the feminine first member takes its masculine form (भद्र-, not भद्रा-).

C. Morphology

  • भीतेः bhīteḥ — Ablative singular of the i-stem भीति ‘fear’ (गुण of the final + -ः); the same form serves as the genitive. Here it is ‘from fear’, with पातु.
  • अशेषासुरसूदनम् aśeṣāsurasūdanam — सूदन is an agent noun in -अन from √सूद् (‘to destroy’), as in मधुसूदन; neuter here to agree with त्रिशूलम्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे भद्रकालि, ज्वालाकरालम् अत्युग्रम् अशेषासुरसूदनं (ते) त्रिशूलं नः भीतेः पातु। ते नमः अस्तु।

he bhadrakāli, jvālākarālam atyugram aśeṣāsurasūdanaṃ (te) triśūlaṃ naḥ bhīteḥ pātu | te namaḥ astu |

The first line is three qualifiers of त्रिशूलम्, the subject of पातु; then the refrain to Bhadrakālī.

6Translation

LiteralMay the trident — dreadful with flames, exceedingly fierce, destroyer of the asuras without remainder — protect us from fear; O Bhadrakālī, let homage be to you.

ReadableMay your trident, fearsome with flames, fiercer than fierce, slayer of every asura, protect us from fear. Bhadrakālī, salutation to you.

‘your’ is supplied from the context of the series (ते in 11.25 and 11.28).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe trident is asked to protect. It is Māheśvarī’s weapon (11.14 त्रिशूलचन्द्राहिधरे; 8.49 माहेश्वरी त्रिशूलेन) and the first weapon named in the gods’ prayer of chapter 4 (4.24 शूलेन पाहि नो देवि). The name Bhadrakālī closed the account of the second carita’s hymn (4.39 तथेत्युक्त्वा भद्रकाली बभूवान्तर्हिता नृप).

Traditional interpretationThe name joins two opposites: भद्र, ‘auspicious’, and काली, ‘the dark one’. The weapon that destroyed every asura is the protection of those who take refuge in her; the fierce form is fearsome only to what is hostile.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the prayer is not ‘protect us from enemies’ but ‘protect us from fear’ (भीतेः). The danger named is the inner state as much as the outer threat.

11.27Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

हिनस्ति दैत्यतेजांसि स्वनेनापूर्य या जगत्।
सा घण्टा पातु नो देवि पापेभ्योऽनः सुतानिव ॥ २७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

हिनस्ति | दैत्यतेजांसि | स्वनेन | आपूर्य | या | जगत्सा | घण्टा | पातु | नः | देवि | पापेभ्यः | नः | सुतान् | इव

3IAST

As recitedhinasti daityatejāṃsi svanenāpūrya yā jagat |
sā ghaṇṭā pātu no devi pāpebhyo'naḥ sutāniva ||

Separatedhinasti | daityatejāṃsi | svanena | āpūrya | yā | jagat
sā | ghaṇṭā | pātu | naḥ | devi | pāpebhyaḥ | naḥ | sutān | iva

हिनस्तिhinastidestroys, strikes down√हिंस् (rudhādi, 7P ‘to injure, destroy’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
दैत्यतेजांसिdaityatejāṃsithe fiery strength of the daityasn. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of हिनस्ति
स्वनेनsvanenawith its soundm. instr. sg. of स्वन ‘sound’; instrument of आपूर्य
आपूर्यāpūryahaving filledआ + √पूर् (curādi, 10 ‘to fill’), absolutive (ल्यप्)
याyāwhichrelative pronoun यद्, f. nom. sg.; subject of हिनस्ति; anticipates घण्टा
जगत्jagatthe worldn. acc. sg. of जगत्; object of आपूर्य
साsāthatpronoun तद्, f. nom. sg.; correlative of या; qualifies घण्टा
घण्टाghaṇṭābellf. nom. sg. of घण्टा; subject of पातु

देविdeviO Devīf. voc. sg. of देवी

नःnaḥourअस्मद्, enclitic gen. pl.; with पापेभ्यः (see Morph for the alternative)
सुतान्sutānchildren, sonsm. acc. pl. of सुत; standard of comparison with इव
इवivaas, likeindeclinable (particle of comparison)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • स्वनेनापूर्य svanenāpūrya → स्वनेन + आपूर्य
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • नो देवि no devi → नः + देवि
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o. The digital text writes देवि and पापेभ्यो together without a space; nothing changes there (i before p).
  • पापेभ्योऽनः pāpebhyo'naḥ → पापेभ्यः + नः
    visarga sandhi (with an avagraha in print) · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced n becomes -o: पापेभ्यो नः. The text prints an avagraha (पापेभ्योऽनः) as though an a had been elided after the o, but no word अनः fits the sentence, and the metre counts eight syllables either way (the avagraha is silent). It is read as पापेभ्यो नः (see flags).
  • सुतानिव sutāniva → सुतान् + इव
    final n before a vowel · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32) does not apply
    The n is doubled only after a short vowel; after the long ā of सुतान् it simply joins the next vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • दैत्यतेजांसि daityatejāṃsi — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: दैत्य + तेजस् · Vigraha: दैत्यानां तेजांसि daityānāṃ tejāṃsi
    Literally “the energies of the daityas”; in the verse: the fiery might of the demon hosts

C. Morphology

  • हिनस्ति hinasti — √हिंस् is a rudhādi (seventh-class) root: the strong present inserts न before the final consonant, and the root’s own nasal falls away: हि-न-स्-ति (pl. हिंसन्ति).
  • आपूर्य āpūrya — Absolutive of the prefixed causative-type stem आ-पूरय-: with a prefix the absolutive takes -य (ल्यप्) and the stem’s -अय- is dropped: आपूर्य ‘having filled’.
  • दैत्यतेजांसि daityatejāṃsi — Neuter s-stem plural: तेजस् → तेजांसि, with lengthened vowel and the nasal before s (as मनांसि, यशांसि).
  • नः naḥ — The enclitic नः occurs twice. The first is the object of पातु (‘protect us’); the second is taken as genitive with पापेभ्यः (‘from our sins’). It may also be the object repeated for emphasis; the sense hardly changes.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे देवि, या (घण्टा) स्वनेन जगत् आपूर्य दैत्यतेजांसि हिनस्ति, सा घण्टा नः पापेभ्यः नः पातु, सुतान् इव।

he devi, yā (ghaṇṭā) svanena jagat āpūrya daityatejāṃsi hinasti, sā ghaṇṭā naḥ pāpebhyaḥ naḥ pātu, sutān iva |

A relative clause (या … हिनस्ति, with the absolutive phrase स्वनेन जगत् आपूर्य) describes the bell; the main clause is सा घण्टा … पातु; सुतान् इव is a comparison.

6Translation

LiteralWhich, having filled the world with its sound, destroys the fiery strength of the daityas — may that bell, O Devī, protect us from our sins, as children.

ReadableYour bell, whose sound fills the world and breaks the daityas’ strength — may that bell, Devī, guard us from our sins, as children are guarded.

‘as children are guarded’ renders सुतान् इव; the Sanskrit does not name who guards them. ‘Your’ is supplied from the series.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe bell is asked to protect. In the battle the Devī’s bell swelled the lion’s roar (8.9 घण्टास्वनेन तन्नादमम्बिका चोपबृंहयत्), and in the fight with Śumbha she filled the quarters with its sound, ‘which destroyed the strength of all the daitya armies’ (9.20 निजघण्टास्वनेन … समस्तदैत्यसैन्यानां तेजोवधविधायिना). The stanza recalls that line almost word for word (हिनस्ति दैत्यतेजांसि).

Traditional interpretationThe sound that undid the demons’ strength is now asked to guard against sins: the outer enemy of the narrative becomes the inner enemy of the worshipper. The gods asked the same in chapter 4 (4.24 घण्टास्वनेन नः पाहि). A bell is rung in the rite of worship, and the hymn gives that sound a protective sense.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: ‘as children’ makes the protection parental, not military — the same power that terrifies the hostile is tender toward its own.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: The Gita Press prints पापेभ्योऽनः with an avagraha (also in its appendix); grammar has पापेभ्यः + नः, and no letter is elided. The avagraha is kept as printed and recorded as an orthographic variant (corrections.json); the witnesses read पापेभ्यो नः. The missing space after देवि in the digital text has been corrected.

11.28Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितस्ते करोज्ज्वलः।
शुभाय खड्गो भवतु चण्डिके त्वां नता वयम् ॥ २८ ॥

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • असुरासृग्वसा asurāsṛgvasā → असुर + असृक् + वसा
    vowel and consonant sandhi inside a compound · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    a + a → ā (असुरासृक्); the final k of असृक् becomes g before the voiced v.
  • चर्चितस्ते carcitaste → चर्चितः + ते
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • करोज्ज्वलः karojjvalaḥ → कर + उज्ज्वलः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.
  • खड्गो भवतु khaḍgo bhavatu → खड्गः + भवतु
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced bh becomes -o.
  • त्वां नता वयम् tvāṃ natā vayam → त्वाम् + नताः + वयम्
    anusvāra and visarga sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    Final m before n becomes anusvāra. -āḥ before the voiced v: the visarga (as ru) becomes y, which is dropped before a consonant: नता वयम्.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितः asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣas (असुरासृग्वसापङ्क, असृग्वसापङ्क) and an itaretara dvandva (असृग्वसा)
    Members: असुर + असृक् + वसा + पङ्क + चर्चित · Vigraha: असृक् च वसा च असृग्वसे; असृग्वसयोः पङ्कः असृग्वसापङ्कः; असुराणाम् असृग्वसापङ्कः असुरासृग्वसापङ्कः; तेन चर्चितः asṛk ca vasā ca asṛgvase; asṛgvasayoḥ paṅkaḥ asṛgvasāpaṅkaḥ; asurāṇām asṛgvasāpaṅkaḥ asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkaḥ; tena carcitaḥ
    Literally “smeared with the mud of the blood and fat of the asuras”; in the verse: the sword still wet from the battle
    Alternative: असृग्वसापङ्क may also be a karmadhāraya of metaphor (असृग्वसे एव पङ्कः, ‘the mire that is blood and fat’); the meaning is the same.
  • करोज्ज्वलः karojjvalaḥ — saptamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: कर + उज्ज्वल · Vigraha: करे उज्ज्वलः kare ujjvalaḥ
    Literally “shining in the hand”; in the verse: flashing in the Goddess’s hand
    Alternative: Also a tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa, करेण उज्ज्वलः, ‘bright through (being held in) the hand’; the saptamī is simpler.

C. Morphology

  • नताः natāḥ — Past participle of √नम् in the active sense (an intransitive root of motion and posture): ‘(we are) bowed’, with the accusative त्वाम् of the one bowed to. The copula अस्मः is understood.
  • शुभाय śubhāya — Dative of purpose: ‘for good, to (our) welfare’ — what the sword is to serve.
  • भवतु bhavatu — Imperative third singular of √भू, the thematic present भव- + -तु: ‘may it be’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे चण्डिके, असुरासृग्वसापङ्कचर्चितः करोज्ज्वलः ते खड्गः (नः) शुभाय भवतु। वयं त्वां नताः (स्मः)।

he caṇḍike, asurāsṛgvasāpaṅkacarcitaḥ karojjvalaḥ te khaḍgaḥ (naḥ) śubhāya bhavatu | vayaṃ tvāṃ natāḥ (smaḥ) |

A benediction (खड्गः … शुभाय भवतु) with two qualifiers of the sword, and a closing statement (वयं त्वां नताः) in place of the refrain.

6Translation

LiteralSmeared with the mire of the asuras’ blood and fat, gleaming in your hand — may your sword be for (our) good, O Caṇḍikā; we are bowed to you.

ReadableYour sword, flashing in your hand and smeared with the blood and fat of the asuras — may it be for our good, Caṇḍikā. We bow to you.

‘our’ is supplied: शुभाय names no one.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last of the protection stanzas asks the sword to be ‘for good’. It is the weapon with which the Devī cut down Muṇḍa (7.21) and beheaded Niśumbha (9.33, 9.36), and which the gods named among her weapons in 4.27 (खड्गशूलगदादीनि … तैरस्मान् रक्ष सर्वतः). The refrain becomes a plain statement: ‘we are bowed to you’.

Traditional interpretationA sword still wet with blood is asked for auspiciousness. The gods of chapter 4 already saw the paradox: her weapons purify even the enemies they strike, who go to the higher worlds (4.18–19 लोकान् प्रयान्तु रिपवोऽपि हि शस्त्रपूता). The violence is directed at what threatens the world.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has चण्डिका (name 755). The name is shared; the works are separate.

11.29Ślokaindravajrā

1Original śloka

रोगानशेषानपहंसि तुष्टा
रुष्टा तु कामान् सकलानभीष्टान्।
त्वामाश्रितानां न विपन्नराणां
त्वामाश्रिता ह्याश्रयतां प्रयान्ति ॥ २९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

रोगान् | अशेषान् | अपहंसि | तुष्टारुष्टा | तु | कामान् | सकलान् | अभीष्टान्त्वाम् | आश्रितानाम् | न | विपत् | नराणाम्त्वाम् | आश्रिताः | हि | आश्रयताम् | प्रयान्ति

3IAST

As recitedrogānaśeṣānapahaṃsi tuṣṭā
ruṣṭā tu kāmān sakalānabhīṣṭān |
tvāmāśritānāṃ na vipannarāṇāṃ
tvāmāśritā hyāśrayatāṃ prayānti ||

Separatedrogān | aśeṣān | apahaṃsi | tuṣṭā
ruṣṭā | tu | kāmān | sakalān | abhīṣṭān
tvām | āśritānām | na | vipat | narāṇām
tvām | āśritāḥ | hi | āśrayatām | prayānti

4Word by word

अशेषान्aśeṣānall, without remainderm. acc. pl. of अशेष; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रोगान्

नnano, notindeclinable (negation)
