नराणाम्narāṇāmfor men, of peoplem. gen. pl. of नर; genitive of the one affected
त्वाम्tvāmyouयुष्मद्, acc. sg.; object of आश्रिताः
आश्रिताःāśritāḥthose who have taken refuge (in)past participle (kta) of आ + √श्रि, m. nom. pl.; subject of प्रयान्ति
हिhiindeedindeclinable (emphatic particle)
आश्रयताम्āśrayatāmthe state of being a refugef. acc. sg. of आश्रयता (abstract noun: आश्रय + taddhita -ता); object of प्रयान्ति
प्रयान्तिprayāntiattain, go toप्र + √या (adādi, 2P ‘to go’), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • रोगानशेषानपहंसि rogānaśeṣānapahaṃsi → रोगान् + अशेषान् + अपहंसि
    final n before a vowel · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32) does not apply
    After the long ā the final n is not doubled; it simply joins the next vowel. The same in सकलानभीष्टान्.
  • विपन्नराणां vipannarāṇāṃ → विपत् + नराणाम्
    consonant sandhi (nasal assimilation) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39), यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45)
    Final t (d) becomes the nasal n before the nasal n: विपन्नराणाम्. Not the participle विपन्न: read न विपत् नराणाम्.
  • त्वामाश्रिता ह्याश्रयतां tvāmāśritā hyāśrayatāṃ → त्वाम् + आश्रिताः + हि + आश्रयताम्
    visarga and vowel sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17); इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final m joins the vowel; -āḥ before the voiced h loses its visarga (ru → y, dropped before a consonant); the i of हि becomes y before ā: ह्याश्रयताम्.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अशेषान् aśeṣān — nañ bahuvrīhi
    Members: अ (नञ्) + शेष · Vigraha: न विद्यते शेषः येषां ते, तान् na vidyate śeṣaḥ yeṣāṃ te, tān
    Literally “those of which no remainder exists”; in the verse: all, every one

C. Morphology

  • अपहंसि apahaṃsi — Present second singular of अप + √हन्: हन् + सि → हंसि, the n becoming anusvāra before s inside the word. With अप ‘away’: ‘you strike away, remove’. It governs both lines: रोगान् … अपहंसि, and (अपहंसि) कामान्.
  • तुष्टा tuṣṭā — Past participles of intransitive roots used in the active sense, with the force of a conditional: तुष्टा ‘(you,) when pleased’, रुष्टा ‘when angry’.
  • आश्रयताम् āśrayatām — Abstract noun in -ता on आश्रय ‘refuge’: ‘refuge-hood’. With प्रयान्ति: ‘they attain the state of being a refuge’, i.e. they become a shelter for others.
  • विपत् vipat — विपद् is a root noun (वि + √पद् ‘to fall apart’) with no visible affix; its final d is pronounced t at a pause, so the padaccheda gives विपत्.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे देवि, त्वं) तुष्टा (सती) अशेषान् रोगान् अपहंसि, रुष्टा तु सकलान् अभीष्टान् कामान् (अपहंसि)। त्वाम् आश्रितानां नराणां विपत् न (भवति)। त्वाम् आश्रिताः हि आश्रयतां प्रयान्ति।

(he devi, tvaṃ) tuṣṭā (satī) aśeṣān rogān apahaṃsi, ruṣṭā tu sakalān abhīṣṭān kāmān (apahaṃsi) | tvām āśritānāṃ narāṇāṃ vipat na (bhavati) | tvām āśritāḥ hi āśrayatāṃ prayānti |

Two balanced clauses with one verb (अपहंसि), conditioned by तुष्टा and रुष्टा; then two statements about those who take refuge in her.

6Translation

LiteralPleased, you strike away diseases without remainder; but angered, all longed-for desires. For men who have taken refuge in you there is no calamity; those who have taken refuge in you indeed attain the state of being a refuge.

ReadablePleased, you take away every illness; angered, every cherished hope. No misfortune befalls those who take refuge in you; those who take refuge in you become a refuge themselves.

‘become a refuge’ renders आश्रयतां प्रयान्ति ‘attain refuge-hood’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn returns to the long metre of its opening stanzas (11.2–6; here indravajrā, eleven syllables a line). The first half sets out her power over fortune: her pleasure removes disease, her anger removes everything one longs for. The second half states the reward of refuge: no calamity, and more — the one who takes refuge becomes a refuge.

Traditional interpretationTraditionally this stanza is recited on its own as a prayer for freedom from illness. The last line is read as the fruit of devotion: the devotee’s protection reaches beyond himself to those who in turn depend on him.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) calls her भवरोगघ्नी (name 842), ‘destroyer of the disease of worldly existence’, and रोगपर्वतदम्भोलिः (name 748), ‘a thunderbolt to the mountain of disease’. The idea is shared; the works are separate.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: refuge is not only received but passed on. Those who are held become able to hold others.

11.30Ślokaindravajrā

1Original śloka

एतत्कृतं यत्कदनं त्वयाद्य
धर्मद्विषां देवि महासुराणाम्।
रूपैरनेकैर्बहुधाऽऽत्ममूर्तिं
कृत्वाम्बिके तत्प्रकरोति कान्या ॥ ३० ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

एतत् | कृतम् | यत् | कदनम् | त्वया | अद्यधर्मद्विषाम् | देवि | महासुराणाम्रूपैः | अनेकैः | बहुधा | आत्ममूर्तिम्कृत्वा | अम्बिके | तत् | प्रकरोति | का | अन्या

3IAST

As recitedetatkṛtaṃ yatkadanaṃ tvayādya
dharmadviṣāṃ devi mahāsurāṇām |
rūpairanekairbahudhā''tmamūrtiṃ
kṛtvāmbike tatprakaroti kānyā ||

Separatedetat | kṛtam | yat | kadanam | tvayā | adya
dharmadviṣām | devi | mahāsurāṇām
rūpaiḥ | anekaiḥ | bahudhā | ātmamūrtim
kṛtvā | ambike | tat | prakaroti | kā | anyā

यत्yatwhichrelative pronoun यद्, n. nom. sg.; agrees with कदनम्
कदनम्kadanamslaughter, carnagen. nom. sg. of कदन
त्वयाtvayāby youयुष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent of the passive कृतम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • एतत्कृतं यत्कदनं etatkṛtaṃ yatkadanaṃ → एतत् + कृतम् + यत् + कदनम्
    no change; anusvāra · खरि च (8.4.55); मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final t before the unvoiced k stays t; the words are only written together. Final m becomes anusvāra before a consonant.
  • त्वयाद्य tvayādya → त्वया + अद्य
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. The same in कृत्वाम्बिके and कान्या.
  • रूपैरनेकैर्बहुधा rūpairanekairbahudhā → रूपैः + अनेकैः + बहुधा
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    After ai (not a or ā) the visarga, as ru, stays r before a vowel and before a voiced consonant.
  • बहुधाऽऽत्ममूर्तिं bahudhā''tmamūrtiṃ → बहुधा + आत्ममूर्तिम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + ā → ā. The Gita Press writes a double avagraha (ऽऽ) to show that the long ā of आत्म- has merged into the ā of बहुधा; it is silent.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • धर्मद्विषाम् dharmadviṣām — upapada tatpuruṣa (द्विष् = √द्विष् with the zero affix क्विप्)
    Members: धर्म + द्विष् · Vigraha: धर्मं द्विषन्ति इति धर्मद्विषः, तेषाम् dharmaṃ dviṣanti iti dharmadviṣaḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of those who hate dharma”; in the verse: the enemies of the right order
  • महासुराणाम् mahāsurāṇām — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान्तः असुराः, तेषाम् mahāntaḥ asurāḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of the great asuras”; in the verse: Śumbha, Niśumbha and their generals
  • अनेकैः anekaiḥ — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अ (नञ्) + एक · Vigraha: न एकानि अनेकानि, तैः na ekāni anekāni, taiḥ
    Literally “not one”; in the verse: many
  • आत्ममूर्तिम् ātmamūrtim — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: आत्मन् + मूर्ति · Vigraha: आत्मनः मूर्तिः, ताम् ātmanaḥ mūrtiḥ, tām
    Literally “the body of (your) self”; in the verse: your own form

C. Morphology

  • कृतम् kṛtam — Passive construction with the past participle: एतत् यत् कदनं त्वया कृतम् ‘this slaughter which was done by you’; the agent stands in the instrumental.
  • प्रकरोति prakaroti — Present third singular of प्र + √कृ (tanādi: करो-ति, strong stem करो-). In a rhetorical question: ‘who else does that?’ — no one.
  • बहुधा bahudhā — Adverb of manner from बहु with the taddhita -धा (‘in … ways’), as द्विधा ‘in two’, सहस्रधा ‘in a thousand ways’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे देवि अम्बिके, अनेकैः रूपैः आत्ममूर्तिं बहुधा कृत्वा त्वया अद्य धर्मद्विषां महासुराणां यत् एतत् कदनं कृतम्, तत् का अन्या प्रकरोति?

he devi ambike, anekaiḥ rūpaiḥ ātmamūrtiṃ bahudhā kṛtvā tvayā adya dharmadviṣāṃ mahāsurāṇāṃ yat etat kadanaṃ kṛtam, tat kā anyā prakaroti?

A relative clause in the passive (यत् … कदनं त्वया कृतम्), with the absolutive phrase (रूपैः … कृत्वा) giving the means; the main clause is a rhetorical question resuming it with तत्.

6Translation

LiteralThis slaughter of the great asuras, haters of dharma, which was wrought by you today, O Devī, having made your own body manifold through many forms — O Ambikā, what other (woman) does that?

ReadableToday, Devī, you made your own form manifold in many shapes and wrought this slaughter of the great asuras who hated dharma. Ambikā, who else could do such a thing?

The passive is turned active; the rhetorical present प्रकरोति is rendered ‘could do’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods recall the battle of chapters 8–10: the Devī appeared as many — the Mātṛkās, Kālī, Śivadūtī — and destroyed the asuras. No other being could have done it.

Traditional interpretationThe stanza answers Śumbha’s taunt that she fought ‘relying on the strength of others’ (10.3 अन्यासां बलमाश्रित्य). The Devī had replied that the many forms were her own powers and drew them back into herself: एकैवाहं जगत्यत्र द्वितीया का ममापरा (10.5), ‘I alone am here in the world; who else is there besides me?’, and ‘I stood here in many forms by my power’ (10.8 अहं विभूत्या बहुभिरिह रूपैर्यदास्थिता). The gods’ question का अन्या echoes her own द्वितीया का.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: plurality here is not division. The many forms are one power acting in many ways.

11.31Ślokaupajāti (triṣṭubh)

1Original śloka

विद्यासु शास्त्रेषु विवेकदीपे-
ष्वाद्येषु वाक्येषु च का त्वदन्या।
ममत्वगर्तेऽतिमहान्धकारे
विभ्रामयत्येतदतीव विश्वम् ॥ ३१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

विद्यासु | शास्त्रेषु | विवेकदीपेषुआद्येषु | वाक्येषु | च | का | त्वदन्याममत्वगर्ते | अतिमहान्धकारेविभ्रामयति | एतत् | अतीव | विश्वम्

3IAST

As recitedvidyāsu śāstreṣu vivekadīpe-
ṣvādyeṣu vākyeṣu ca kā tvadanyā |
mamatvagarte'timahāndhakāre
vibhrāmayatyetadatīva viśvam ||

Separatedvidyāsu | śāstreṣu | vivekadīpeṣu
ādyeṣu | vākyeṣu | ca | kā | tvadanyā
mamatvagarte | atimahāndhakāre
vibhrāmayati | etat | atīva | viśvam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
विद्यासुvidyāsuin the branches of knowledgef. loc. pl. of विद्या
शास्त्रेषुśāstreṣuin the treatises, the śāstrasn. loc. pl. of शास्त्र
विवेकदीपेषुvivekadīpeṣuin the lamps of discernmentm. loc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to आद्येषु वाक्येषु (see Samāsa, alt)
आद्येषुādyeṣuprimal, firstn. loc. pl. of आद्य (‘being at the beginning’); qualifies वाक्येषु
वाक्येषुvākyeṣuin the utterances, the sayingsn. loc. pl. of वाक्य
चcaandindeclinable (conjunctive particle); closes the series of locatives
काkāwhointerrogative pronoun किम्, f. nom. sg.
त्वदन्याtvadanyāother than youf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); with का
ममत्वगर्तेmamatvagartein the pit of ‘mine-ness’, of possessivenessm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
अतिमहान्धकारेatimahāndhakāre(the pit) of exceedingly great darknessm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ममत्वगर्ते
विभ्रामयतिvibhrāmayatimakes (it) whirl, sends wanderingवि + √भ्रम् (‘to wander, whirl’), causative (णिच्), laṭ (present), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
एतत्etatthisdemonstrative pronoun एतद्, n. acc. sg.; qualifies विश्वम्
अतीवatīvaexceedingly, utterlyindeclinable (adverb)
विश्वम्viśvamthe universe, the whole worldn. acc. sg. of विश्व; object of विभ्रामयति

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विवेकदीपेष्वाद्येषु vivekadīpeṣvādyeṣu → विवेकदीपेषु + आद्येषु
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) across the printed line · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    u before the unlike vowel ā becomes v. The printed line breaks inside the word (विवेकदीपे- / ष्वाद्येषु); the word belongs to the first line.
  • त्वदन्या tvadanyā → त्वत् + अन्या
    consonant sandhi inside a compound (voicing) · झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    The t of the pronoun stem त्वत् becomes d before a vowel.
  • ममत्वगर्तेऽतिमहान्धकारे mamatvagarte'timahāndhakāre → ममत्वगर्ते + अतिमहान्धकारे
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa); savarṇa-dīrgha inside the compound · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    -e + a: the a is absorbed (avagraha). Inside the compound, महा + अन्धकार → महान्धकार.
  • विभ्रामयत्येतदतीव vibhrāmayatyetadatīva → विभ्रामयति + एतत् + अतीव
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) and consonant sandhi (voicing) · इको यणचि (6.1.77); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39)
    i before e becomes y; the final t of एतत् becomes d before the vowel of अतीव.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विवेकदीपेषु vivekadīpeṣu — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विवेक + दीप · Vigraha: विवेकस्य दीपाः, तेषु vivekasya dīpāḥ, teṣu
    Literally “in the lamps of discernment”; in the verse: in what illuminates the power to distinguish the real from the unreal
    Alternative: Also a karmadhāraya of metaphor (विवेकः एव दीपः, ‘discernment, which is a lamp’). It may stand as a third item of the list (‘in the lamps of discernment’) or in apposition to आद्येषु वाक्येषु (‘in the primal sayings, which are lamps of discernment’). The second is taken here; the first is equally grammatical.
  • त्वदन्या tvadanyā — pañcamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: त्वद् + अन्या · Vigraha: त्वत् अन्या tvat anyā
    Literally “other than you”; in the verse: anyone besides you (अन्य takes the ablative)
  • ममत्वगर्ते mamatvagarte — karmadhāraya (rūpaka: the pit that is possessiveness)
    Members: ममत्व + गर्त · Vigraha: ममत्वम् एव गर्तः, तस्मिन् mamatvam eva gartaḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in the pit which is ‘mine-ness’”; in the verse: in the abyss of attachment to ‘me and mine’
    Alternative: Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, ममत्वस्य गर्तः ‘the pit of possessiveness’; the sense is the same.
  • अतिमहान्धकारे atimahāndhakāre — bahuvrīhi (qualifying ममत्वगर्ते); अतिमहत् is a prādi tatpuruṣa (अतिशयेन महत्)
    Members: अतिमहत् + अन्धकार · Vigraha: अतिमहान् अन्धकारः यस्मिन् सः, तस्मिन् atimahān andhakāraḥ yasmin saḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in which there is exceedingly great darkness”; in the verse: pitch-dark
    Alternative: It may also be a karmadhāraya (अतिमहान् अन्धकारः) in a second locative, ‘in the very great darkness’, parallel to ममत्वगर्ते.

C. Morphology

  • विभ्रामयति vibhrāmayati — Causative of √भ्रम् ‘to wander, whirl’: the root vowel takes vṛddhi before -अय- (भ्रम् → भ्रामय-), ‘makes wander’; with वि ‘astray, about’. Its subject is का त्वदन्या, third person — so the question of the second line runs on into the second half: ‘who but you makes this world whirl?’.
  • ममत्वगर्ते mamatvagarte — ममत्व is an abstract noun in -त्व formed on मम, the genitive ‘of me, mine’: ‘mine-ness’, possessiveness. Abstracts formed on a case-form are rare and expressive.
  • अतीव atīva — अति + इव fused (अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः, 6.1.101) into one adverb, ‘exceedingly’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

विद्यासु शास्त्रेषु विवेकदीपेषु आद्येषु वाक्येषु च त्वदन्या का (वर्तते)? अतिमहान्धकारे ममत्वगर्ते एतत् विश्वम् अतीव (त्वदन्या का) विभ्रामयति?

vidyāsu śāstreṣu vivekadīpeṣu ādyeṣu vākyeṣu ca tvadanyā kā (vartate)? atimahāndhakāre mamatvagarte etat viśvam atīva (tvadanyā kā) vibhrāmayati?

One rhetorical question (का त्वदन्या) governs both halves: in the first it is the subject of an understood ‘is’ with five locatives; in the second it is the subject of विभ्रामयति.

6Translation

LiteralIn the branches of knowledge, in the śāstras, in the primal sayings that are lamps of discernment — who is there other than you? (Who other than you) makes this universe whirl exceedingly in the pit of possessiveness, of very great darkness?

ReadableIn all knowledge and every scripture, in the ancient words that are lamps of discernment, who is there but you? And who but you sets this whole world whirling in the dark abyss of ‘mine’?

The question का त्वदन्या is carried into the second half, as the third-person verb requires.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningTwo sides of one power. The Goddess is present in every source of knowledge and discernment; and it is she who sends the world whirling in the darkness of possessiveness. The same question, ‘who but you?’, covers both.

Traditional interpretationThe teaching is Medhas’s in the first chapter: creatures are thrown ‘into the whirlpool of mine-ness, the pit of delusion’ (1.53 ममतावर्त्ते मोहगर्ते निपातिताः) by the power of Mahāmāyā, who drags even the minds of the wise into delusion (1.55–56) and is also the supreme knowledge that frees (1.57). The ‘primal sayings’ are commonly understood as the Vedas. The question का त्वदन्या was asked in the gods’ hymn of chapter 4 as well (4.17).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) holds both sides in two names: विश्वभ्रमणकारिणी (name 889), ‘she who makes the universe whirl’, and ममताहन्त्री (name 165), ‘destroyer of possessiveness’. The texts are separate; the paired idea is shared.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the pit is ‘mine’. What darkens the world in this stanza is not ignorance of facts but the habit of possession.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: Interpretive: विवेकदीपेषु may be a separate locative of the list or stand in apposition to आद्येषु वाक्येषु; अतिमहान्धकारे may be a bahuvrīhi qualifying ममत्वगर्ते or a parallel locative. The sense hardly changes.

11.32Ślokaindravajrā

1Original śloka

रक्षांसि यत्रोग्रविषाश्च नागा
यत्रारयो दस्युबलानि यत्र।
दावानलो यत्र तथाब्धिमध्ये
तत्र स्थिता त्वं परिपासि विश्वम् ॥ ३२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

रक्षांसि | यत्र | उग्रविषाः | च | नागाःयत्र | अरयः | दस्युबलानि | यत्रदावानलः | यत्र | तथा | अब्धिमध्येतत्र | स्थिता | त्वम् | परिपासि | विश्वम्

3IAST

As recitedrakṣāṃsi yatrograviṣāśca nāgā
yatrārayo dasyubalāni yatra |
dāvānalo yatra tathābdhimadhye
tatra sthitā tvaṃ paripāsi viśvam ||

Separatedrakṣāṃsi | yatra | ugraviṣāḥ | ca | nāgāḥ
yatra | arayaḥ | dasyubalāni | yatra
dāvānalaḥ | yatra | tathā | abdhimadhye
tatra | sthitā | tvam | paripāsi | viśvam

रक्षांसिrakṣāṃsirākṣasas, demonsn. nom. pl. of रक्षस् (s-stem); अस्ति/सन्ति understood

तत्रtatrathereindeclinable (correlative adverb of place)

त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of परिपासि
परिपासिparipāsiyou protectपरि + √पा (adādi, 2P ‘to protect’), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
विश्वम्viśvamthe worldn. acc. sg. of विश्व; object of परिपासि

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • यत्रोग्रविषाश्च yatrograviṣāśca → यत्र + उग्रविषाः + च
    vowel sandhi (guṇa); visarga sandhi · आद्गुणः (6.1.87); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    a + u → o. The visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś: -श्च.
  • नागा यत्रारयो nāgā yatrārayo → नागाः + यत्र + अरयः
    visarga and vowel sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); हशि च (6.1.114)
    -āḥ before the voiced y loses its visarga (across the printed line); a + a → ā (यत्रारयः); -aḥ before the voiced d becomes -o (अरयो दस्यु…).
  • दावानलो यत्र dāvānalo yatra → दावानलः + यत्र
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced y becomes -o.
  • तथाब्धिमध्ये tathābdhimadhye → तथा + अब्धिमध्ये
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • उग्रविषाः ugraviṣāḥ — bahuvrīhi
    Members: उग्र + विष · Vigraha: उग्रं विषं येषां ते ugraṃ viṣaṃ yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “those whose venom is fierce”; in the verse: deadly serpents
  • दस्युबलानि dasyubalāni — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: दस्यु + बल · Vigraha: दस्यूनां बलानि dasyūnāṃ balāni
    Literally “forces of robbers”; in the verse: robber bands
  • दावानलः dāvānalaḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: दाव + अनल · Vigraha: दावस्य अनलः dāvasya analaḥ
    Literally “the fire of the forest”; in the verse: a forest fire
    Alternative: दाव itself can mean ‘forest fire’; then a karmadhāraya, दावः एव अनलः. The ṣaṣṭhī reading, दाव = ‘forest’, is the usual one.
  • अब्धिमध्ये abdhimadhye — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अब्धि ‘holder of waters’ is itself an upapada compound of अप् and धि)
    Members: अब्धि + मध्य · Vigraha: अब्धेः मध्यम्, तस्मिन् abdheḥ madhyam, tasmin
    Literally “in the middle of the ocean”; in the verse: at sea, far from any help

C. Morphology

  • रक्षांसि rakṣāṃsi — Neuter s-stem plural: रक्षस् → रक्षांसि, as तेजांसि in 11.27.
  • अरयः arayaḥ — Nominative plural of the i-stem अरि with guṇa of the stem vowel: अरि → अरय् + अः.
  • परिपासि paripāsi — Present second singular of परि + √पा ‘to protect’ (adādi: पा-सि); परि ‘all around’ strengthens the sense: ‘you guard on every side’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

यत्र रक्षांसि, यत्र उग्रविषाः नागाः च, यत्र अरयः, यत्र दस्युबलानि, यत्र दावानलः, तथा अब्धिमध्ये, तत्र स्थिता त्वं विश्वं परिपासि।

yatra rakṣāṃsi, yatra ugraviṣāḥ nāgāḥ ca, yatra arayaḥ, yatra dasyubalāni, yatra dāvānalaḥ, tathā abdhimadhye, tatra sthitā tvaṃ viśvaṃ paripāsi |

A series of relative clauses of place (यत्र …) with the verb ‘to be’ understood, closed by the correlative तत्र and the main clause.

6Translation

LiteralWhere there are rākṣasas, where serpents of fierce venom, where enemies, where bands of robbers, where a forest fire, and likewise in the midst of the ocean — standing there, you protect the world.

ReadableWhere demons are, and serpents of deadly venom, where enemies and robber bands, where the forest is ablaze, and out on the open sea — there you stand and guard the world.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningA catalogue of the dangers of the wild and of travel: demons, snakes, enemies, robbers, fire, the sea. Wherever danger is, the Goddess is there (तत्र स्थिता) and protects. The repeated यत्र, four times in three lines, builds the list.

Traditional interpretationThe Devī makes the matching promise in chapter 12: one who remembers her story is freed from distress when surrounded by forest fire, beset by robbers or held by enemies, or tossed by the wind ‘in a ship on the great ocean’ (12.25–29 दावाग्निपरिवारितः … दस्युभिर्वा वृतः … स्थितः पोते महार्णवे).

Modern reflectionA modern reading: the hymn does not say she removes every danger from the world, but that she stands within it. Protection is presence.

11.33Ślokaindravajrā

1Original śloka

विश्वेश्वरि त्वं परिपासि विश्वं
विश्वात्मिका धारयसीति विश्वम्।
विश्वेशवन्द्या भवती भवन्ति
विश्वाश्रया ये त्वयि भक्तिनम्राः ॥ ३३ ॥

विश्वाश्रयाःviśvāśrayāḥrefuges of the universem. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of भवन्ति
येyethose whorelative pronoun यद्, m. nom. pl.; subject of भवन्ति
त्वयिtvayito you (lit. ‘in you’)युष्मद्, loc. sg.; locative of the object of devotion
भक्तिनम्राःbhaktinamrāḥbowed in devotionm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies ये

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • विश्वेश्वरि viśveśvari → विश्व + ईश्वरि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + ī → e. The same in विश्वेश- (विश्व + ईश).
  • विश्वं विश्वात्मिका viśvaṃ viśvātmikā → विश्वम् + विश्वात्मिका
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before the consonant v becomes anusvāra, across the printed line.
  • धारयसीति dhārayasīti → धारयसि + इति
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    i + i → ī.
  • विश्वाश्रया ये viśvāśrayā ye → विश्वाश्रयाः + ये
    visarga sandhi · भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before the voiced y loses its visarga.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विश्वेश्वरि viśveśvari — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विश्व + ईश्वरी · Vigraha: विश्वस्य ईश्वरी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ viśvasya īśvarī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “ruler of the universe”; in the verse: the sovereign of all that is (as in 11.3 विश्वेश्वरि पाहि विश्वम्)
  • विश्वात्मिका viśvātmikā — bahuvrīhi (with the suffix -क, feminine -इका)
    Members: विश्व + आत्मन् · Vigraha: विश्वम् आत्मा यस्याः सा viśvam ātmā yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose self is the universe”; in the verse: the universe is her very being
    Alternative: Also ‘she who is the self of the universe’ (विश्वस्य आत्मा यस्याः स्वरूपम्); the -आत्मिका formation allows both. The reason clause (इति) fits either: because she is one with it, she holds it.
  • विश्वेशवन्द्या viśveśavandyā — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa (with the gerundive वन्द्य) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (विश्वेश)
    Members: विश्वेश + वन्द्य · Vigraha: विश्वस्य ईशाः विश्वेशाः; विश्वेशैः वन्द्या viśvasya īśāḥ viśveśāḥ; viśveśaiḥ vandyā
    Literally “to be revered by the lords of the universe”; in the verse: honoured even by the gods who rule the world
  • विश्वाश्रयाः viśvāśrayāḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: विश्व + आश्रय · Vigraha: विश्वस्य आश्रयाः viśvasya āśrayāḥ
    Literally “refuges of the universe”; in the verse: a shelter for the whole world
  • भक्तिनम्राः bhaktinamrāḥ — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa
    Members: भक्ति + नम्र · Vigraha: भक्त्या नम्राः bhaktyā namrāḥ
    Literally “bent low by devotion”; in the verse: bowing in devotion

C. Morphology

  • भवती bhavatī — The honorific pronoun भवत् ‘Your Honour’ (feminine भवती) takes a third-person verb. It sits beside भवन्ति (√भू, 3 pl.): the same sound in two different words, a deliberate echo.
  • धारयसि dhārayasi — धारयति ‘holds, supports’ has the causative form but the sense of the simple root √धृ. With the preceding विश्वात्मिका … इति: ‘being the universe’s self, you uphold it’.
  • विश्वेशवन्द्या viśveśavandyā — वन्द्य is the gerundive (ण्यत्) of √वन्द् (bhvādi 1Ā ‘to praise, revere’): ‘to be revered’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे विश्वेश्वरि, त्वं विश्वं परिपासि। (त्वं) विश्वात्मिका (असि) इति विश्वं धारयसि। भवती विश्वेशवन्द्या (अस्ति)। ये त्वयि भक्तिनम्राः, (ते) विश्वाश्रयाः भवन्ति।

he viśveśvari, tvaṃ viśvaṃ paripāsi | (tvaṃ) viśvātmikā (asi) iti viśvaṃ dhārayasi | bhavatī viśveśavandyā (asti) | ye tvayi bhaktinamrāḥ, (te) viśvāśrayāḥ bhavanti |

Four short statements, one to a line: she protects; being its self she sustains; she is revered by its lords; her devotees become its refuge.

6Translation

LiteralO Queen of the universe, you protect the universe; being the self of the universe, therefore you uphold the universe. Your Ladyship is to be revered by the lords of the universe; those who are bowed in devotion to you become the refuges of the universe.

ReadableQueen of the universe, you guard the universe; as its very self you hold the universe up. The lords of the universe revere you, and those who bow to you in devotion become the shelter of the universe.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe word विश्व ‘universe’ is struck six times in four lines. She protects the universe, sustains it because it is her own self, is honoured by its rulers, and makes her devotees its refuge. The last line takes up 11.29: those who take refuge in her become a refuge (आश्रयतां प्रयान्ति).

Traditional interpretationThe thought that she holds the world because she is its self is Brahmā’s in the first carita (1.75 त्वयैतद्धार्यते विश्वं), and the gods opened this hymn with the same title (11.3 प्रसीद विश्वेश्वरि पाहि विश्वम्). ‘The lords of the universe’ are the ruling gods, who, as Brahmā says, took their bodies through her (1.84–85).

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has विश्वधारिणी (name 759), ‘she who upholds the universe’. A shared idea; separate works.

11.34Ślokavasantatilakā

1Original śloka

देवि प्रसीद परिपालय नोऽरिभीते-
र्नित्यं यथासुरवधादधुनैव सद्यः।
पापानि सर्वजगतां प्रशमं नयाशु
उत्पातपाकजनितांश्च महोपसर्गान् ॥ ३४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देवि | प्रसीद | परिपालय | नः | अरिभीतेःनित्यम् | यथा | असुरवधात् | अधुना | एव | सद्यःपापानि | सर्वजगताम् | प्रशमम् | नय | आशुउत्पातपाकजनितान् | च | महोपसर्गान्

3IAST

As reciteddevi prasīda paripālaya no'ribhīte-
rnityaṃ yathāsuravadhādadhunaiva sadyaḥ |
pāpāni sarvajagatāṃ praśamaṃ nayāśu
utpātapākajanitāṃśca mahopasargān ||

Separateddevi | prasīda | paripālaya | naḥ | aribhīteḥ
nityam | yathā | asuravadhāt | adhunā | eva | sadyaḥ
pāpāni | sarvajagatām | praśamam | naya | āśu
utpātapākajanitān | ca | mahopasargān

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • नोऽरिभीतेर्नित्यं no'ribhīternityaṃ → नः + अरिभीतेः + नित्यम्
    visarga sandhi, then pūrvarūpa; visarga as r · अतो रोरप्लुतादप्लुते (6.1.113), आद्गुणः (6.1.87), एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109); ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66)
    -aḥ before a becomes -o and absorbs the a (नोऽरि…). After e the visarga of अरिभीतेः stays r before the voiced n. The printed line breaks inside the word (अरिभीते- / र्नित्यं); the word belongs to the first line.
  • यथासुरवधादधुनैव yathāsuravadhādadhunaiva → यथा + असुरवधात् + अधुना + एव
    vowel and consonant sandhi · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); झलां जशोऽन्ते (8.2.39); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88)
    ā + a → ā; the final t becomes d before a vowel; ā + e → ai (अधुनैव).
  • नयाशु nayāśu → नय + आशु
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • नयाशु उत्पात nayāśu utpāta → आशु + उत्पात…
    no sandhi at the end of a pāda · —
    आशु ends the third pāda and उत्पात… begins the fourth. The pause between metrical lines is not bridged, so u + u stay apart.
  • उत्पातपाकजनितांश्च utpātapākajanitāṃśca → उत्पातपाकजनितान् + च
    consonant sandhi (n before c) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Final n before c becomes anusvāra + s, and the s becomes ś beside c: -ांश्च.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अरिभीतेः aribhīteḥ — pañcamī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अरि + भीति · Vigraha: अरिभ्यः भीतिः, तस्याः aribhyaḥ bhītiḥ, tasyāḥ
    Literally “fear from enemies”; in the verse: the dread of hostile powers
    Alternative: Also a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa, अरीणां भीतिः ‘enemies’ fear’, i.e. fear caused by enemies; the sense is the same.
  • असुरवधात् asuravadhāt — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: असुर + वध · Vigraha: असुराणां वधः, तस्मात् asurāṇāṃ vadhaḥ, tasmāt
    Literally “from the slaying of the asuras”; in the verse: through your victory over Śumbha and his host
  • सर्वजगताम् sarvajagatām — karmadhāraya
    Members: सर्व + जगत् · Vigraha: सर्वाणि जगन्ति, तेषाम् sarvāṇi jaganti, teṣām
    Literally “of all worlds”; in the verse: of every world
  • उत्पातपाकजनितान् utpātapākajanitān — tṛtīyā tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (उत्पातपाक)
    Members: उत्पात + पाक + जनित · Vigraha: उत्पातानां पाकः उत्पातपाकः; उत्पातपाकेन जनिताः, तान् utpātānāṃ pākaḥ utpātapākaḥ; utpātapākena janitāḥ, tān
    Literally “produced by the ripening of portents”; in the verse: the disasters that portents foretell, coming to fruition
  • महोपसर्गान् mahopasargān — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + उपसर्ग · Vigraha: महान्तः उपसर्गाः, तान् mahāntaḥ upasargāḥ, tān
    Literally “great calamities”; in the verse: plagues and disasters (महा + उपसर्ग → महोपसर्ग, आद्गुणः 6.1.87)

C. Morphology

  • परिपालय paripālaya — Imperative second singular of the curādi root √पाल् (stem पालय-), with परि ‘all around’: ‘protect on every side’. The stanza has three imperatives: प्रसीद, परिपालय, नय.
  • नय naya — Imperative of √नी (thematic stem नय-). With the accusative of the goal प्रशमम्, ‘lead to quiescence’: an idiom for ‘put an end to’.
  • उत्पातपाकजनितान् utpātapākajanitān — जनित, the last member, is the past participle of the causative of √जन् ‘to be born’: ‘brought to birth, produced’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे देवि, प्रसीद। यथा अधुना एव असुरवधात् सद्यः (अस्मान् अपालयः), (तथा) नित्यं नः अरिभीतेः परिपालय। सर्वजगतां पापानि उत्पातपाकजनितान् महोपसर्गान् च आशु प्रशमं नय।

he devi, prasīda | yathā adhunā eva asuravadhāt sadyaḥ (asmān apālayaḥ), (tathā) nityaṃ naḥ aribhīteḥ paripālaya | sarvajagatāṃ pāpāni utpātapākajanitān mahopasargān ca āśu praśamaṃ naya |

Three imperatives. The second is qualified by a comparison (यथा … सद्यः) whose verb is understood; the third has two objects joined by च and the goal प्रशमम्.

6Translation

LiteralO Devī, be gracious; protect us always from the fear of enemies, as (you did) just now, at once, through the slaying of the asuras; lead quickly to extinction the sins of all the worlds and the great calamities born of the ripening of portents.

ReadableBe gracious, Devī. Guard us always from fear of our foes, as you did just now, at once, by slaying the asuras. Swiftly bring to rest the sins of all the worlds and the great calamities that ripen from evil portents.

‘as you did’ supplies the verb implied by यथा; ‘our’ is supplied with ‘foes’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last long stanza of the hymn, in vasantatilakā (fourteen syllables a line), the metre of the gods’ hymn in chapter 4, which also opened a stanza with देवि प्रसीद (4.14). The gods ask for continued protection, citing what she has just done, and for the calming of sin and calamity in every world.

Traditional interpretationThe Devī herself promises the same in chapter 12: hearing her story ‘would quell all calamities born of plague, and the threefold portent’ (12.8 उपसर्गानशेषांस्तु महामारीसमुद्भवान्। तथा त्रिविधमुत्पातं माहात्म्यं शमयेन्मम), and she prescribes its hearing in every rite of pacification (12.16–17). The prayer of chapter 11 and the promise of chapter 12 answer each other.

Modern reflectionA modern reading: ‘as you did just now’ — the prayer rests on remembered help. Gratitude for the past becomes trust for the future.

11.35Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

प्रणतानां प्रसीद त्वं देवि विश्वार्तिहारिणि।
त्रैलोक्यवासिनामीड्ये लोकानां वरदा भव ॥ ३५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

प्रणतानाम् | प्रसीद | त्वम् | देवि | विश्वार्तिहारिणित्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् | ईड्ये | लोकानाम् | वरदा | भव

3IAST

As recitedpraṇatānāṃ prasīda tvaṃ devi viśvārtihāriṇi |
trailokyavāsināmīḍye lokānāṃ varadā bhava ||

Separatedpraṇatānām | prasīda | tvam | devi | viśvārtihāriṇi
trailokyavāsinām | īḍye | lokānām | varadā | bhava

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
प्रणतानाम्praṇatānāmto those who bow downpast participle (kta) of प्र + √नम् (bhvādi, 1P ‘to bow’), m. gen. pl.; genitive with प्रसीद
प्रसीदprasīdabe graciousप्र + √सद् (bhvādi, 1P), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
त्वम्tvamyouयुष्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of प्रसीद and भव

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • प्रणतानां प्रसीद त्वं देवि praṇatānāṃ prasīda tvaṃ devi → प्रणतानाम् + प्रसीद; त्वम् + देवि
    anusvāra sandhi · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23)
    Final m before a consonant becomes anusvāra; the same in लोकानां वरदा.
  • विश्वार्तिहारिणि viśvārtihāriṇi → विश्व + आर्ति + हारिणि
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā.
  • त्रैलोक्यवासिनामीड्ये trailokyavāsināmīḍye → त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् + ईड्ये
    final m before a vowel · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) applies only before a consonant
    The m stays and is written together with the following vowel.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • विश्वार्तिहारिणि viśvārtihāriṇi — upapada tatpuruṣa (हारिन् = √हृ with the agent affix -इन्, feminine -ई) containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (विश्वार्ति)
    Members: विश्व + आर्ति + हारिन् · Vigraha: विश्वस्य आर्तिः विश्वार्तिः; विश्वार्तिं हरति इति विश्वार्तिहारिणी, तत्सम्बुद्धौ viśvasya ārtiḥ viśvārtiḥ; viśvārtiṃ harati iti viśvārtihāriṇī, tatsambuddhau
    Literally “she who takes away the affliction of the world”; in the verse: remover of all suffering
  • त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् trailokyavāsinām — upapada tatpuruṣa (वासिन् = √वस् with the agent affix -इन्)
    Members: त्रैलोक्य + वासिन् · Vigraha: त्रैलोक्ये वसन्ति इति त्रैलोक्यवासिनः, तेषाम् trailokye vasanti iti trailokyavāsinaḥ, teṣām
    Literally “of those who dwell in the three worlds”; in the verse: all beings, gods and men
  • वरदा varadā — upapada tatpuruṣa (√दा with the affix -अ)
    Members: वर + दा · Vigraha: वरं ददाति इति वरदा varaṃ dadāti iti varadā
    Literally “boon-giver”; in the verse: she who grants what is asked

C. Morphology

  • ईड्ये īḍye — Gerundive of √ईड् ‘to praise’: ईड्य ‘to be praised’, feminine ईड्या, vocative ईड्ये. With a gerundive the agent may stand in the genitive (त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् ‘by the dwellers of the three worlds’).
  • प्रणतानाम् praṇatānām — Genitive plural of the past participle प्रणत ‘bowed down’ (the n of √नम् becomes ṇ after the r of the prefix प्र). प्रसीद takes the genitive of the person favoured: ‘be gracious to those who bow’.
  • त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् trailokyavāsinām — त्रैलोक्य ‘the three worlds’ is a taddhita (with vṛddhi of the first vowel) from त्रिलोक; वासिन् ‘dweller’ is an agent noun of √वस् ‘to dwell’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

हे विश्वार्तिहारिणि त्रैलोक्यवासिनाम् ईड्ये देवि, त्वं प्रणतानां प्रसीद, लोकानां वरदा भव।

he viśvārtihāriṇi trailokyavāsinām īḍye devi, tvaṃ praṇatānāṃ prasīda, lokānāṃ varadā bhava |

Two imperatives addressed to one subject (त्वम्), each with a genitive of the persons concerned (प्रणतानाम्, लोकानाम्); three vocatives name her.

6Translation

LiteralBe gracious, you, to those who bow down, O Devī, remover of the world’s affliction; O you who are to be praised by the dwellers of the three worlds, be the giver of boons to the worlds.

ReadableBe gracious to those who bow before you, Devī, who take away the sorrow of the world. Praised by all who dwell in the three worlds, be the giver of boons to all the worlds.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe last verse of the gods’ hymn (the Nārāyaṇī Stuti, 11.3–35) returns to the anuṣṭubh and closes with a request: ‘be the giver of boons’ (वरदा भव). The Devī answers at once in the next verses with the same word: वरदाहं सुरगणा वरं यन्मनसेच्छथ (11.37), ‘I am the giver of boons, hosts of gods; choose the boon you wish’.

Traditional interpretationThe hymn is framed by one prayer. It opened with देवि प्रपन्नार्तिहरे प्रसीद (11.3), ‘O Devī who remove the affliction of those who take refuge, be gracious’, and it ends with प्रसीद … विश्वार्तिहारिणि: the remover of the suppliants’ pain is now the remover of the world’s.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the name वरदा (name 331). A shared epithet; separate works.

11.36Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

देव्युवाच ॥ ३६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देवी | उवाच

देवीdevīthe Goddessf. nom. sg. of देवी (ī-stem); subject of उवाच
उवाचuvācasaid√वच् (adādi, 2P ‘to speak’), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada

  • देव्युवाच devyuvāca → देवी + उवाच
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final ī before the unlike vowel u becomes y: devy uvāca.

C. Morphology

  • उवाच uvāca — Perfect of √वच् with samprasāraṇa in the reduplication (उ-वाच), as in 1.1: the narrative past, ‘(she) said’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवी उवाच।

devī uvāca |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe Goddess said:

ReadableThe Goddess said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe hymn of the gods (11.2–35) is over, and the Goddess answers it. This is her first speech since the death of Śumbha; within the frame it is still Medhas who reports it to Suratha and Samādhi (the ṛṣi of 11.1).

11.37Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

वरदाहं सुरगणा वरं यन्मनसेच्छथ।
तं वृणुध्वं प्रयच्छामि जगतामुपकारकम् ॥ ३७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

वरदा | अहम् | सुरगणाः | वरम् | यत् | मनसा | इच्छथतम् | वृणुध्वम् | प्रयच्छामि | जगताम् | उपकारकम्

3IAST

As recitedvaradāhaṃ suragaṇā varaṃ yanmanasecchatha |
taṃ vṛṇudhvaṃ prayacchāmi jagatāmupakārakam ||

Separatedvaradā | aham | suragaṇāḥ | varam | yat | manasā | icchatha
tam | vṛṇudhvam | prayacchāmi | jagatām | upakārakam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
वरदाvaradāgiver of boonsf. nom. sg. of वरदा (वरद + ṭāp); compound (see Samāsa); predicate of अहम्, with (अस्मि) understood
अहम्ahamIfirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, nom. sg.; subject of the nominal sentence
सुरगणाःsuragaṇāḥO hosts of godsm. voc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); address
वरम्varamboonm. acc. sg. of वर; object of इच्छथ inside the relative clause, and taken up by तम्
यत्yatwhich, whateverrelative pronoun यद्, n. acc. sg., used generically (‘whatever’) with the masculine वरम्; correlative तम्
मनसाmanasāin (your) mind, at heartn. instr. sg. of मनस् (s-stem); instrument with इच्छथ
इच्छथicchathayou desire√इष् (tudādi, 6P ‘to wish’, present stem इच्छ-), laṭ (present), madhyama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
तम्tamthat (boon)pronoun तद्, m. acc. sg.; correlative of यत्, agreeing with वरम्; object of वृणुध्वम्
वृणुध्वम्vṛṇudhvamchoose√वृ (वृञ्, svādi, 5U ‘to choose’), loṭ (imperative), madhyama-puruṣa bahuvacana, ātmanepada
प्रयच्छामिprayacchāmiI grant, I shall giveप्र + √दा (bhvādi, 1P, present stem यच्छ-), laṭ (present), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
जगताम्jagatāmto the worlds, for the worldsn. gen. pl. of जगत्; with उपकारकम्
उपकारकम्upakārakambeneficial, helpfulm. acc. sg. of उपकारक (उप + √कृ + ṇvul, agent noun in -aka); qualifies तम् (वरम्)

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वरदाहं varadāhaṃ → वरदा + अहम्
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    ā + a → ā. The final m of अहम् becomes anusvāra before the s of सुरगणाः (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • सुरगणा वरं suragaṇā varaṃ → सुरगणाः + वरम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before the voiced v: the visarga (as ru) becomes y, and before a consonant that y is always lost, leaving -ā.
  • यन्मनसेच्छथ yanmanasecchatha → यत् + मनसा + इच्छथ
    consonant sandhi (nasalisation); vowel sandhi (guṇa) · यरोऽनुनासिकेऽनुनासिको वा (8.4.45); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    Final t (voiced to d at word end, 8.2.39) becomes the nasal n before the m of मनसा; then ā + i → e (मनसा + इच्छथ → मनसेच्छथ).
  • जगतामुपकारकम् jagatāmupakārakam → जगताम् + उपकारकम्
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    The m stands before the vowel u and is written with it.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • वरदा varadā — upapada tatpuruṣa
    Members: वर + द · Vigraha: वरं ददाति इति वरदा varaṃ dadāti iti varadā
    Literally “she who gives boons”; in the verse: the bestower of boons — the title with which she offers the gods their wish
  • सुरगणाः suragaṇāḥ — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: सुर + गण · Vigraha: सुराणां गणाः surāṇāṃ gaṇāḥ
    Literally “the hosts of the gods”; in the verse: the assembled gods, addressed together

C. Morphology

  • वृणुध्वम् vṛṇudhvam — Imperative, 2nd plural, ātmanepada, of √वृ (svādi, stem वृणु-): ‘choose for yourselves’. The middle voice suits a choice made for one’s own benefit.
  • प्रयच्छामि prayacchāmi — √दा forms its present on the substitute stem यच्छ-; with प्र, ‘to give, to grant’. The present stands for the immediate future: ‘I (will) grant it’.
  • इच्छथ icchatha — Present of √इष् (tudādi), stem इच्छ-, 2nd plural: ‘you wish’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) सुरगणाः, अहं वरदा (अस्मि)। यत् वरं मनसा इच्छथ, जगताम् उपकारकं तं वृणुध्वम्; (तं) प्रयच्छामि।

(he) suragaṇāḥ, ahaṃ varadā (asmi) | yat varaṃ manasā icchatha, jagatām upakārakaṃ taṃ vṛṇudhvam; (taṃ) prayacchāmi |

A nominal sentence (अहं वरदा) and the address; then a relative clause (यत् वरं … इच्छथ) picked up by तम्, the object of the imperative वृणुध्वम्; प्रयच्छामि closes with an implied object.

6Translation

LiteralI am the giver of boons, O hosts of gods. The boon which you desire in your mind — choose that, beneficial to the worlds; I grant it.

ReadableI am the giver of boons, you hosts of gods. Choose the boon your minds desire, one that will benefit the worlds, and I shall grant it.

The object of प्रयच्छामि is supplied (‘it’), and the present is rendered as a promise (‘I shall grant’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningPleased by the hymn, the Goddess offers a boon, as she did after the hymn of chapter 4 (4.32). She sets one condition herself: the boon is to be जगताम् उपकारकम्, of benefit to the worlds, not to the gods alone.

Traditional interpretationEach of the text’s great hymns is followed by a boon (1.95–98 to Viṣṇu from the asuras, 4.32–37 and here). In the Śākta reading the pattern teaches that praise is answered: the Goddess is वरदा, the one who grants what is asked.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) gives the same epithet as a name: 331, वरदा. Here it is the Goddess’s own word for herself in the Mārkaṇḍeya Purāṇa; the shared title is a shared Śākta idea, not a borrowing between the texts.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: यत् … तम्: the relative is neuter and the correlative masculine. Read here as a generic ‘whatever (boon)’; it may also be a loose agreement for यम्. The sense is the same either way.

11.38Uvāca · speaker

1Original śloka

देवा ऊचुः ॥ ३८ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

देवाः | ऊचुः

3IAST

As reciteddevā ūcuḥ ||

Separateddevāḥ | ūcuḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
देवाःdevāḥthe godsm. nom. pl. of देव; subject of ऊचुः
ऊचुःūcuḥsaid√वच् (adādi, 2P), liṭ (perfect), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • देवा ऊचुः devā ūcuḥ → देवाः + ऊचुः
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17), लोपः शाकल्यस्य (8.3.19)
    -āḥ before a vowel: the visarga becomes y and the y is dropped; the two vowels then stand apart and are not joined again.

C. Morphology

  • ऊचुः ūcuḥ — Perfect 3rd plural of √वच्: samprasāraṇa (va → u) in both the reduplication and the root, उ + उच् → ऊच्, with the ending -उः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

देवाः ऊचुः।

devāḥ ūcuḥ |

Subject and verb: a speaker label.

6Translation

LiteralThe gods said:

ReadableThe gods said:

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods reply to the Goddess’s offer of a boon (11.37). Their request follows in a single verse (11.39).

11.39Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनं त्रैलोक्यस्याखिलेश्वरि।
एवमेव त्वया कार्यमस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम् ॥ ३९ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनम् | त्रैलोक्यस्य | अखिलेश्वरिएवम् | एव | त्वया | कार्यम् | अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम्

3IAST

As recitedsarvābādhāpraśamanaṃ trailokyasyākhileśvari |
evameva tvayā kāryamasmadvairivināśanam ||

Separatedsarvābādhāpraśamanam | trailokyasya | akhileśvari
evam | eva | tvayā | kāryam | asmadvairivināśanam

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनम्sarvābādhāpraśamanamthe quelling of all afflictionsn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); subject of (भवतु) कार्यम्, together with अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम्
त्रैलोक्यस्यtrailokyasyaof the three worldsn. gen. sg. of त्रैलोक्य (taddhita from त्रिलोकी/त्रिलोक: ‘the triad of worlds’); with the afflictions
अखिलेश्वरिakhileśvariO Queen of allf. voc. sg. of अखिलेश्वरी; compound (see Samāsa)
एवम्evamthus, in this wayindeclinable (adverb of manner)
एवevajust, exactlyindeclinable (emphatic particle); with एवम्, ‘in just this way’
त्वयाtvayāby yousecond-person pronoun युष्मद्, instr. sg.; agent with the gerundive कार्यम्
कार्यम्kāryamis to be done, must be donegerundive (kṛtya, ṇyat) of √कृ (tanādi, 8U), n. nom. sg.; predicate
अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम्asmadvairivināśanamthe destruction of our enemiesn. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); second subject of कार्यम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनं sarvābādhāpraśamanaṃ → सर्व + आबाधा + प्रशमनम्
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + ā → ā (सर्व + आबाधा → सर्वाबाधा). The final m becomes anusvāra before the t of त्रैलोक्यस्य (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • त्रैलोक्यस्याखिलेश्वरि trailokyasyākhileśvari → त्रैलोक्यस्य + अखिलेश्वरि
    vowel sandhi (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + a → ā. Inside the vocative compound, अखिल + ईश्वरि gives -e- by guṇa (आद्गुणः, 6.1.87).
  • एवमेव evameva → एवम् + एव
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    The m is written with the following e. The same in कार्यमस्मद्.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनम् sarvābādhāpraśamanam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member सर्वाबाधा is a karmadhāraya: सर्वाः च ताः आबाधाः च)
    Members: सर्वाबाधा + प्रशमन · Vigraha: सर्वासाम् आबाधानां प्रशमनम् sarvāsām ābādhānāṃ praśamanam
    Literally “the calming of all afflictions”; in the verse: the removal of every trouble that besets the three worlds
  • अखिलेश्वरि akhileśvari — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: अखिल + ईश्वरी · Vigraha: अखिलस्य ईश्वरी akhilasya īśvarī
    Literally “ruler of the whole”; in the verse: sovereign of everything
  • अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम् asmadvairivināśanam — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (अस्मद्वैरि)
    Members: अस्मद् + वैरि + विनाशन · Vigraha: अस्माकं वैरिणः अस्मद्वैरिणः; तेषां विनाशनम् asmākaṃ vairiṇaḥ asmadvairiṇaḥ; teṣāṃ vināśanam
    Literally “the destruction of the enemies of us”; in the verse: the slaying of our enemies — as she has just slain Śumbha and Niśumbha

C. Morphology

  • कार्यम् kāryam — Gerundive of √कृ with ṇyat (कृ + य, vṛddhi → कार्य): ‘to be done’. The agent goes in the instrumental (त्वया), the thing to be done is the grammatical subject; neuter singular agrees with प्रशमनम् and विनाशनम् taken together as one task.
  • त्रैलोक्यस्य trailokyasya — Taddhita with vṛddhi of the first syllable: त्रि-लोक → त्रैलोक्य ‘the three worlds as a whole’ (neuter collective).

D. Anvaya — prose order

(हे) अखिलेश्वरि, त्रैलोक्यस्य सर्वाबाधाप्रशमनम् (तथा) अस्मद्वैरिविनाशनम् एवम् एव त्वया कार्यम्।

(he) akhileśvari, trailokyasya sarvābādhāpraśamanam (tathā) asmadvairivināśanam evam eva tvayā kāryam |

A passive construction with the gerundive: the two nouns of action are the subjects, कार्यम् the predicate, त्वया the agent; एवम् एव points back to what she has just done.

6Translation

LiteralO Queen of all, the quelling of all afflictions of the three worlds — and in just this way the destruction of our enemies — is to be done by you.

ReadableO Sovereign of all, quell every affliction of the three worlds, and destroy our enemies just as you have done now.

The gerundive (‘is to be done by you’) is rendered as a request; ‘as you have done now’ interprets एवम् एव, ‘in just this way’.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe gods ask for two things: that every affliction of the three worlds be quelled, and that their enemies be destroyed एवम् एव, ‘in just this way’ — as Śumbha and Niśumbha have just been. They ask not for one deliverance but for a standing promise.

Traditional interpretationThe request matches the Goddess’s own condition in 11.37: the boon is for the three worlds, not for the gods alone. Her answer (11.41–55) turns it into the prophecy of her future descents.

Textual Verification Required / uncertain: सर्वाबाधा: read as सर्व + आबाधा ‘all afflictions’ (the usual reading); सर्व + अबाधा would make no sense here.

  • देव्युवाच devyuvāca → देवी + उवाच
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    As in 11.36: ī before u becomes y.

Direct textual meaningThe Goddess grants the gods’ request. What follows (11.41–55) is her prophecy: the future forms in which she will come down to destroy the enemies of the worlds. The speech runs to the end of the chapter; chapter 12 continues it with the fruits of hearing the text.

11.41Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

वैवस्वतेऽन्तरे प्राप्ते अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे।
शुम्भो निशुम्भश्चैवान्यावुत्पत्स्येते महासुरौ ॥ ४१ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

वैवस्वते | अन्तरे | प्राप्ते | अष्टाविंशतिमे | युगेशुम्भः | निशुम्भः | च | एव | अन्यौ | उत्पत्स्येते | महासुरौ

3IAST

As recitedvaivasvate'ntare prāpte aṣṭāviṃśatime yuge |
śumbho niśumbhaścaivānyāvutpatsyete mahāsurau ||

Separatedvaivasvate | antare | prāpte | aṣṭāviṃśatime | yuge
śumbhaḥ | niśumbhaḥ | ca | eva | anyau | utpatsyete | mahāsurau

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
वैवस्वतेvaivasvatein the Vaivasvata (age)n. loc. sg. of वैवस्वत (taddhita from विवस्वत् ‘the Sun’: belonging to Vaivasvata, the seventh Manu); qualifies अन्तरे
अन्तरेantaremanvantara, intervaln. loc. sg. of अन्तर; short for मन्वन्तरे, as in 1.4; locative absolute with प्राप्ते
प्राप्तेprāptehaving come, when (it) has arrivedpast passive participle (kta) of प्र + √आप् (svādi, 5P ‘to reach’), n. loc. sg.; predicate of the locative absolute
अष्टाविंशतिमेaṣṭāviṃśatimein the twenty-eighthordinal of अष्टाविंशति ‘twenty-eight’, n. loc. sg.; qualifies युगे
युगेyugein the yuga, the agen. loc. sg. of युग; here one great cycle (mahāyuga) of the manvantara
शुम्भःśumbhaḥŚumbham. nom. sg.; proper name; subject (with निशुम्भः) of उत्पत्स्येते
निशुम्भःniśumbhaḥNiśumbham. nom. sg.; proper name
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
एवevaindeed, likewiseindeclinable (emphatic particle)
अन्यौanyautwo others, another (pair)pronominal adjective अन्य, m. nom. du.; qualifies the pair शुम्भः निशुम्भः
उत्पत्स्येतेutpatsyetewill arise, will be bornउद् + √पद् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to go’; with उद् ‘to arise’), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa dvivacana, ātmanepada
महासुरौmahāsurautwo great asurasm. nom. du.; compound (see Samāsa); in apposition to the pair

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • वैवस्वतेऽन्तरे vaivasvate'ntare → वैवस्वते + अन्तरे
    vowel sandhi (pūrvarūpa) · एङः पदान्तादति (6.1.109)
    -e + a- → -e’-: the initial a is absorbed (avagraha). The same junction is left open at प्राप्ते अष्टा- (see sandhi_exceptions).
  • शुम्भो निशुम्भश्चैवान्यावुत्पत्स्येते śumbho niśumbhaścaivānyāvutpatsyete → शुम्भः + निशुम्भः + च + एव + अन्यौ + उत्पत्स्येते
    visarga sandhi; vowel sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114); विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40); वृद्धिरेचि (6.1.88); अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101); एचोऽयवायावः (6.1.78)
    शुम्भः before the voiced n becomes शुम्भो; निशुम्भः before c becomes निशुम्भश्; च + एव → चैव (a + e → ai); चैव + अन्यौ → चैवान्यौ (a + a → ā); अन्यौ before u becomes अन्याव् (au → āv); that v could optionally be dropped (लोपः शाकल्यस्य, 8.3.19, giving अन्या उत्-), but the text keeps it.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महासुरौ mahāsurau — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान्तौ च तौ असुरौ च mahāntau ca tau asurau ca
    Literally “two great asuras”; in the verse: a mighty demon pair (महत् becomes महा- as the first member of a karmadhāraya)

C. Morphology

  • उत्पत्स्येते utpatsyete — Future of उद् + √पद् (aniṭ): stem पत्स्य- (d → t before s), dual ātmanepada -इते → -एते after the stem vowel: ‘(the two) will arise’. The first future verb of the prophecy.
  • अष्टाविंशतिमे aṣṭāviṃśatime — An ordinal in -म from अष्टाविंशति. The regular ordinals of the twenties are अष्टाविंश and अष्टाविंशतितम; the shortened अष्टाविंशतिम is a Purāṇic form, kept by the metre.
  • प्राप्ते prāpte — Past participle of प्र + √आप् in the locative absolute with अन्तरे: ‘the Vaivasvata age having come’, i.e. in the Vaivasvata age.

D. Anvaya — prose order

वैवस्वते अन्तरे प्राप्ते अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे अन्यौ शुम्भः निशुम्भः च एव महासुरौ उत्पत्स्येते।

vaivasvate antare prāpte aṣṭāviṃśatime yuge anyau śumbhaḥ niśumbhaḥ ca eva mahāsurau utpatsyete |

Time is given by a locative absolute (वैवस्वते अन्तरे प्राप्ते) and a locative (अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे); the subject is the pair शुम्भः निशुम्भः च with अन्यौ and महासुरौ; the verb is the dual future उत्पत्स्येते.

Left unjoined: प्राप्ते | अष्टाविंशतिमे — Hiatus at the pāda boundary: वैवस्वतेऽन्तरे प्राप्ते is a complete eight-syllable pāda and अष्टाविंशतिमे युगे the next. Eliding the a (प्राप्तेऽष्टाविंशतिमे) would take a syllable from the second pāda; the verse, like much Purāṇic verse, leaves the vowels apart across the pāda break (a metrical licence), and the Gita Press prints them so.

6Translation

LiteralWhen the Vaivasvata manvantara has come, in the twenty-eighth yuga, two others, Śumbha and Niśumbha, great asuras, will arise.

ReadableIn the age of Vaivasvata Manu, in its twenty-eighth cycle, two other great asuras named Śumbha and Niśumbha will be born.

‘Cycle’ renders युग, which here means a whole round of four ages; ‘named’ interprets अन्यौ: a second pair bearing the same names.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe prophecy begins with a date. The present story is set long before; the Goddess now looks ahead to the seventh manvantara, that of Vaivasvata Manu, and to its twenty-eighth great cycle, when a second Śumbha and Niśumbha will be born. Her answer to the gods’ request (11.39) is a promise to return.

Traditional interpretationIn Purāṇic reckoning the present world-age is the twenty-eighth great cycle of the Vaivasvata manvantara, the cycle in which Kṛṣṇa was born. The next verse confirms the setting: the Goddess will be born in Nanda’s house.

11.42Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

नन्दगोपगृहे जाता यशोदागर्भसम्भवा।
ततस्तौ नाशयिष्यामि विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी ॥ ४२ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

नन्दगोपगृहे | जाता | यशोदागर्भसम्भवाततः | तौ | नाशयिष्यामि | विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी

3IAST

As recitednandagopagṛhe jātā yaśodāgarbhasambhavā |
tatastau nāśayiṣyāmi vindhyācalanivāsinī ||

Separatednandagopagṛhe | jātā | yaśodāgarbhasambhavā
tataḥ | tau | nāśayiṣyāmi | vindhyācalanivāsinī

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
नन्दगोपगृहेnandagopagṛhein the house of Nanda the cowherdn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); place of जाता
जाताjātābornpast participle (kta) of √जन् (divādi, 4Ā ‘to be born’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies the subject ‘I’ (अहम्, understood from the verb)
यशोदागर्भसम्भवाyaśodāgarbhasambhavāborn from the womb of Yaśodāf. nom. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies the subject ‘I’
ततःtataḥthen, thereafterindeclinable (adverb of time, तद् + tas)
तौtauthose twopronoun तद्, m. acc. du.; object of नाशयिष्यामि (Śumbha and Niśumbha)
नाशयिष्यामिnāśayiṣyāmiI shall destroycausative of √नश् (divādi, 4P ‘to perish’; causative ‘to destroy’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनीvindhyācalanivāsinīdwelling on the Vindhya mountainf. nom. sg. of विन्ध्याचलनिवासिन्; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies the subject

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ततस्तौ tatastau → ततः + तौ
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34)
    Visarga before t becomes s.
  • विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी vindhyācalanivāsinī → विन्ध्य + अचल + निवासिनी
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (savarṇa-dīrgha) · अकः सवर्णे दीर्घः (6.1.101)
    a + a → ā (विन्ध्य + अचल → विन्ध्याचल).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • नन्दगोपगृहे nandagopagṛhe — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (its first member नन्दगोप is a karmadhāraya: नन्दः च असौ गोपः च)
    Members: नन्दगोप + गृह · Vigraha: नन्दगोपस्य गृहम्, तस्मिन् nandagopasya gṛham, tasmin
    Literally “in the house of Nanda the cowherd”; in the verse: in the cowherd settlement where Kṛṣṇa grew up
  • यशोदागर्भसम्भवा yaśodāgarbhasambhavā — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (यशोदागर्भ)
    Members: यशोदागर्भ + सम्भव · Vigraha: यशोदायाः गर्भः यशोदागर्भः; यशोदागर्भात् सम्भवः यस्याः सा yaśodāyāḥ garbhaḥ yaśodāgarbhaḥ; yaśodāgarbhāt sambhavaḥ yasyāḥ sā
    Literally “she whose birth is from Yaśodā’s womb”; in the verse: born as Yaśodā’s daughter
    Alternative: Also read as a pañcamī tatpuruṣa of the upapada kind, यशोदागर्भात् सम्भवति इति, ‘arising from Yaśodā’s womb’; the sense is the same. The bahuvrīhi matches चैत्रवंशसमुद्भवः in 1.4.
  • विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी vindhyācalanivāsinī — upapada tatpuruṣa (saptamī; the agent suffix -in of habit), its first member a karmadhāraya: विन्ध्यः च असौ अचलः च
    Members: विन्ध्याचल + निवासिन् · Vigraha: विन्ध्याचले निवसति इति (शीला) vindhyācale nivasati iti (śīlā)
    Literally “she who dwells on the Vindhya mountain”; in the verse: Vindhyavāsinī, the Goddess of the Vindhya hills

C. Morphology

  • नाशयिष्यामि nāśayiṣyāmi — Causative stem नाशय- (√नश् with vṛddhi + णिच्) with the future suffix -इष्य- and the 1st singular -आमि: ‘I shall cause to perish’.
  • जाता jātā — Past participle of √जन्: the root’s n is replaced by ā (ज + आ + त → जात), feminine जाता. Used as a finite verb would be: ‘(I,) born …’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

(अहं) नन्दगोपगृहे यशोदागर्भसम्भवा जाता विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी (सती) ततः तौ नाशयिष्यामि।

(ahaṃ) nandagopagṛhe yaśodāgarbhasambhavā jātā vindhyācalanivāsinī (satī) tataḥ tau nāśayiṣyāmi |

The subject is the ‘I’ of the verb; the three feminine nominatives (जाता, यशोदागर्भसम्भवा, विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी) describe her; ततः तौ नाशयिष्यामि is the main clause.

6Translation

LiteralBorn in the house of Nanda the cowherd, born from the womb of Yaśodā, then, dwelling on the Vindhya mountain, I shall destroy those two.

ReadableBorn in the house of Nanda the cowherd, from the womb of Yaśodā, I shall then dwell on the Vindhya mountain and destroy them both.

The participial nominative विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी is turned into a finite clause (‘I shall dwell’).

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe first future form: the Goddess will be born to Yaśodā in the house of Nanda the cowherd, and, dwelling on the Vindhya hills, will slay the second Śumbha and Niśumbha. The text names no new name here; the epithet विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी serves as one.

Traditional interpretationThis is the daughter of Yaśodā whom the Purāṇic Kṛṣṇa story (Harivaṃśa, Bhāgavata) tells of: exchanged for the infant Kṛṣṇa, she escapes the hands of Kaṃsa and goes to dwell on the Vindhya. The tradition identifies her with Yogamāyā and with Vindhyavāsinī, still worshipped on the Vindhya hills.

Comparative Śākta / ŚrīvidyāLalitā Sahasranāma (Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa) has the identical compound as a name: 336, विन्ध्याचलनिवासिनी. The two texts are distinct; they share the cult of the Vindhya Goddess.

11.43Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

पुनरप्यतिरौद्रेण रूपेण पृथिवीतले।
अवतीर्य हनिष्यामि वैप्रचित्तांस्तु दानवान् ॥ ४३ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

पुनः | अपि | अतिरौद्रेण | रूपेण | पृथिवीतलेअवतीर्य | हनिष्यामि | वैप्रचित्तान् | तु | दानवान्

3IAST

As recitedpunarapyatiraudreṇa rūpeṇa pṛthivītale |
avatīrya haniṣyāmi vaipracittāṃstu dānavān ||

Separatedpunaḥ | api | atiraudreṇa | rūpeṇa | pṛthivītale
avatīrya | haniṣyāmi | vaipracittān | tu | dānavān

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
पुनःpunaḥagainindeclinable (adverb); its visarga stands for r
अपिapialso, once moreindeclinable (particle); with पुनः, ‘yet again’
अतिरौद्रेणatiraudreṇaexceedingly terriblen. instr. sg. of अतिरौद्र; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies रूपेण
रूपेणrūpeṇain a form, with a formn. instr. sg. of रूप; instrumental of manner (‘in a form’)
पृथिवीतलेpṛthivītaleon the surface of the earthn. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); place of अवतीर्य
अवतीर्यavatīryahaving descendedabsolutive (lyap) of अव + √तॄ (bhvādi, 1P ‘to cross’; with अव ‘to descend’)
हनिष्यामिhaniṣyāmiI shall slay√हन् (adādi, 2P ‘to strike, kill’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
वैप्रचित्तान्vaipracittānthe Vaipracittas, descendants of Vipracittim. acc. pl. of वैप्रचित्त (taddhita aṇ of descent from विप्रचित्ति); qualifies दानवान्
तुtuand, in turnindeclinable (particle, connective)
दानवान्dānavānDānavas (demons, sons of Danu)m. acc. pl. of दानव (taddhita from दनु); object of हनिष्यामि

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • पुनरप्यतिरौद्रेण punarapyatiraudreṇa → पुनः + अपि + अतिरौद्रेण
    visarga sandhi (r restored); vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    The visarga of पुनः stands for an original r, which reappears before a vowel (पुनर् + अपि). Then i before the unlike vowel a becomes y (अपि + अति → अप्यति).
  • वैप्रचित्तांस्तु vaipracittāṃstu → वैप्रचित्तान् + तु
    consonant sandhi (n before t) · नश्छव्यप्रशान् (8.3.7)
    Final n before t becomes ṃs: the vowel is nasalised (written as anusvāra) and an s is inserted.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • अतिरौद्रेण atiraudreṇa — prādi tatpuruṣa
    Members: अति + रौद्र · Vigraha: अतिशयेन रौद्रम्, तेन atiśayena raudram, tena
    Literally “exceedingly fierce”; in the verse: terrible beyond measure — the form that will devour the Vaipracittas (11.44)
  • पृथिवीतले pṛthivītale — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: पृथिवी + तल · Vigraha: पृथिव्याः तलम्, तस्मिन् pṛthivyāḥ talam, tasmin
    Literally “on the surface of the earth”; in the verse: on earth, among men

C. Morphology

  • अवतीर्य avatīrya — Absolutive with a prefix (-य, lyap) of अव + √तॄ; the root vowel ṝ becomes īr (तीर्): ‘having descended’. The verb from which avatāra, ‘descent’, is formed.
  • हनिष्यामि haniṣyāmi — Future of √हन् with the connecting vowel i (हन् + इष्य + आमि): ‘I shall kill’.
  • वैप्रचित्तान् vaipracittān — Taddhita of descent with vṛddhi of the first syllable: विप्रचित्ति → वैप्रचित्त ‘son or descendant of Vipracitti’, a Dānava chief.

D. Anvaya — prose order

पुनः अपि अतिरौद्रेण रूपेण पृथिवीतले अवतीर्य (अहं) वैप्रचित्तान् दानवान् तु हनिष्यामि।

punaḥ api atiraudreṇa rūpeṇa pṛthivītale avatīrya (ahaṃ) vaipracittān dānavān tu haniṣyāmi |

The absolutive अवतीर्य, with its manner (अतिरौद्रेण रूपेण) and place (पृथिवीतले), precedes the main verb हनिष्यामि and its object वैप्रचित्तान् दानवान्.

6Translation

LiteralYet again, having descended in an exceedingly terrible form onto the surface of the earth, I shall slay the Dānavas, the descendants of Vipracitti.

ReadableAnd again, descending to earth in a form of utter ferocity, I shall slay the Dānavas born of Vipracitti.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe second descent. The Goddess will come down to earth in a most terrible form to kill the Vaipracittas, the Dānava descendants of Vipracitti. The verb अवतीर्य, ‘having descended’, names what the whole passage describes: her avatāras.

Traditional interpretationThe form is named only in the next verse, from its result: her teeth will be red as pomegranate flowers, and she will be called Raktadantikā (11.44–45).

11.44Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

भक्षयन्त्याश्च तानुग्रान् वैप्रचित्तान्महासुरान्।
रक्ता दन्ता भविष्यन्ति दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः ॥ ४४ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

भक्षयन्त्याः | च | तान् | उग्रान् | वैप्रचित्तान् | महासुरान्रक्ताः | दन्ताः | भविष्यन्ति | दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः

3IAST

As recitedbhakṣayantyāśca tānugrān vaipracittānmahāsurān |
raktā dantā bhaviṣyanti dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ ||

Separatedbhakṣayantyāḥ | ca | tān | ugrān | vaipracittān | mahāsurān
raktāḥ | dantāḥ | bhaviṣyanti | dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
भक्षयन्त्याःbhakṣayantyāḥof (me) as I devourpresent participle (śatṛ) of √भक्ष् (curādi, 10, stem भक्षय- ‘to eat’), f. gen. sg. (भक्षयन्ती); possessive with दन्ताः, ‘my’ understood
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
तान्tānthosepronoun तद्, m. acc. pl.; qualifies the asuras
उग्रान्ugrānfiercem. acc. pl. of उग्र; qualifies महासुरान्
वैप्रचित्तान्vaipracittānthe Vaipracittasm. acc. pl. of वैप्रचित्त (as in 11.43); qualifies महासुरान्
महासुरान्mahāsurāngreat asurasm. acc. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); object of भक्षयन्त्याः
रक्ताःraktāḥredpast participle (kta) of √रञ्ज् (‘to be coloured’), m. nom. pl.; predicate with भविष्यन्ति
दन्ताःdantāḥteethm. nom. pl. of दन्त; subject
भविष्यन्तिbhaviṣyantiwill become√भू (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाःdāḍimīkusumopamāḥlike pomegranate flowersm. nom. pl.; compound (see Samāsa); qualifies दन्ताः

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • भक्षयन्त्याश्च bhakṣayantyāśca → भक्षयन्त्याः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.
  • तानुग्रान् tānugrān → तान् + उग्रान्
    no change (final n before a vowel) · ङमो ह्रस्वादचि ङमुण्नित्यम् (8.3.32) does not apply
    The n is not doubled, because the vowel before it (ā) is long; it is simply written with उग्रान्.
  • रक्ता दन्ता भविष्यन्ति raktā dantā bhaviṣyanti → रक्ताः + दन्ताः + भविष्यन्ति
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), भोभगोअघोअपूर्वस्य योऽशि (8.3.17)
    -āḥ before a voiced consonant (d, bh): the visarga (as ru) becomes y, and before a consonant that y is always lost, leaving -ā.
  • दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ → दाडिमीकुसुम + उपमाः
    vowel sandhi inside a compound (guṇa) · आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    a + u → o.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • महासुरान् mahāsurān — karmadhāraya
    Members: महत् + असुर · Vigraha: महान्तः च ते असुराः च, तान् mahāntaḥ ca te asurāḥ ca, tān
    Literally “great asuras”; in the verse: the mighty demons
  • दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ — bahuvrīhi containing a ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa (दाडिमीकुसुम)
    Members: दाडिमीकुसुम + उपमा · Vigraha: दाडिम्याः कुसुमं दाडिमीकुसुमम्; दाडिमीकुसुमम् उपमा येषां ते dāḍimyāḥ kusumaṃ dāḍimīkusumam; dāḍimīkusumam upamā yeṣāṃ te
    Literally “whose likeness is the pomegranate flower”; in the verse: red as pomegranate blossoms
    Alternative: उपम may also be taken as the adjective ‘like’, giving a tatpuruṣa दाडिमीकुसुमैः उपमाः ‘comparable to pomegranate flowers’; the sense is the same.

C. Morphology

  • भक्षयन्त्याः bhakṣayantyāḥ — Present participle of the curādi stem भक्षय-: भक्षयत्, feminine भक्षयन्ती (with the nasal), genitive singular -याः. It refers to the speaker: ‘of (me) devouring’, so ‘my teeth’.
  • रक्ताः raktāḥ — Past participle of √रञ्ज्, the nasal lost and j → k before t (रक्त), used as the adjective ‘red’.

D. Anvaya — prose order

तान् उग्रान् वैप्रचित्तान् महासुरान् भक्षयन्त्याः च (मम) दन्ताः दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः रक्ताः भविष्यन्ति।

tān ugrān vaipracittān mahāsurān bhakṣayantyāḥ ca (mama) dantāḥ dāḍimīkusumopamāḥ raktāḥ bhaviṣyanti |

The genitive participial phrase (भक्षयन्त्याः … महासुरान्) gives the owner of the teeth; the main clause is दन्ताः रक्ताः भविष्यन्ति with the comparison दाडिमीकुसुमोपमाः.

6Translation

LiteralAnd of (me) as I devour those fierce Vaipracitta great asuras, the teeth will become red, like pomegranate flowers.

ReadableAnd as I devour those fierce great asuras of Vipracitti’s line, my teeth will turn red, like the flowers of the pomegranate.

‘My’ renders the genitive feminine participle, which refers to the speaker.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe terrible form of 11.43 devours the Vaipracittas, and her teeth are stained red like pomegranate flowers. The simile is visual and exact: the red of the blossom, not of blood, is the image the text chooses.

Traditional interpretationThe feature gives the form its name in the next verse, Raktadantikā, ‘the Red-toothed’. The devouring recalls Cāmuṇḍā (Kālī) in the Raktabīja battle, told to range the field ‘devouring’ the asuras born of his blood — the same participle, भक्षयन्ती (8.55; the battle 8.53–61).

11.45Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततो मां देवताः स्वर्गे मर्त्यलोके च मानवाः।
स्तुवन्तो व्याहरिष्यन्ति सततं रक्तदन्तिकाम् ॥ ४५ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | माम् | देवताः | स्वर्गे | मर्त्यलोके | च | मानवाःस्तुवन्तः | व्याहरिष्यन्ति | सततम् | रक्तदन्तिकाम्

3IAST

As recitedtato māṃ devatāḥ svarge martyaloke ca mānavāḥ |
stuvanto vyāhariṣyanti satataṃ raktadantikām ||

Separatedtataḥ | mām | devatāḥ | svarge | martyaloke | ca | mānavāḥ
stuvantaḥ | vyāhariṣyanti | satatam | raktadantikām

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
ततःtataḥtherefore, from then onindeclinable (adverb, तद् + tas)
माम्māmmefirst-person pronoun अस्मद्, acc. sg.; object of व्याहरिष्यन्ति
देवताःdevatāḥthe deitiesf. nom. pl. of देवता; subject (with मानवाः)
स्वर्गेsvargein heavenm. loc. sg. of स्वर्ग
मर्त्यलोकेmartyalokein the world of mortalsm. loc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
मानवाःmānavāḥhuman beings (lit. ‘descendants of Manu’)m. nom. pl. of मानव (taddhita from मनु); subject
स्तुवन्तःstuvantaḥpraisingpresent participle (śatṛ) of √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), m. nom. pl.; qualifies the subjects
व्याहरिष्यन्तिvyāhariṣyantiwill call, will nameवि + आ + √हृ (bhvādi, 1U ‘to take’; with वि-आ ‘to utter, to call’), lṛṭ (future), prathama-puruṣa bahuvacana, parasmaipada
सततम्satatamconstantly, everindeclinable (adverbial acc. of सतत)
रक्तदन्तिकाम्raktadantikāmRaktadantikā, ‘the Red-toothed’f. acc. sg.; compound (see Samāsa); predicate accusative with माम्

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • ततो मां tato māṃ → ततः + माम्
    visarga sandhi · ससजुषो रुः (8.2.66), हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced m becomes -o. The final m of माम् becomes anusvāra before d (मोऽनुस्वारः, 8.3.23).
  • स्तुवन्तो व्याहरिष्यन्ति stuvanto vyāhariṣyanti → स्तुवन्तः + व्याहरिष्यन्ति
    visarga sandhi · हशि च (6.1.114), आद्गुणः (6.1.87)
    -aḥ before the voiced v becomes -o.
  • देवताः स्वर्गे devatāḥ svarge → देवताः + स्वर्गे
    no change (visarga before s) · खरवसानयोर्विसर्जनीयः (8.3.15)
    Before a sibilant the visarga may stand; the Gita Press keeps it (देवताः स्वर्गे, not देवतास्स्वर्गे).

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • मर्त्यलोके martyaloke — ṣaṣṭhī tatpuruṣa
    Members: मर्त्य + लोक · Vigraha: मर्त्यानां लोकः, तस्मिन् martyānāṃ lokaḥ, tasmin
    Literally “in the world of mortals”; in the verse: on earth, among men
  • रक्तदन्तिकाम् raktadantikām — bahuvrīhi (with the suffix -ka and the feminine -ā, the a before k becoming i)
    Members: रक्त + दन्त (+ ka) · Vigraha: रक्ताः दन्ताः यस्याः सा रक्तदन्तिका, ताम् raktāḥ dantāḥ yasyāḥ sā raktadantikā, tām
    Literally “she whose teeth are red”; in the verse: the Red-toothed Goddess, named from 11.44

C. Morphology

  • व्याहरिष्यन्ति vyāhariṣyanti — Future of वि + आ + √हृ (हर् + इष्य + अन्ति); वि before आ becomes व्या (yaṇ). With two accusatives, ‘to call someone something’: मां … रक्तदन्तिकाम्.
  • स्तुवन्तः stuvantaḥ — Present participle of √स्तु (adādi): the root u becomes uv before the vowel (स्तुवत्), nom. pl. स्तुवन्तः.

D. Anvaya — prose order

ततः स्वर्गे देवताः मर्त्यलोके च मानवाः स्तुवन्तः मां सततं रक्तदन्तिकां व्याहरिष्यन्ति।

tataḥ svarge devatāḥ martyaloke ca mānavāḥ stuvantaḥ māṃ satataṃ raktadantikāṃ vyāhariṣyanti |

Two subjects with their places (स्वर्गे देवताः, मर्त्यलोके मानवाः), the participle स्तुवन्तः, the verb व्याहरिष्यन्ति with object (माम्) and predicate accusative (रक्तदन्तिकाम्).

6Translation

LiteralTherefore the deities in heaven and the humans in the world of mortals, praising me, will constantly call me Raktadantikā.

ReadableSo the gods in heaven and men on earth will praise me and call me ever by the name Raktadantikā, the Red-toothed.

‘The Red-toothed’ glosses the name; ‘by the name’ makes the double accusative explicit.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe second form receives its name: Raktadantikā, ‘she whose teeth are red’. Gods in heaven and humans on earth will both use it — the prophecy names the worshippers as well as the form.

Traditional interpretationThe Mūrtirahasya, one of the three rahasyas recited after the Saptaśatī in the Gita Press pāṭha, describes the future forms of this passage for meditation, Raktadantikā among them. In the chapter itself she is known only by what these two verses say.

11.46Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

भूयश्च शतवार्षिक्यामनावृष्ट्यामनम्भसि।
मुनिभिः संस्तुता भूमौ सम्भविष्याम्ययोनिजा ॥ ४६ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

भूयः | च | शतवार्षिक्याम् | अनावृष्ट्याम् | अनम्भसिमुनिभिः | संस्तुता | भूमौ | सम्भविष्यामि | अयोनिजा

3IAST

As recitedbhūyaśca śatavārṣikyāmanāvṛṣṭyāmanambhasi |
munibhiḥ saṃstutā bhūmau sambhaviṣyāmyayonijā ||

Separatedbhūyaḥ | ca | śatavārṣikyām | anāvṛṣṭyām | anambhasi
munibhiḥ | saṃstutā | bhūmau | sambhaviṣyāmi | ayonijā

4Word by word

SanskritIASTMeaningGrammar
भूयःbhūyaḥagain, once moreindeclinable (comparative भूयस् used as an adverb)
चcaandindeclinable (conjunction)
शतवार्षिक्याम्śatavārṣikyāmlasting a hundred yearsf. loc. sg. of शतवार्षिकी (taddhita from the dvigu शतवर्ष; see Samāsa); qualifies अनावृष्ट्याम्
अनावृष्ट्याम्anāvṛṣṭyāmin a drought (lit. ‘absence of rain’)f. loc. sg. of अनावृष्टि; compound (see Samāsa); locative of time
अनम्भसिanambhasiwithout waterloc. sg. of the bahuvrīhi अनम्भस् ‘waterless’ (see Samāsa); an s-stem bahuvrīhi has one form for all genders, so it agrees with अनावृष्ट्याम्
मुनिभिःmunibhiḥby the sagesm. instr. pl. of मुनि (i-stem); agent with संस्तुता
संस्तुताsaṃstutāpraisedpast passive participle (kta) of सम् + √स्तु (adādi, 2U ‘to praise’), f. nom. sg.; qualifies the subject ‘I’
भूमौbhūmauon the earthf. loc. sg. of भूमि (i-stem)
सम्भविष्यामिsambhaviṣyāmiI shall come into being, I shall appearसम् + √भू (bhvādi, 1P), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, parasmaipada
अयोनिजाayonijānot born from a wombf. nom. sg. of अयोनिज; compound (see Samāsa); predicate of the subject ‘I’

5Vyākaraṇa

A. Sandhi and sandhi-viccheda

  • भूयश्च bhūyaśca → भूयः + च
    visarga sandhi · विसर्जनीयस्य सः (8.3.34), स्तोः श्चुना श्चुः (8.4.40)
    Visarga before c becomes s, and s next to c becomes ś.
  • शतवार्षिक्यामनावृष्ट्यामनम्भसि śatavārṣikyāmanāvṛṣṭyāmanambhasi → शतवार्षिक्याम् + अनावृष्ट्याम् + अनम्भसि
    no change (final m before a vowel) · मोऽनुस्वारः (8.3.23) does not apply before a vowel
    Both final m’s stand before a vowel and are written with it: three locatives in one written word.
  • सम्भविष्याम्ययोनिजा sambhaviṣyāmyayonijā → सम्भविष्यामि + अयोनिजा
    vowel sandhi (yaṇ) · इको यणचि (6.1.77)
    Final i before the unlike vowel a becomes y.

B. Samāsa and samāsa-vigraha

  • शतवार्षिक्याम् śatavārṣikyām — dvigu (समाहार) as the base of a taddhita
    Members: शतवर्ष + (taddhita -इक, feminine -ई) · Vigraha: शतं वर्षाणि शतवर्षम्; शतवर्षं भवा शतवार्षिकी, तस्याम् śataṃ varṣāṇi śatavarṣam; śatavarṣaṃ bhavā śatavārṣikī, tasyām
    Literally “of a hundred years’ duration”; in the verse: a drought that lasts a century
  • अनावृष्ट्याम् anāvṛṣṭyām — nañ tatpuruṣa
    Members: अन् (नञ्) + आवृष्टि · Vigraha: न आवृष्टिः अनावृष्टिः, तस्याम् na āvṛṣṭiḥ anāvṛṣṭiḥ, tasyām
    Literally “in the non-raining”; in the verse: in a drought
  • अनम्भसि anambhasi — bahuvrīhi (with the negative particle)
    Members: अन् (नञ्) + अम्भस् · Vigraha: न विद्यते अम्भः यस्यां सा अनम्भाः, तस्याम् na vidyate ambhaḥ yasyāṃ sā anambhāḥ, tasyām
    Literally “in (one) that has no water”; in the verse: when no water is left
    Alternative: It may also stand on its own as ‘when (the earth) is waterless’, with भुवि or जगति understood; the sense does not change.
  • अयोनिजा ayonijā — nañ tatpuruṣa, its second member an upapada tatpuruṣa (योनिज)
    Members: अ (नञ्) + योनिज · Vigraha: न योनिजा अयोनिजा; योनौ जायते इति योनिजा na yonijā ayonijā; yonau jāyate iti yonijā
    Literally “not born in a womb”; in the verse: self-manifest, without human birth

C. Morphology

  • सम्भविष्यामि sambhaviṣyāmi — Future of सम् + √भू (भो + इष्य → भविष्य): ‘I shall come into being’. The prefix सम् gives the sense of appearing or being born.
  • संस्तुता saṃstutā — Past participle of सम् + √स्तु: ‘praised’. The sages’ praise comes before her appearance: they call, and she appears.

D. Anvaya — prose order

भूयः च शतवार्षिक्याम् अनम्भसि अनावृष्ट्यां (सत्यां) मुनिभिः संस्तुता (अहम्) अयोनिजा भूमौ सम्भविष्यामि।

bhūyaḥ ca śatavārṣikyām anambhasi anāvṛṣṭyāṃ (satyāṃ) munibhiḥ saṃstutā (aham) ayonijā bhūmau sambhaviṣyāmi |

The locatives give the occasion (a waterless hundred-year drought); मुनिभिः संस्तुता gives the cause; the main clause is (अहम्) अयोनिजा भूमौ सम्भविष्यामि.

6Translation

LiteralAnd again, in a drought lasting a hundred years, without water, praised by the sages, I shall come into being on the earth, not born from a womb.

ReadableAnd once more, when the earth lies a hundred years without rain or water, I shall appear on earth at the sages’ praise, born of no womb.

‘At the sages’ praise’ renders मुनिभिः संस्तुता (‘praised by the sages’) as the occasion of her coming.

7Commentary

Direct textual meaningThe third descent begins: a drought of a hundred years, the sages praise her, and she appears on earth अयोनिजा, born of no mother. Unlike the daughter of Yaśodā (11.42), this form has no human birth.

Traditional interpretationVerses 11.46–50 are one episode with three names: Śatākṣī (11.47), Śākambharī (11.49) and Durgā (11.50), all of the same appearance (तत्रैव, ‘right there’, 11.49). The tradition treats them as one Goddess named by three of her deeds.

11.47Ślokaanuṣṭubh (śloka)

1Original śloka

ततः शतेन नेत्राणां निरीक्षिष्यामि यन्मुनीन्।
कीर्तयिष्यन्ति मनुजाः शताक्षीमिति मां ततः ॥ ४७ ॥

2Padaccheda — the words separated

ततः | शतेन | नेत्राणाम् | निरीक्षिष्यामि | यत् | मुनीन्कीर्तयिष्यन्ति | मनुजाः | शताक्षीम् | इति | माम् | ततः

As recitedtataḥ śatena netrāṇāṃ nirīkṣiṣyāmi yanmunīn |
kīrtayiṣyanti manujāḥ śatākṣīmiti māṃ tataḥ ||

Separatedtataḥ | śatena | netrāṇām | nirīkṣiṣyāmi | yat | munīn
kīrtayiṣyanti | manujāḥ | śatākṣīm | iti | mām | tataḥ

नेत्राणाम्netrāṇāmof eyesn. gen. pl. of नेत्र; with शतेन (‘a hundred of eyes’)
निरीक्षिष्यामिnirīkṣiṣyāmiI shall look uponनिर् + √ईक्ष् (bhvādi, 1Ā ‘to see’), lṛṭ (future), uttama-puruṣa ekavacana, here in parasmaipada
